### 0014 — Update credential operations use a value-safe provisioning boundary (TASK-260810-3uh3mc)

- DECISION: the update bootstrap tool receives credential bytes only on stdin or validated owner-only files and invokes `gh secret set NAME --env ENV` without `--body`; it suppresses `gh` output. It maps six `updates-test` names and one `release` name at bootstrap, with versioned Sparkle generations constrained to their own environment.
- SECURITY: every Sparkle generation has a distinct versioned Keychain account and its private export goes to encrypted offline escrow before a setter consumes it. Rotation is forward-only through immutable bridge assets; no private export is copied between test and stable channels or retained in staging.
- SECURITY: `security import -P` necessarily places the `.p12` password in process argv briefly. The runbook records its temporary dedicated-runner isolation and cleanup controls, followed by mandatory Developer ID reissue/revocation after the iteration.
- RECOVERY: stable trust rotation is URL-generational: V1's old-key-signed bridge embeds V2's key/URL, V1 bytes remain live as immutable checksummed release assets, and old-client evidence is required before/after cutover. A recovery is forward-only, never an in-place DMG mutation or lower-build downgrade.
- VALIDATION: the focused setter/inventory and Sparkle-command regression suite (10 tests), all 270 script tests, `make updates-secret-inventory`, the 2/2 `make check-repo` gate, and `git diff --check` exited 0 after the prior Sparkle-command correction. ### 0011 — Committed-exit watchdog permit is implemented; acceptance remains review-owned (TASK-260810-1cf7o7)

- FIX: removed the watchdog-free `AgentLifecycle` commit path. The only commit permit now receives an explicit watchdog arm operation under the lifecycle lock; arm failure leaves the ready lease intact, and cancellation restores the serving agent rather than claiming an irreversible commit.
- SIGNAL SAFETY: startup constructs and installs the `SIGALRM` hard-exit watchdog before engine work. `signal == SIG_ERR` and `pthread_sigmask` failures are retained as installation failures; no later `setitimer` call can manufacture a permit. The installed handler calls only `Darwin._exit(0)`.
- REGRESSION: deterministic watchdog tests cover signal-install failure, unmask failure, successful timer arm, and lifecycle arm failure followed by rollback-safe cancellation. The source audit is producer evidence only; it must not be read as independent acceptance.
- STATUS: implemented and locally validated in this task. Independent review remains the authority for whether this establishes the required process-death protocol.

### 0010 — Exact-process termination protocol is the accepted implementation contract (TASK-260811-krfusi)

- DECISION: keep every stallable drain and every rollback-capable resource attached before commit. Commit may linearize only after an agent-owned `SIGALRM` watchdog is armed; after the typed acknowledgement write attempt, orderly teardown closes health last and calls `_exit(0)`, while the watchdog supplies the same kernel-cleanup result if Swift tasks or locks stall.
- TERMINAL PROOF: the companion captures `instanceID + pid + kernel process start time + build` and registers process observation before commit. AppKit `true` requires death/start-identity change of that exact old process (with endpoint plus kernel-flock release as fallback), so unconditional launchd restart cannot hide the witness. AppKit `false` remains precommit-only and requires the same current-build agent to publish request-correlated running rollback after admission, endpoints, namespaces, and Finder readiness are restored.
- FALLBACK: after the agent hard-exit deadline, the companion revalidates the kernel start identity before bounded `SIGTERM`/`SIGKILL`; it never signals a reused PID. A commit acknowledgement, live `.stopped` payload, timeout, EOF, or socket pathname gap is not terminal evidence.
- OVERLAP: the packaged launch agent must replace unconditional `KeepAlive = true` with `KeepAlive.SuccessfulExit = false`; planned exit 0 stays down, crash/signal exits restart, and a later app launch directly starts a missing current-session agent without changing the login preference. The single-instance flock remains held until kernel death.
- STATUS: this entry records the architecture handoff, not independent evidence that production code satisfies it. `TASK-260810-1cf7o7` is the implementation owner and its outcome must distinguish local evidence from reviewer acceptance. Outcome: `TASK-260811-krfusi_bounded-terminal-protocol.md`.

### 0009 — Accepted Sparkle commit has no safe bounded fallback yet (TASK-260810-1cf7o7)

- REVIEW FINDING: once `acceptTerminationCommit` succeeds, `AgentLifecycle` has atomically detached its serving resources and entered `stopped`. The ordinary cancellation path can recover only from `termination-ready`; it cannot resurrect an accepted commit.
- REJECTED APPROACH: a companion timeout after commit acceptance must not call the pre-commit cancellation reconciler and return `false` based on a synthetic `termination-cancelled` health sample. Production cannot publish that recovery from `stopped`, so the test would mask a split-brain/half-terminated state.
- REQUIRED INVARIANT: AppKit may receive `true` only after the old endpoint/process disappearance is proven. It may receive `false` only after a production-capable rollback has restored a usable current agent. A bounded protocol that proves neither result remains an open architecture boundary, not a passing implementation.
- STATUS: active rework; entry 0008 remains valid for the observable acceptance and normal disappearance path, but its lost-response recovery claim is not accepted until this boundary is resolved and independently reviewed.

### 0008 — Sparkle commit acceptance and replacement now observe teardown (TASK-260810-1cf7o7)

The host finishes teardown only after writing that result; the coordinator and the matching-build replacement both wait for the old endpoint to disappear before allowing AppKit termination or launching the bundled agent.

The update path retains the existing App Group/profile/domain.

- VALIDATION: full Swift package suite (462), arm64 release build, packaging suite (65), `make check-apple` (2/2), scoped SwiftFormat lint (0/2), and `git diff --check` each exited 0.

### 0006 — Sparkle termination now has a finite commit-or-abort lease (TASK-260810-1cf7o7)

- SAFETY: a UUID-correlated `prepare` drains only to `termination-ready`; the agent retains its sockets, App Group state, File Provider domain, and profile until a separate matching `commit`. It cannot exit from an uncommitted prepared state.
- BOUNDED RECOVERY: cancellation reconciliation now has a real finite bound. If control/health remain unavailable, the coordinator returns one `false` reply without ever sending `commit`; the agent's five-second lease auto-restores transfer admission and authorized namespace owners. This corrects the earlier unbounded-retry claim in entry 0005.
- COMMIT: a matched `termination-ready` health result sends `commit` only for that UUID. Acknowledged commit permits teardown/relaunch; stale readiness cannot commit a retry request.
- APPKIT: `ApplicationTerminationRequestDriver` is the tested delegate seam for one real reply across joins and Keep GramDrive Open cancellation.
- VALIDATION: focused lifecycle/control/coordinator suite (58), full Swift package suite (458), packaging suite (65), arm64 release build, `make check-apple` (2/2), scoped SwiftFormat lint (0/2), and `git diff --check` each exited 0.

### 0005 — Sparkle cancellation never emits an inconclusive reply-false (TASK-260810-1cf7o7)

- SAFETY: cancellation reconciliation no longer turns a local deadline, dropped cancellation acknowledgement, or stalled health socket into `reply(false)`. It re-sends the idempotent UUID-correlated cancellation request on each cancellation interval and waits for either request-matching `termination-cancelled` recovery or endpoint disappearance.
- EXPLICIT CANCEL: “Keep GramDrive Open” now switches the in-flight coordinator directly into that reconciliation path, so it does not wait for the original drain deadline before the single reply-gate decision.
- REGRESSION: deterministic coordinator tests hold health in timeout/error readings beyond the cancellation interval, then prove a late correlated recovery yields exactly one false reply and a late stopped/not-running teardown yields exactly one allowed reply.
- VALIDATION: focused coordinator tests (12), full Swift package suite (454), arm64 release build, packaging tests (65), `make check-apple` (2/2), scoped SwiftFormat lint (0/2), and `git diff --check` each exited 0.

### 0004 — Sparkle termination cancellation now restores a serving agent before reply-false (TASK-260810-1cf7o7)

- RECOVERY: a cancelled or abandoned drain snapshots its active namespace owners, temporarily restores the lifecycle's serving state, reopens transfer admission, recreates those owners, and only then publishes the request-correlated `termination-cancelled` result. The current app can therefore remain usable after “Keep GramDrive Open”; the File Provider domain and Telegram profile are retained.
- CORRELATION: the companion accepts a cancellation only when its UUID matches the current request. It continues polling a correlated live `stopped` health payload until the endpoint disappears, so irreversible teardown cannot be reported as a failed quit.
- TIMEOUT: a drain deadline sends the matching cancel command and enters a separate bounded reconciliation phase. It permits termination only after endpoint disappearance, or replies false after request-matching serving recovery; stale cancellation cannot cancel a newer retry.
- VALIDATION: scoped SwiftFormat lint (0/2 files), focused lifecycle/control/coordinator tests (52), packaging tests (65), full Swift suite (452), arm64 release build, `make check-apple` (2/2), and `git diff --check` each exited 0.

### 0001 — Self-hosted CI restores a missing Rust toolchain without shell-profile state (BUG-260720-27inl5)

- ROOT CAUSE: public CI run `31443550054` (`rust-core`) and run `31443550101` (`tdlib` and `apple-build-test`) each invoked `rustup` before any bootstrap. Their clean macOS self-hosted runner had no command on `PATH`, so each job stopped with `rustup: command not found` and exit 127.
- FIX: every Rust-consuming job in `ci.yml` and `native-ci.yml` now calls one shell bootstrap. It locates or non-interactively installs runner-owned rustup with `--no-modify-path`, reads the channel/profile/components exclusively from `rust-toolchain.toml`, reconciles them idempotently, and adds Cargo's bin directory through `GITHUB_PATH` for later steps.
- REGRESSION: a clean-runner mock runs the bootstrap twice with no rustup in the incoming path. It proves exactly one bootstrap download, repeated reconciliation of Rust 1.91.0 with rustfmt/clippy, a single `GITHUB_PATH` entry, and no executable `source` command. A structural test pins all four workflow call sites to the shared bootstrap.
- VALIDATION: focused bootstrap tests (2), existing toolchain tests (18), public metadata tests (3), `sh -n`, `actionlint`, and `git diff --check` each exited 0. A malformed dotted-path unittest attempt exited 1 before discovery was corrected; no test ran in that attempt.
- DELIVERY LIMIT: local work is on dirty `main` with no associated PR, so fresh protected-main and remote `rust-core`/`secret-scan`/native evidence remain unclaimed. The existing public run logs are retained under `.temp/BUG-260720-27inl5/` for reviewer verification.

## 2026-08-10

### 1138 — Folder deletion requires a catalog witness, not a fabricated chat departure (BUG-260810-3nob99)

- ROOT CAUSE: The `folder_catalog` fixture cleared a deleted folder with a whole-list `replace_chat_list([])`. That path correctly treats every omitted chat as a potentially unsafe departure, so an unwitnessed 2-to-0 shrink failed with `UnsafeChatListShrink`.
- FIX: The fixture now persists `FolderCatalogMachine::definitions()` through `replace_folders`, matching the FFI adapter. A missing folder definition is the positive catalog-deletion witness that clears only that folder's appearances; it does not claim that chats left Telegram.
- REGRESSION: `unwitnessed_strict_subset_folder_membership_shrink_fails_closed` pins the ordinary 2-to-1 whole-list path to `UnsafeChatListShrink` and proves the two baseline memberships remain intact.
- VALIDATION: folder-catalog, state snapshot, and FFI folder/fail-closed tests; workspace rustfmt, Clippy with `-D warnings`, and `make check-core` exited 0.

### 1132 — Installed generated-document acceptance is externally blocked by a stale signed candidate (BUG-260728-a0yc0e)

- CONSTRAINT: The only discoverable Developer ID app is `/Applications/GramDrive.app` and its byte-identical `.temp/app-packaging` copy: 0.1.1 (75), signed and timestamped 2026-07-28. Both predate the uncommitted 2026-08-10 hydration source and regression tests; no `.app` or bundled `gramdrive-agent` newer than that source exists.
- EVIDENCE: The installed File Provider extension process is from that July 28 bundle, while no `gramdrive-agent` process is running. The current active-run directive requires a current signed candidate plus runnable bundled agent and explicitly forbids claiming installed E2E from synthetic tests.
- ATTEMPT: The authorized current release path staged a TDLib-linked core and compiled fresh arm64 File Provider, agent, and companion binaries. `make package-app` then exited 2 when `codesign` first attempted `libcrypto.3.dylib`: `errSecInternalComponent`, although `security find-identity` lists `Developer ID Application: Relux Works, LLC (262RZ595FP)`. The staged agent is unsigned (`TeamIdentifier=not set`), so it cannot replace the installed app.
- DECISION: Do not rerun synthetic or broad suites as a substitute for the required 20-read, relaunch, signed-installed acceptance. The existing deterministic regression coverage is useful but cannot establish current-source Finder behavior.
- REQUIRED INPUT: Unlock or repair the login-keychain private-key access for `Developer ID Application: Relux Works, LLC (262RZ595FP)` so noninteractive `codesign` can sign the current bundle, or provide an equivalently current Developer ID-signed candidate. Then rerun the saturated generated-document acceptance before and after app/agent relaunch without resetting the profile or File Provider domain.

It proves every result is byte-exact, active hydration never exceeds the bound, and every per-chat `requested` signal is paired with exactly one `background` release.

### 0835 — Foreground hydration no longer inherits utility/cooperative scheduling (BUG-260729-3k8o93)

- FIX: Finder demand enters `.userInitiated`; SQLite snapshot/context calls and the native hydration wait, generated-descriptor `open(2)`, socket terminal I/O, and synchronous lease reclamation all execute on dedicated user-initiated dispatch queues, never on a Swift cooperative worker. The FFI `Hydrator` now owns an independent two-worker Tokio runtime; its UniFFI wrapper only awaits that runtime's join handle.
- CEILING RESOLVED: UniFFI 0.32 may still poll the outer wrapper through `async-compat`, but no SQLite, filesystem, transfer-driver, or retry work is scheduled onto that fallback current-thread runtime. The dedicated runtime shuts down with Tokio's nonblocking `shutdown_background`, so exported-future teardown does not wait for retained transfer-result timers. No priority flags or test-only production bypasses were added.
- REGRESSION: deterministic five-fetch saturation uses four actual blocking user-initiated dispatch workers (not suspended async tasks), then measures both fresh start and materialized completion below 250 ms while an independent utility crawl continuously earns at least three slices. Agent wire coverage records user-initiated priority for admitted demand and for generated-document lease release. Attachment-driver/exported-runtime, content-version restart, cancellation, descriptor hand-off, and history-demand coverage remain green.
- VALIDATION: focused exported-hydrator regression, `cargo check -p gramdrive-ffi`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, staged-core `swift build --package-path apple/GramDriveSupport`, and staged-core `swift test --package-path apple/GramDriveSupport` (425 passed) exited 0. The package's configured Apple gate is Swift build/test; an ad-hoc strict SwiftLint report remains pre-existing (47 violations in large legacy files) and is not a configured gate.

### 0620 — Generated hydration retains its pathname through materialization and transfers crash-safe ownership (BUG-260729-2ao3y1)

- FIX: generated hydration claims the immutable render file under the Rust reclamation mutex, opens it, and transfers that descriptor with `SCM_RIGHTS`. The server retains the Rust lease and original pathname until the File Provider peer closes after its synchronous `hydrateAndMaterialize` callback; raw `hydrate()` rejects generated content rather than returning a stale descriptor value.
- CRASH/LIFECYCLE: the received descriptor remains a stable inode reference if the agent crashes, restarts, or must force-close a wedged post-done connection after the lifecycle deadline. That bounded force-close releases server ticket/lease/path state without invalidating an already-running clone.
- REGRESSION: deterministic Rust reclaim/lease tests prove an acquire either wins and preserves exact bytes or loses as a cache miss. Swift barriers prove the pathname survives normal and cancelled materialization, exact bytes survive a bounded shutdown/reclamation after descriptor transfer, and success/disconnect cleanup leaves no server connection or lease.

## 2026-07-29

### 1930 — Cache-hit LRU bookkeeping is no longer a provider failure path (BUG-260729-dtezp9)

- FIX: `cached_file` verifies the durable materialization before performing only a coarsened, zero-wait LRU touch. A busy or locked WAL writer leaves the authoritative cache row unchanged and cannot surface as `cannotSynchronize`.
- ACCOUNTING: a skipped touch changes neither cache bytes nor eviction eligibility; generated documents remain visibly counted while their existing quota-exemption policy stays unchanged.
- REGRESSION: a deterministic second `StateStore` holds `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` while generated hydration returns its exact bytes in under one second, rather than waiting for the normal five-second SQLite busy timeout.

### 1900 — Generated-document quota eviction is structurally excluded (BUG-260729-17tdhj)

- CORRECTION: the earlier dirty-on-eviction approach left a filesystem/publication TOCTOU: a renderer could republish the same immutable reference after the cache-row delete but before the evictor removed that path. A clean cache row could then point at absent bytes.
- DECISION: generated documents are now excluded from the destructive LRU frontier and the quota-governed unpinned/evictable totals. This is the explicit acceptance-allowed exemption, and it removes the aliasing race rather than attempting to schedule around it. The state-layer dirty-on-direct-eviction safeguard remains for non-quota callers.
- VISIBILITY: `CacheAccounting.generated_doc_bytes` still reports all generated-document bytes and the new `generated_doc_exempt_bytes` makes their exemption explicit. Total cache use continues to include those bytes.
- REGRESSION: a zero-quota over-quota core walk makes a newer thumbnail the sole LRU victim while a production-valid monthly NDJSON generated document is older; it proves the document is not selected, its cache row and bytes remain present, and its clean render state remains paired with real bytes.

### 1850 — Generated-document eviction is an atomic re-render request (BUG-260729-17tdhj)

- FIX: `WriteTxn::evict_cache_entry` now marks an existing render-state row dirty in the same SQLite transaction as its eligible cache-row deletion. A published generated document can therefore retain its provider-visible logical size and content version after quota eviction only while it is already present in the durable re-render worklist.
- DECISION: keep generated documents eligible for quota reclamation instead of adding a hidden exemption. `CacheAccounting.generated_doc_bytes` remains the distinct visible category; the regression asserts it before a zero-quota LRU pass.
- REGRESSION: a realistic clean publication fixture drives the quota walk at zero quota, proves the cache row and host bytes are removed, and proves the published item facts coexist with a dirty render state and deterministic worklist entry.
- VALIDATION: `cargo test -p gramdrive-engine --test cache_quota -- --nocapture` (12 passed); `cargo test -p gramdrive-state --test repo_cache_render -- --nocapture` (11 passed); `cargo clippy -p gramdrive-engine --test cache_quota -- -D warnings`; `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo check -p gramdrive-engine` (all exit 0).

### 1845 — Chat-list snapshots now fail closed before Finder can observe a false removal (BUG-260729-28hnfq)

- FIX: `replace_chat_list_with_audit` rejects every proposed Main/archive/folder membership shrink unless the snapshot is explicitly complete and each omitted chat has `left_at_ms` or `deleted_at_ms`. The typed, identity-free `UnsafeChatListShrink` reports only counts; no titles, chat ids, account data, or message data are exposed.
- FIX: `apply_snapshot_commit` maps that rejection to retryable `snapshot-membership-incomplete` and rolls back before its resume token is written. The next snapshot therefore starts from the retained checkpoint, while the existing list and projected subtrees stay live.
- REGRESSION: deterministic 140-chat fixtures reproduce the installed 11:53 folder and 11:56 Main short snapshots. Both retain all membership, keep the prior checkpoint, reconcile stably, and emit no tombstoned item-change records (therefore no `didDeleteItems`). A `left_at_ms` witness still removes and tombstones only the departed chat branch.
- VALIDATION: `cargo test -p gramdrive-state --test repo_snapshots` (18 passed); `cargo test -p gramdrive-ffi` (161 passed); `cargo clippy -p gramdrive-state -p gramdrive-ffi --tests -- -D warnings`; `cargo fmt --check`; `swift test --package-path apple/GramDriveSupport --filter GramDriveEnumeratorTests` (31 Swift Testing cases passed). No installed signed-extension/backfill run was performed in this workspace; that privacy-sensitive validation remains explicitly unclaimed.

### 1830 — Provider fetch observability keeps identity local and reasons durable (TASK-260729-376m7o)

- DECISION: content and thumbnail fetches emit one signed-process record per item with a stable 96-bit SHA-256 token, fixed callback/outcome vocabulary, retryability and elapsed milliseconds. The token never crosses the control socket or enters SQLite; durable health is aggregate booleans/counters only, and the coordinator remains the sole writer.
- FINDING: mapping a typed hydration failure to `NSFileProviderError` before telemetry classified it erased engine attribution. The callback now carries the original fixed classification beside the mapped macOS error, so engine failures, provider mappings and `noSuchItem` remain distinguishable end to end.
- FINDING: nullable provenance without a migration backfill did not satisfy “every tombstone”; SQL `NOT (...)` also lets NULL bypass a trigger under three-valued logic. Schema v18 now backfills installed rows (retention pass marker vs reconcile), uses NULL-safe insert/update triggers, and repository upserts assign reconcile provenance to already-tombstoned legacy layout rows.
- AUDIT: chat-list replacement records before/after membership counts, completeness and commit time atomically. Tombstone reasons are the closed vocabulary `reconcile` / `retention` / `policy`; neither surface stores names, Telegram/account identifiers, message content, filenames or secrets.
- VALIDATION: focused state migration/repository/schema suites, FFI lib tests, Rust clippy/fmt/check, Swift content/thumbnail/control/lifecycle tests and Swift build are green. Installed signed-extension log observation was not run and is not claimed.
- STATUS: implementation handed off to review; evidence is `TASK-260729-376m7o_observability-outcome.md`.

### 1825 — The durable render worklist is livelocked; no generated document can ever be published

- ROOT CAUSE: `dirty_render_items` selects the four lowest-sorted dirty rows in a fixed `ORDER BY r.input_watermark_seq, r.rendered_at_ms, r.item_id LIMIT ?1` with **no cursor and no skip** (`crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/render.rs:219-235`; limit is `MAX_RENDER_WORKLIST_ITEMS_PER_TICK = 4`, `crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs:114`). `render_pending_months` refuses a protected chat's `.chat.json` and `continue`s **without clearing `dirty`** (`namespace.rs:3292-3312`). `enforce_chat_protection_txn` invalidates only *month* partitions and explicitly skips everything else (`namespace.rs:4296-4310`), so those rows stay live, dirty and unpublishable forever. Every `.chat.json` row sits at `input_watermark_seq = 0` by design (`crates/gramdrive-engine/src/render_plan/plan.rs:259-262` — measured: all 2,208 live rows), so they always sort into the first tier. The same four rows are re-planned every tick, permanently.
- EVIDENCE (decoded from the item-id codec over a read-only snapshot): the four head rows of the worklist are `.chat.json` documents of chats with `is_protected = 1`. 34 protected `chat_list_entries` vs 36 dirty never-rendered `.chat.json` rows. exit=0.
- EVIDENCE (installed, three read-only samples 311 s apart): `render_state` rows with `rendered_at_ms IS NOT NULL` **frozen at 31,942**; `max(rendered_at_ms)` unchanged, i.e. **no publication of any kind for 671 s and counting**; dirty grew 62,351 → 62,363; live generated docs lacking `logical_size` grew 56,018 → 56,030 — while `messages` grew by 2,924 (≈9.4/s). **The backlog is diverging, not converging.** exit=0.
- FINDING (scale): pre-livelock throughput ≈**214 appearance-doc publications/hour** (24 h histogram, a lower bound since re-renders overwrite) against a loop design ceiling of ≈16/s. 0.4 % of its own cap before; zero now. Draining 56,050 rows at the pre-livelock rate is ≈11 days.
- FINDING (this is the "files that will not open"): 56,030 of 82,478 live generated-doc appearances (68.0 %) have neither `logical_size` nor `content_version`; `checkFetchable` refuses any file item with no `logicalSize` before any IPC (`ContentFetcher.swift:281-298`). Canonical: 38,172 of 57,660 (66.2 %); appearance/canonical duplication ≈1.43.
- ANOMALY (adjacent, same path): `chat_render_catalog` scans **every** generated-doc item in the account and filters in Rust (`render.rs:466-503`), while `month_render_catalog` is parent-indexed (`:419-463`). Today the loop plans nothing *but* `.chat.json` jobs, so that scan runs every tick.
- SCOPE: `.research/260729_hierarchy-preload-progress-and-eta-policy.md` §4 (TASK-260729-lw7emc outcome). Filed as BUG-260729-ozfw4n; index fix as TASK-260729-25zz19.
- STATUS: pending — read-only research, no product change. Directly explains the §6.5 anomaly left undiagnosed in the 1801 entry.

### 1820 — Generated-document eviction is a one-way de-publication

- FINDING: `eviction_candidates` filters only on `pinned = 0 AND verification = 'verified'` with **no `kind` predicate** (`crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/cache.rs:392-415`); the quota walk skips only items with an open read or live transfer (`crates/gramdrive-engine/src/cache/quota.rs:396-446`); no call site marks the render row dirty afterwards (grep over `mark_render_dirty`: only `render_plan/plan.rs:185`, `namespace.rs:4378`, `namespace.rs:5460`).
- ROOT CAUSE (hazard): generated docs are `cache_only` — a miss fails fast and cannot be refetched from Telegram. Evicting one leaves an item still advertising `logical_size` + `content_version` with **absent bytes** and no pending re-render: the `serverUnreachable` retry storm again, with no path back to published.
- FINDING (why it will fire): generated docs are **1.107 GB across 31,942 cache entries** against a 10 GiB default quota (`quota.rs:16`), while blobs are only **37 MB across 61 entries**. Nobody opens a monthly export, so generated docs are the least-recently-accessed class by construction and the first victims once real media hydration starts.
- SCOPE: `.research/260729_hierarchy-preload-progress-and-eta-policy.md` §4.4. Filed as BUG-260729-17tdhj.
- STATUS: pending — hazard, not yet observed on the installed profile.

### 1815 — Hierarchy preload is a scheduling and honesty problem, not a download feature

- FINDING (the decomposition): making the hierarchy visible is three stages with unrelated costs. L1 discovery (chat lists/folders) and L2 history (`getChatHistory`, paced at 250 ms, `backfill/pace.rs:51`) touch the network; **L3 publication (render) touches none**. Only L3 is stuck.
- FINDING (the media half is already free): attachment placeholders carry their exact extent from `mapped.descriptor.size` at message-map time (`namespace.rs:2959`), copied into `logical_size` by the projection (`:5173-5197`). Measured: **8 of 1,651,779 live attachment rows lack `logical_size` — 0.0005 %**. No byte is fetched to make an attachment visible *and* openable.
- FINDING (the denominator trap): `chat_needs_history` requires chat-list membership (`backfill/mod.rs:626-645`). Measured: **1,658 chats have membership; only 168 of them still need history**. The "6,889 incomplete chats" in the brief is residue — 9,184 `chat_content_progress` rows belong to unlisted, unschedulable chats. **70 % of Finder-visible chats (1,162 of 1,658) are already hierarchy-complete**; the remaining work is concentrated in 496 chats (p50 = 98 unpublished appearance docs each).
- DECISION (no fake denominators): three phase-scoped meters with explicit denominators — chats complete / work set (headline, verifiable in Finder), history complete / work set, documents published / documents live — plus separate `excluded_protected` / `excluded_unavailable` / `failed_retryable` buckets that are never folded into a numerator. Report **appearances**, schedule **canonical**, convert once from measured duplication. Never use `chats` (32,886), `chat_sync_state`, `chat_content_progress` or `render_state.dirty` as a denominator.
- DECISION (ETA): EWMA over completed canonical documents per second of *active* time (excluding paused/offline/flood/power), durable across relaunch, with `unknown`/`rough`/`estimated` confidence and four hard refusal rules — no ETA while the denominator outgrows the numerator, no ETA at rate 0 (show **"Not making progress"**), never a wall-clock countdown, cap the horizon at "more than a week". The installed profile right now must render as `stalled`, and today the product has no way to say so.
- FINDING (message ids are not a progress proxy): all 2,661 anchored `chat_sync_state` rows are exact multiples of 2²⁰ (`server_id << 20`), but `server_id` is per-chat only for supergroups/channels — the naive account-wide sum is 718,032,736, meaningless as a message count.
- FINDING (storage disclosure): publishing the remaining 38,172 canonical documents projects to **≈1.3 GB of local text** (measured mean 34.6 KB/doc) and **zero payload bytes**. That number belongs in Settings before the toggle commits.
- DECISION (ordering): the livelock fix (BUG-260729-ozfw4n), the eviction fix (BUG-260729-17tdhj), maintained counters (TASK-260729-vztkco) and fail-closed chat-list membership (BUG-260729-28hnfq) all land **before** the mode itself (TASK-260729-3che3j). A progress bar over a pipeline that cannot drain is worse than none; and a denominator built on `chat_list_entries` jumps to 100 % every time the whole-list-replace false delete fires.
- DECISION (pause is a new switch): preload pause must not reuse `backfill_control.paused` (`backfill/mod.rs:365-367`) — that stops *all* history including foreground demand, so an opened chat would stop loading.
- SCOPE: `.research/260729_hierarchy-preload-progress-and-eta-policy.md` (TASK-260729-lw7emc outcome). Feeds TASK-260729-3che3j, TASK-260729-1j1oti.
- STATUS: pending — design only, no product change. `make check` / `cargo test` / `swift test` were not run; no claim rests on a gate.

### 1801 — Generated-document Finder timeouts are system retry rounds, not one slow read
- FINDING (critical path): generated docs hydrate `cache_only` (`crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/hydration.rs:743`) — no network, no render, no transfer, and **no await point** on either the hit or miss branch (`:703-750`). The `has_account` session gate at `:751-755` is only on the non-`cache_only` branch; existing test asserts "generated hydration never requires a Telegram session" (`:3819`).
- FINDING (budget): measured ≈**7.7 ms** per fresh-connection point read on the live installed 9.47 GB DB / 521 MB WAL (10× `sqlite3 -readonly`, 0.077 s total, exit=0). Whole path budgets **< 50 ms**. Observed round-6 wall clock 4.8–112.4 s ⇒ gap of 10²–10³.
- ROOT CAUSE (reframing): no code in this repo emits ETIMEDOUT or caps a read at 27 s / 112 s. Our timeouts are 5 s (SQLite busy, agent request line), 60 s (client *idle-between-events*, not total), 1 s (hint socket). Every refusal maps to `serverUnreachable`/`cannotSynchronize` (`ContentFetcher.swift:436-451`), which macOS retries while `open(2)` stays blocked. Success and failure latencies are **one** population — retry rounds until the local refusal cleared — truncated by the OS materialization deadline. The 26.3 s success vs 27.2 s failure straddle that truncation.
- ROOT CAUSE (H1, ranked first): monthly doc `content_version` is a function of `input_watermark_seq` (`crates/gramdrive-engine/src/render_pipeline.rs:88-106`), so it bumps on **every ingested batch**; the crawl loop republishes 4 docs/tick at 250 ms ticks (`namespace.rs:114,88,947`). Extension pins one version and allows **`maxVersionRestarts = 1`** (`ContentFetcher.swift:14,222-228`); agent refuses any mismatch (`hydration.rs:2362-2366`). A chat under active crawl can lose that race indefinitely. `.chat.json` versions come from the bytes, not a watermark — predicts it fails far less than `.md`/`.ndjson`.
- ROOT CAUSE (H2): the cache-**hit** path runs `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` + `touch_cache_entry` purely for LRU bookkeeping (`hydration.rs:2499-2502`, `repo/mod.rs:187-192`). `SQLITE_BUSY` after the 5 s busy timeout (`state/store.rs:24`) → `DriveError::Storage` (`hydration.rs:2650`) → **`cannotSynchronize`** (`ContentFetcher.swift:449`) — a hard, non-retryable failure for a read whose bytes were already verified on disk.
- FINDING (H3): `ContentHydrating` promises the staged path lives until the connection closes (`HydrationServer.swift:19-20`), but the client's `defer { connection.finish() }` (`HydrationClient.swift:106`) fires **before** `materialize()` clones it (`ContentFetcher.swift:213`), while `reclaim_unreferenced_generations` deletes the prior generation right after publish commit (`render_pipeline.rs:713-720,540-585`). Narrow race, not systemic: 60/60 sampled generated-doc refs present on disk.
- FINDING (hints exonerated): `AgentHistoryPriorityClient.signal` only enqueues and returns (`HistoryPriorityClient.swift:29-39`); socket work is a separate `.utility` queue with 1 s timeouts and swallowed errors. Agent side writes an in-memory map, no TDLib/state I/O (`namespace.rs:523-541`). Hint routing **cannot** cause the timeouts and must stay untouched by the fix.
- FINDING (installed state): `render_state` 62,310/87,962 dirty (30,604 `Messages.md` + 30,604 `Messages.ndjson` + 1,102 `chat.json`); `chat_sync_state` 6,889/10,804 histories incomplete; 31,927 generated-doc cache entries. Read-only, exit=0.
- ANOMALY: 62,310 dirty render rows yet only ~0.45 commits/s observed (5 Hz `PRAGMA data_version`, 20 s window = 9 transitions; a 6 s window saw 0) with `backfill_control.paused=0` and the pacer armed. Either the plan correctly skips docs already current at their watermark and the dirty flag is over-set, or publication is not draining. Overlaps BUG-260727-2ct7o0 / BUG-260727-3k11lo; not diagnosed here.
- NOTE (scaling ceiling, not this bug): `#[uniffi::export(async_runtime = "tokio")]` wraps futures in `async_compat::Compat` (`uniffi_macros-0.32.0/src/export/scaffolding.rs:273-274`) whose fallback runtime is a **single-threaded** `new_current_thread` (`async-compat-0.2.5/src/lib.rs:456-469`). Off the generated-doc path (no await, nothing spawned) but shared by all attachment transfer drivers (`hydration.rs:650`). Both hydration queues are `.utility`, and the agent's per-connection `Task` does blocking SQLite on the cooperative pool (`HydrationServer.swift:211-214,345`).
- DECISION (not implemented): five task boundaries — T1 drop the write from the hit path; T2 make generated-doc version churn non-fatal (serve current version for `cache_only`); T3 honour the staged-file lifetime; T4 fix QoS inversion; T5 fetch observability **first**, since today's evidence can only measure the outside of the retry loop.
- SCOPE: `.research/260729_generated-document-hydration-latency.md` (TASK-260729-3vphyu outcome). Feeds BUG-260728-a0yc0e.
- STATUS: pending — read-only research, no product change. Contended state was **not** reproduced live (profile quiescent during snapshot); H2/H4/H5 remain inferred with probes P3/P5/P6 defined to settle them.

### 1744 — Finder mass disappearance is a chat-list false delete, not a failed fetch
- ROOT CAUSE: `apply_snapshot_commit` → `replace_chat_list` (`crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs:3971-3984`, `crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/chats.rs:476-506`) is a whole-list replace; any chat absent from one snapshot response is treated as removed. `reconcile_nodes` then cascades `tombstone_projection_subtree` over its whole subtree (`namespace.rs:5249-5254`, `:5475-5490`).
- FINDING: installed profile, read-only snapshot — two commits at 2026-07-29 11:53 UTC (folder lists) and 11:56 UTC (Main list) tombstoned **32,144 items**: 22,452 attachments, 5,540 generated docs, 2,700 month dirs, 1,311 story appearances, 140 chats. 99.0 % of the dead attachments had a tombstoned chat as grandparent — a cascade, not independent deletions.
- FINDING (false delete): `SELECT sum(left_at_ms IS NOT NULL), sum(deleted_at_ms IS NOT NULL) FROM chats` → `0 | 0` over 32,815 chats. Telegram never witnessed a departure for any of the 140 chats. There is no corroboration check and no shrink floor on a membership commit.
- FINDING: 41,864 `item_changes` rows point at tombstoned items, so `GramDriveEnumerator.changeOutcome` (`GramDriveEnumerator.swift:275-286`) reported them to macOS as `didDeleteItems` — Finder removing them was correct provider behaviour.
- FINDING (causal separation): no production `tombstone_item` caller is reachable from a content fetch. `namespace.rs:5487` (reconcile) and `accounts.rs:650` (`set_retention_mode`) are the only two; every other call site is inside `mod tests`. `set_chat_history_priority` writes only the scheduler ledger (`namespace.rs:520-536`). A failed Preview/Open cannot delete an item.
- FINDING (the other, separate mechanism): the provider has six sites that answer a *read failure* with `NSFileProviderError.noSuchItem` — `ContentFetcher.swift:268,273,296,439`, `FileProviderExtension.swift:179,188`, `ThumbnailFetcher.swift:130,200,205`, `GramDriveEnumerator.swift:177,334`. The chain `SourceError::NotFound` → `FailureCategory::NotFound` → `DriveError::NotFound` → wire `notFound` → `noSuchItem` fires on a **live** row; `is_source_object_gone` (`crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/download.rs:1261-1267`) matches TDLib code 404 / `MESSAGE_ID_INVALID` / literal `Not Found`. One `failed/not_found` transfer is already recorded on this profile.
- FINDING: 55,947 of 82,198 live generated docs (68.1 %) have neither `content_version` nor `logical_size` — `capabilities` `[]`, `fileSystemFlags` `[]` (mode 0000), `fetchContents` refuses with `serverUnreachable` before any IPC. That is the honest cause of "downloads fail even for small files"; it does **not** remove items.
- ANOMALY: the File Provider extension has **zero** logging (`grep -rn "Logger(\|os_log" apple/GramDriveSupport/Sources/GramDriveFileProvider` → empty). Which error was returned to macOS for which item is unobservable; this investigation needed a durable-state autopsy instead.
- ANOMALY: attachment `metadata_seed` (`namespace.rs:4727-4738`) omits availability, so a fetchable→unavailable flip can leave `itemVersion` unchanged and macOS may ignore the capability change. Story seeds do include it.
- SCOPE: `.research/260729_finder-item-disappearance-after-failed-fetch.md` (TASK-260729-28fwdq outcome). Feeds BUG-260729-3uclm3.
- STATUS: pending — read-only research, no product change. Highest-severity follow-up is fail-closed chat-list membership; the `noSuchItem`-on-live-row narrowing is BUG-260729-3uclm3's core.

### 1615 — `log` is shadowed in this shell — use `/usr/bin/log` for unified-log evidence
- ANOMALY: `log show --predicate … --style compact` fails with `(eval):log:2: too many arguments` — `log` resolves to an oh-my-zsh function, not `/usr/bin/log`. It exits non-zero with empty stdout, which reads exactly like "no matching entries".
- NOTE: cost one wrong intermediate conclusion during TASK-260729-2iuklm (`log show … | grep` misread as "no auth log lines" when the command had not run). Corrected in the artefact.
- FIX (convention): always invoke `/usr/bin/log` by absolute path in agent evidence commands, and never pipe a gate command into `grep`/`tee` without checking the real exit code.
- STATUS: resolved — recorded so the next session does not repeat it.

### 1612 — Sign-in finalization mutates the live account key-first and non-atomically
- FINDING: `persist_account` (`crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/auth.rs:1012-1034`) runs `store_database_key(real, slot_key)` → `wipe_account(real)` → `rename(slot → real)` → `write_account_row`. Step 1 replaces the incumbent account's keychain DB key **before anything is proven**; a failure at step 2 or 3 leaves the previous database either gone or unopenable.
- FINDING: combined with the write-once `auth_state` (entry 1608), the wreckage presents as durable row `authorized` + namespace `auth-required` + Sign In offering login — the exact divergence under investigation.
- ANOMALY (coupling): the `_account_guard` at `:1012` excludes the namespace session (`namespace.rs:444` takes the same scope) only because `stopAllNamespaces()` blocks on an unbounded `worker.join()` (`namespace.rs:556-563`). Making that teardown non-blocking to fix the UI stall would silently re-open the finalize race.
- STATUS: pending — hazard, not observed on the installed profile. FIX-5 in `.research/260729_auth-state-divergence-and-sign-in-noop.md`; must not be split from FIX-3 across releases.

### 1610 — Auth control path has no bounded wait anywhere, and `isSubmitting` can latch for the app's lifetime
- ROOT CAUSE (perceived Sign In no-op): `begin()` sets `isSubmitting = true` and renders **nothing** — `.starting` only arrives as the agent's first wire event, so the whole observable effect of a click is the button greying out (`AuthorizationView.swift:56-59`).
- FINDING: the wait is unbounded on both sides — auth socket `SO_RCVTIMEO = {0,0}` (`ControlClient.swift:162-165`), bare `await iterator.next()` (`LiveAuthorizationSession.swift:66`) — and the agent cannot write its first event until `beforeSession` → `stopAllNamespaces()` → `NamespaceSession::shutdown()` joins the worker thread unbounded (`AgentMain.swift:91`, `namespace.rs:556-563`).
- FINDING (permanent latch): `LiveAuthorizationSession.cancel()` awaits `waitUntilFinished()` with **no timeout and without closing the channel** (`:110-113`); it resolves only when the agent tears the connection down, i.e. after the Rust pump exits — and `pump()` returns on only `Closed` / `Ready` / `AuthState::Closed`, looping forever on `Timeout` (`auth.rs:862-895`). A stalled pump disables every Sign In control until the app is relaunched.
- FINDING: three buttons call `submit(...)` with no `isSubmitting` disable — "Use QR code instead", "Resend code", "Cancel" (`AuthorizationView.swift:61, 100, 115`) — and `submit()` silently returns while submitting. Literal unmarked no-ops.
- EVIDENCE (installed, 2026-07-29): the app was launched 3× in 38 s (15:50:45, 15:51:13, 15:51:23), each spawning and killing its own agent child, immediately before a sign-in that then succeeded at 15:52:32 → 15:53:19. Both mechanisms predict that relaunching is what unblocks sign-in.
- STATUS: pending — FIX-3 in `.research/260729_auth-state-divergence-and-sign-in-noop.md`.

### 1608 — "Account: Authorized" while Sign In offers login is a modelling defect, not a race
- ROOT CAUSE: `accounts.auth_state` is **write-once `"authorized"`**. The sole production writer is `write_account_row` with a hard-coded literal (`crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/auth.rs:1073`); the only removal is `DELETE FROM accounts` on account removal (`crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/accounts.rs:518`). Nothing ever demotes it — not revocation, not password change, not key loss, not database corruption. Verified by grep over the whole crate tree.
- ROOT CAUSE: `AuthorizationViewModel.state` is seeded from **nothing** — initial `.idle` (`:16`), sole mutator `apply()` fed only by a live session stream (`:118`). It never reads health, the account row, or TDLib. `.idle` renders "Not signed in. Start to authorize a Telegram account." (`AuthorizationView.swift:87`).
- FINDING: therefore every launch with an existing account shows Account = "Authorized" and Sign In = "Not signed in". Deterministic, reproducible offline, no timing involved.
- FINDING: the agent *does* detect real auth loss — `SessionFailure::AUTH { retryable: false }` (`namespace.rs:590-594`) — and then discards it. One health payload can simultaneously carry `accounts[].authState == "authorized"` and `finderContentFailure = "Telegram authorization expired. Sign in again to retry."` (`AgentLifecycle.swift:508, 567, 628-630`). Three screens, three different answers, one payload.
- EVIDENCE (installed profile): account row `created 2026-07-20 17:04Z`, `updated 2026-07-29 11:53:19Z`; `upsert_account` preserves `created_at_ms` (`accounts.rs:391-400`), so both timestamps are independent facts — a full re-sign-in ran to success on a machine whose row had read `authorized` for nine days.
- NOTE (observability): no GramDrive-originated line exists in the unified log — `subsystem == "com.reluxworks.gramdrive"` returns 0 entries over 24 h, and the agent's stdout/stderr go to `FileHandle.nullDevice` (`AgentLauncher.swift:127-128`). The only sign-in telemetry that exists is *incidental*: Security.framework's `SecKeyVerifySignature` bursts, which dated this machine's session start (15:52:32) and finalize (15:53:19) to the second.
- STATUS: pending — TASK-260729-2iuklm research handed to review; FIX-1/FIX-2/FIX-6 in `.research/260729_auth-state-divergence-and-sign-in-noop.md`.

## 2026-07-28

### 2306 — Generated-document hydration times out under crawl load: 2 of 6 reads failed (BUG-260728-2qfzbd review → new bug)
- FINDING (reviewer's own sample, live installed build 0.1.1 (75), preserved profile, while the agent was crawling): five fetchable generated documents plus one probe read — successes at **4.8 s, 5.3 s, 10.5 s, 26.3 s**, failures at **27.2 s and 112.4 s with `ETIMEDOUT` (errno 60)**. 3 of 6 over ten seconds, 2 of 6 failed outright. Corroborates and *strengthens* the round-6 disclosure (4.6–11.8 s, 2 timeouts) — it was under-reported, not overstated.
- USER-VISIBLE: opening `Messages.md` / `.chat.json` in Finder fails with a timeout a meaningful fraction of the time while backfill is running.
- DECISION (scope): **not rework in BUG-260728-2qfzbd.** Byte-delivery latency of an already-projected document is outside that bug's scope (history discovery/cursors, projection, migrations, item metadata) and outside every clause of its AC. Not caused by the round-6 change either: the demand hint is raised when the fetch resolves its item, before a byte moves — the after-relaunch probe row returned zero bytes with errno 60 and still delivered its hint pair and still bought its chat a 751-message turn.
- DECISION (routing): needs its own bug, not a reopen of `BUG-260727-j6o56o installed-generated-documents-fail-to-open` (done) — that was "fails to open", this is "slow and intermittently times out under load".
- NOTE (hypothesis for that bug, untested): both failures landed while the agent was saturated crawling — worth checking contention between the hydration handler and the namespace crawl worker over the state store. Also worth folding in: `ContentFetcher.performFetch` raises the demand hint *behind* `gate.acquire()` (`maxConcurrentFetches = 4`), so under exactly this latency a fifth read's hint can wait minutes, while everything the raise needs is a local SQLite read with no IPC.
- STATUS: pending — recommended to the orchestrator as a separately scoped bug; evidence in `BUG-260728-2qfzbd_round6-reviewer-evidence.json`.

### 2253 — Every 1970 date on the profile is a real 1970 Telegram timestamp (BUG-260728-2qfzbd review)
- FINDING: the installed probe's `every_epoch_date_comes_from_epoch_stamped_source` is a *global existence* check (`epoch_dated == 0 or epoch_stamped_source_messages > 0`) — it cannot fail while any epoch-stamped message exists anywhere in the account, so it does not by itself close the AC clause "never report 1970 when message timestamps exist".
- EVIDENCE (resolved per chat against the live database instead): the 4 epoch-dated live directories are two appearances of one chat (`created = 0`, `modified` real) and their two `1970-01` month folders. Exactly one epoch-stamped source message exists, in chat `-1002418145096`, which holds 3,910 messages with `min(sent_at_ms) = 0`, `max = 1785263274000`. Every 1970 shown is derived from a genuine 1970 Telegram timestamp.
- NOTE: to make that gate carry its own weight it needs the item-id → chat mapping the foreground probe already implements in `chat_appearance_item_id` (`.scripts/acceptance/run_installed_foreground_demand.py`); the index-metadata probe's comment says SQL cannot join the two, which is true only without that helper.
- STATUS: clause verified; check strength is a follow-up nicety, not a defect.

### 2240 — Reviewer accepted BUG-260728-2qfzbd round 6 (RUN-260728-4c6f97)
- DECISION: **accepted**. Round 6 delivers exactly the orchestrator's A+B decision, both halves mutation-tested and measured on the exact signed installed build.
- EVIDENCE (re-verified live by the reviewer, not the developer's numbers): agent hint counters stood at `accepted 12 = requested 6 + background 6` — confirming round 6's prose figure that was not in the attached JSON — and one generated-document read moved them to `accepted 14 = requested 7 + background 7`, delta exactly `requested +1, background +1`. One raise, one release, correct account routing, nothing leaked.
- EVIDENCE (gates re-run independently on the handed-off tree): `check-core` 6/6 (test leg 1655.7 s), `check-apple` 2/2 (411 tests in 65 suites, count verified in the run log), `check-repo` 2/2, `check-live-content` 1/1 (344.5 s). All exit 0.
- CLOSED (round-4 blocking finding 1): every headline number in the round-6 results reproduces exactly from the `relaunch` phase of the attached evidence JSON.
- CLOSED (round-4 blocking finding 2): the AC's named completion clause is now satisfied by measurement — chat 216133163 at `history_complete = 1` with exact set equality against a fresh MTProto export on both months (July 2,542/2,542, June 1,829/1,829, nothing earlier).
- CLOSED (round-4 finding 3): `reconcile_nodes` now leaves `aggregate_size: None` for directories owning no rollup; `no_directory_claims_a_rollup_it_does_not_own` went 29 → 0.
- NOTE (non-blocking, carried): raise/release ordering across two reads of one chat is not sequenced (harmless only because of the admission ledger); `enumerateChanges` raising `visible` also sits on the system's change-signal path, not only on a user gesture; first-boot retryable failures are still terminal (pre-existing, gated on `namespaceReadyAccounts`).
- STATUS: resolved — verdict in `BUG-260728-2qfzbd_round6-review-verdict.md`.

### 2227 — The reliable "user is in this chat" signal is the content read, not the folder open (BUG-260728-2qfzbd round 6)
- DECISION (orchestrator, adopted): A+B from the round-5 stop. `fetchContents` raises `Requested` for the enclosing chat; folder-open-only interaction is served by the fair v17 background rotation. Option C (Accessibility / Apple Events) rejected — no extra permissions.
- FIX (A): `ContentFetcher.performFetch` raises `requested` demand for `ItemMetadata.chat_id` as soon as the item resolves — *before* the fetchability refusals, because a POL-4 restricted attachment a user tried to open says the same thing about where they are — and releases it when the read settles (`apple/.../GramDriveFileProvider/ContentFetcher.swift`). Refcounted per chat so overlapping reads raise once and release once. The extension hands the fetcher its one `HistoryPrioritySignaling` seam (`FileProviderExtension.swift:90`).
- DECISION (why released, not left standing): `plan_next` walks the requested tier before the backlog, so an unreleased hint pins that chat to the foreground until its history completes; a session of them rebuilds the starvation this bug exists to fix. The release is safe precisely because of round 5's admission ledger — the chat is owed one turn even when the release wins the race to the next scheduler boundary.
- EVIDENCE (installed signed build, preserved profile, same probe, 60 s windows): pre-fix build — a content read delivered **0** hints, no turn, exit 1. Candidate — **2** hints (1 requested, 1 background), turn granted, **751 messages** crawled backward, exit 0. After a real app+agent relaunch — same, on a fresh agent process. `--gesture open` on the fixed build — **0** hints, no turn: decision B measured rather than asserted.
- EVIDENCE (release discipline): six content reads after the relaunch moved the agent's counters to exactly `accepted 12 = requested 6 + background 6`. One raise, one release, per read, none leaked.
- FINDING (latency, out of scope, worth its own bug): generated-document hydration on this profile takes 4.6–11.8 s per `.chat.json` and **timed out** (`ETIMEDOUT`) twice while the agent was saturated crawling right after a relaunch. The demand path is unaffected — the hint is raised before any byte moves, and the timed-out read still bought its chat a turn — but a user reading `Messages.md` under load can see a failed open.
- FIX (instrument): the probe reports a failed read (`document_bytes_read: 0` + errno) instead of crashing, so the windows either side stay measurable; `--gesture read|open` selects the gesture; hint counters are read from the agent status endpoint either side of it, connecting by leaf from inside the socket directory because the group container's path exceeds `sun_path`.
- SCOPE: `apple/.../ContentFetcher.swift`, `FileProviderExtension.swift`, `crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs` (regression test), `.scripts/acceptance/run_installed_foreground_demand.py`, `.scripts/tests/test_run_installed_foreground_demand.py`, `.scripts/acceptance/README.md`, `README.md`.
- STATUS: delivered; check-core 6/6, check-apple 2/2 (411 tests), check-repo 2/2, check-live-content 1/1, package + package-app all exit 0; index-metadata after/relaunch all seven booleans true, 0 cursors regressed.

### 2117 — macOS never asks the extension when a materialized folder is opened: the foreground demand signal does not exist (BUG-260728-2qfzbd round 5)
- FINDING (platform, blocking): on this replicated File Provider domain, macOS answers a read of an already-materialized folder entirely from its own copy of the namespace and never invokes the extension's enumerator. A plain `readdir` and a real Finder window held open on a chat folder for 90 s both delivered **0** history-priority hints to the agent — not `accepted`, not `unroutable`. Corroborated by the extension process's CPU time, which does not move across those reads. `enumerateItems` is therefore not a usable "the user opened this chat" trigger, and neither is `enumerateChanges`.
- DECISION (instrumentation): added `AgentHealthSnapshot.historyPriorityHints` — counts by kind plus `unroutable`, one timestamp, never a chat identity (`apple/.../AgentHealth.swift`, `AgentLifecycle.swift:703`). The demand path spans two processes and a socket, so "the opened chat did not advance" had two indistinguishable causes; a count at the receiving end separates them on any installed build. This is what turned the finding above from an inference into a measurement.
- ROOT CAUSE (agent side, fixed): `ContentDemandState::set(chat, Background)` removed the chat outright. The provider signals `Visible` on enumeration and `Background` on `invalidate()` milliseconds later, while the namespace worker is still inside another chat's crawl — so both edges landed between two snapshots and cancelled out.
- FIX: the demand queue now separates the host's **live view** (still the only input to slice preemption) from a bounded **admission ledger** of hints not yet spent on a turn. A release clears the live view and leaves the admission standing. `open_next_history_turn` plans against live+unspent and settles only what the plan demonstrably reached — `Paused`/`Wait`/`Idle{Offline}` spend nothing, reaching `Requested` proves the whole visible tier was walked, reaching `Background` proves both tiers were. An admission watermark covers the window where a hint lands while `plan_next` runs outside the lock (`crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs`).
- FIX: `should_continue_history_slice` reads the live view only — a chat that is *owed* a turn is not a chat *on screen*, and letting it preempt would cut every background slice to one page while it waited — but never lowers the active chat below the priority its turn was granted at, so a released `Visible` hint still buys a foreground-sized turn.
- EVIDENCE (installed candidate, after an app+agent relaunch on the preserved profile): the exact `visible`→immediate-`background` pair a Finder open produces granted a turn 45 s after delivery and crawled **751 messages backward**. On the round-4 build the identical sequence produced no turn in 75 s.
- ANOMALY (own instrument): the first version of the installed probe passed once and failed twice on the same build. Cause: it selected the chat with the newest `last_backfill_at_ms`, and the scheduler stamps a turn when it *hands work out* then keeps the crawl for several ticks — so the newest stamp is usually the crawl in flight, whose own progress reads as an effect of the open. Now excluded by a 180 s grace window, mutation-checked.
- BLOCKED: the contract clause "verify ordinary Finder open without a manual socket hint" cannot pass without a product decision on which signal represents "the user is in this chat". Options: (A) derive the hint from `fetchContents`, which provably does reach the extension; (B) drop the folder-open-prioritises claim and rely on the v17 rotation, which already converges every chat fairly with no foreground signal; (C) observe Finder from outside the File Provider model — invasive, needs granted permissions, and is the compensating-hack path. Recommended A+B.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs`, `apple/.../AgentHealth.swift`, `AgentLifecycle.swift`, `GramDriveEnumerator.swift`, `.scripts/acceptance/run_installed_foreground_demand.py`.
- STATUS: fix implemented and proven on the installed build; all gates green; verification clause blocked on the decision above.

### 1912 — The backfill rotation key was contaminated by live delivery: busy chats starve (BUG-260728-2qfzbd round 3)
- ROOT CAUSE: `ReadTxn::backfill_backlog` ordered the backward-crawl rotation by `chat_sync_state.last_sync_at_ms`, and that column has two writers — `apply_history_commit` *and* `apply_live_commit` (`crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs`). Every incoming message therefore reset its own chat's place in the queue. The busier a correspondence is, the less history it gets: exactly inverted, and a third starvation path distinct from the `degraded`-exclusion one fixed in round 1.
- EVIDENCE (installed profile, round-1 candidate, 28.7-minute window, 285-chat rotation): the reported chat took 1 real turn and crawled 1,040 messages backward. A second sampled chat took **0** turns and was reset **twice** by live traffic alone with zero backward progress — it climbed to rank **11**, one turn away after ~40 minutes of waiting, and a single incoming message threw it back to rank **284**. A third chat, quiet during the window, crawled 6,835 messages backward in one turn.
- FIX: schema v17 adds `chat_sync_state.last_backfill_at_ms`, written only by `WriteTxn::record_backfill_turn`, and the backlog orders on it. `last_sync_at_ms` keeps its own meaning (when anything was last observed) and its live writer; it just stops deciding who crawls next. Index `chat_sync_state_backfill_turns` replaces `chat_sync_state_backlog`, which had no reader left and was maintained on every live message.
- FIX: the turn is stamped where the work is *handed out* — `open_next_history_turn` in `crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs`, extracted from `drive_history_page` precisely so the wiring is testable — not where a page commits. A turn spent on a spacing wait, a source error, or an empty page is still a turn; keying on success would let one stuck chat hold the head of the queue forever.
- DECISION: existing rows migrate to NULL rather than inheriting `last_sync_at_ms`. NULL sorts first, so every incomplete chat is guaranteed one turn before any chat gets a second — a bounded, self-correcting repair for profiles that already starved, costing exactly one rotation.
- NOTE: v14 made the same correction on the render side (recently refreshed chats monopolizing the publication worklist). Same failure shape, different queue; worth checking any future ordering key for a second writer.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/schema/v17.sql`, `src/schema.rs`, `src/migrate.rs`, `src/repo/changes.rs`, `crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs`, `.scripts/acceptance/run_installed_index_metadata.py`.
- STATUS: implemented, regression tests mutation-checked; installed verification in progress.

### 1647 — Installed-evidence tables must be restatable from a retained probe run (BUG-260728-2qfzbd review → to-dev)
- FINDING: the reviewer re-ran every gate on the handed-off tree and all four passed independently — `check-core` 6/6, `check-apple` 2/2, `check-repo` 2/2, `check-live-content` 1/1, each exit 0. The implementation itself was accepted on the merits; both blocking items were in the delivered evidence.
- ANOMALY: the Outcome table in `BUG-260728-2qfzbd_results.md`, labelled as the post-relaunch state, matches neither phase of the `installed-evidence.json` attached beside it — 35,447 / 35,414 / 5,525,607,834,124 bytes / 428 cursors / 62 newly complete / 1,178,837 messages, against `after` 35,295 / 35,262 / 5,457,389,376,664 / 426 / 61 / 1,171,681 and `relaunch` 36,253 / 36,220 / 5,809,788,151,228 / 433 / 72 / 1,248,775. The board notes carry a third set again (33,391→45, 34,780/34,780, 423/45, 5.24 TB). The `before` column matches its run exactly, so the drift is confined to the post-fix column; values sit between the two retained runs and move in the right direction, so this is a table written against a probe run that was later overwritten, not an invented figure.
- DECISION: on a live namespace that grows during the observation window, every number quoted on the board must be restatable from a retained run, and each column must name the phase it is. Interleaving several probe runs into one table makes the primary durable record uncheckable even when the underlying acceptance booleans are all true.
- FINDING: the AC clause naming complete July for the reported chat was unmet at handoff and said so only in `.temp/.../reproduction-private.md`, deliberately kept off the board: 1,436 → 1,803 of 2,547 July messages, oldest loaded id still inside July, the earlier month still absent. The board artifact reported only that the month folder grew from 174 to 202 entries, which reads as convergence. The mechanism fix is separately proven (the previously excluded chat moved `degraded` → `syncing`; 426/433 cursors advanced, 0 regressed, 61/72 newly complete; every listed incomplete chat reachable).
- DECISION: a throughput-bound AC clause is legitimate to leave open, but it has to be left open *on the board* with its measured counts. A private file the coordinator never reads does not discharge the disclosure.
- FINDING (code, non-blocking): `reconcile_nodes` gives every directory node `facts` via `unwrap_or`, but only chat / month / `Active Stories` nodes are inserted into `directory_facts` — so chat-list and folder-catalog directories publish `aggregate_size = Some(0)` for subtrees holding terabytes, where v16's SQL correctly left `NULL`. Invisible only because macOS ignores `documentSize` on directory items, the same platform behavior this task documented.
- FINDING (code, non-blocking): namespace recovery is still gated on `namespaceReadyAccounts.contains(accountId)`, so a `retryable: true` failure before an account ever reaches ready — including the `startNamespace` catch path — remains terminal until relaunch. Pre-existing, same failure shape as the six-hour stall this bug fixed.
- SCOPE: review only, no code modified. Verdict and full evidence in `BUG-260728-2qfzbd_review-verdict.md`.
- STATUS: changes requested → `to-dev`; artifact correction and one AC disclosure, no product-code rework implied.

### 1520 — Chat folders had no dates, no size, and two starvation paths (BUG-260728-2qfzbd → to-review)
- INSTALLED BASELINE (preserved authorized profile, schema v15): 33,391 of 33,395 live directories carried a null timestamp, which Finder renders as 1 Jan 1970; 599,771 of 624,223 files reported an epoch Date Modified; no directory published any size at all — the v1 `items` CHECK forbids `logical_size` on a directory.
- ROOT CAUSE (stall): `AgentLifecycle.isRecoverableSourceFailure` allow-listed only source-ish categories, so a `retryable: true` storage/projection/render failure permanently stopped the owned namespace session. The live agent had reported `finderContentState=failed`, category `storage`, after fifteen `namespace-ready` events, and had then sat idle for six hours while still running, still holding the domain, and telling the user to relaunch.
- ROOT CAUSE (starvation): `backfill_backlog` excluded every `degraded` chat as a "terminal retry-on-demand" row. Degradation is the engine's own re-crawl fence (live gap, buffer overflow, unretained edit ids), not a source refusal. 59 of 388 incomplete listed chats were unreachable by background history work and could only advance if the user opened them in Finder.
- SOURCE GROUND TRUTH: three pre-existing chats each exposed exactly one month (2026-07). Telegram source held 2,547 July messages for the target against 1,436 indexed (43.6% missing) plus a whole earlier month; the other two reach back to 2019-06 and 2021-02.
- FIX: schema v16 adds `items.aggregate_size` and backfills every chat/month/`Active Stories` directory in pure SQL, identifier-preserving. The projection derives directory dates from `message_instants` in the pass that already reads them (chat: first/last indexed instant; month: first/last inside the month; no-correspondence chat: `chats.last_update_at_ms`), and attachments/story appearances publish their source instant as both dates. Recovery keys on the core's own `retryable` flag with exponential backoff capped at 300 s, reset on ready.
- REGRESSION AVOIDED: the first implementation re-reconciled the whole chat after each render publication and measured 81.6% sustained agent CPU — above the ~70% bound BUG-260727-2ct7o0 established. Replaced with `WriteTxn::refresh_ancestor_rollups`, bounded by tree depth and sibling count. Both owners compose the directory metadata version through one shared helper (`gramdrive_model::version::directory_metadata_version`) so neither can undo the other.
- FINDING (platform): macOS FPFS does **not** surface a directory item's `NSFileProviderItem.documentSize`. A chat folder whose provider item published 292,062,466 bytes reads back as `documentSize = 0` through `fileproviderctl evaluate`, while `creationDate`/`contentModificationDate` from the same fetch are exact. Finder's folder size comes from the descendant walk instead — which now matches the published rollup byte-for-byte (4/4 sampled chats) and adds no verified media blob.
- FINDING (delivery): a one-time metadata migration over a 661k-item namespace leaves a large working-set backlog (709k journalled changes). Directory dates appeared in Finder for 1,017 of ~1,053 chat folders within the first hour; the remainder drain behind that queue. Not a correctness defect, but installed evidence must account for it.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/schema/v16.sql`, `repo/items.rs`, `repo/changes.rs`, `crates/gramdrive-model/src/version.rs`, `crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/namespace.rs`, `crates/gramdrive-engine/src/render_pipeline.rs`, `apple/.../FileProviderItem.swift`, `FileProviderExtension.swift`, `AgentLifecycle.swift`.
- STATUS: to-review.

## 2026-07-27

### 0281 — Account-wide backfill is dominated by ineligible canonical chats (BUG-260727-2ct7o0)
- INSTALLED BASELINE: the preserved authorized current namespace contains 10,244 canonical chats but only 1,697 chats with a live Main, Archive, or custom-folder membership (2,268 provider appearances). All 10,244 canonical rows own history cursors; 8,036 are incomplete, including 6,350 incomplete cursors for chats with no live list membership.
- ROOT CAUSE: the `chats_seed_sync_state` trigger and relaunch initialization seed runnable history state for every canonical metadata chat. The bounded scheduler correctly orders its input, but that input includes thousands of chats outside the approved provider namespace; 6,145 ineligible rows were actively `pending` or `syncing`, keeping the installed agent at 99.3% CPU while ordinary visible chats rarely advanced.
- PUBLICATION GAP: 2,268 visible chat appearances still measured 1,552 with zero direct months, 696 with exactly one, 20 with multiple, and 26 with any pre-2026-07 month; 39 render rows remained dirty. Eligibility and cursor lifecycle must be corrected before scheduler tuning can demonstrate broad convergence.
- FIX: schema v13 makes live Main, Archive, or custom-folder membership the durable history eligibility boundary, seeds cursors only on first eligibility, retires ineligible work, and schedules bounded least-advanced chat slices without foreground starvation. Schema v14 orders dirty generated publication by its durable input watermark/render time instead of item identity, so one deep control chat cannot monopolize monthly export work. Startup projection reads only listed chats, history projection is incremental, provider signals are coalesced to real changes, and both TDLib and render quanta are bounded.
- HYDRATION RACE: a generated export request can race a newly published version after admission. A cache miss for that stale admitted version is now a retryable version conflict rather than a false source-unavailable result; the next File Provider request binds to the current generated bytes.
- HUMAN FEEDBACK: the old installed candidate reproduced selective older folders and an intermittent generated-document open that remained blocked for over 90 seconds. The fixed acceptance probe selects three independent chats that have a terminal cursor and every currently stored source month cleanly published, then compares Finder-opened `Messages.md` and `Messages.ndjson` byte-for-byte with managed current materializations twice at each oldest/newest boundary (24 opens total). This catches partial month coverage, incomplete current-month exports, and stale generated hydration rather than counting folders alone.
- BOUNDEDNESS: the first v14 candidate still measured marginal 80.2–81.4% five-second CPU windows while draining publication work. Raising the sustained-update yield to 150 ms and reducing the provider-visible render worklist from eight to four appearances per tick gives deliberate headroom without changing durable least-advanced-first fairness or eager-hydrating media.
- PRIVACY: all measurements are aggregate-only; no account/chat identifiers, names, message content, paths, or source IDs were recorded.

### 0280 — Active-story-only chats have no approved provider parent (BUG-260727-2txbfr → blocked)
- INSTALLED EVIDENCE: privacy-safe aggregate reads on the preserved authorized profile found 31 live active-story appearances across 10 chats, including 20 save-permitted stories with primary locators. Ordinary chat-list membership implies exactly 30 provider story appearances and the projection contains exactly 30 live `story_appearance` items under 9 non-empty `Active Stories` folders; discovery, locator persistence, ordinary-view projection, and publication therefore reconcile without a count gap.
- ROOT CAUSE: 8 active stories across 3 chats are exposed by TDLib `storyListMain` while those chats have zero Main, Archive, or custom-folder position. The canonical story rows and active appearances persist, but the approved provider tree constructs chat folders only from `chat_list_entries`, so these story-list-only chats have no parent appearance and cannot be shown in Finder.
- CONSTRAINT: the approved domain model limits chat appearances to Main, Archive, and custom folder views, while PRD-010/J2 require those views to preserve Telegram chat-list membership and order. Synthesizing a Main membership would misstate Telegram truth; adding a distinct top-level Stories view requires a new product layout, view identity/order contract, migration/projection behavior, and File Provider acceptance scope.
- DECISION NEEDED: choose either (A) add an explicit Telegram Stories chat-list view driven by `chatActiveStories.list/order` (recommended: truthful source semantics, larger additive model/layout change), or (B) define Chats as a documented union of Telegram Main plus story-list-only chats (smaller change, but Main membership/order are no longer exact). Until one is approved, product-code work stops rather than fabricating membership.

### 0279 — Inclusive TDLib cursors and scoped publication restore installed history (BUG-260727-3k11lo → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: `getChatHistory(offset=0)` includes `from_message_id`, but both history crawl and live gap recovery rejected that boundary as a contract violation. The strict-less-than mock hid the production stop after the first page. Independently, every history page and every snapshot commit rebuilt the account-wide provider tree (some paths rebuilt it twice), so a growing namespace could saturate the agent and delay later chat scheduling/publication.
- FIX: the source machines accept exactly one leading inclusive boundary, advance only on older IDs, treat boundary-only backward pages as terminal, and preserve typed no-progress failures. The owned session commits each normalized page/cursor transaction immediately, runs bounded 8-page background or 16-page foreground slices, publishes message/history changes through a chat-scoped projection, defers snapshot projection to its durable boundary, and keeps live/story/history scheduling fair without issuing media downloads. Scoped publication falls back to a full projection only for a never-projected chat and has a sibling-subtree preservation regression.
- PROOF: on the preserved authorized profile, one untouched target moved from one discovered/rendered month and no cursor to a terminal cursor with 116 non-empty months; every discovered month has both generated exports, the earliest discovered month precedes the initial projection, and zero verified media cache entries were added. After signed-app relaunch all 116 months remained rendered, the selected oldest and initial month export pairs were visible through File Provider paths, 83 cursors progressed with zero missing/regressed rows, health stayed ready, and root enumeration remained under 10 ms during background metadata work. Full source and 136-test FFI suites, strict Clippy/rustfmt, workspace build, four provider change-signal tests, linked core packaging, signed app packaging, and installed deep-signature verification exited 0.

### 0278 — Installed hydration must rebind through the canonical owner (BUG-260727-ukx740 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: persisted TDLib numeric `file_id` values are process-local. `getRemoteFile` restored a current numeric ID after relaunch, but its standalone file had no owner source and an empty Telegram file reference; the production trace reached `FILE_REFERENCE_EMPTY` and then `File source is not found`. Hydration now resolves the durable remote locator with its exact TDLib file type, refreshes and verifies the canonical message/story owner, persists only the owner-backed locator, and then issues ranged downloads.
- INDEPENDENT STORAGE ROOT CAUSE: retention replay legitimately queued generated `Messages.md`/`Messages.ndjson` objects, while the physical cache host accepted only blob-store paths. Startup rejected its own generated-document purge journal as `snapshot-cache-policy-storage`. The host now permits only exact owned generated filenames under a canonically contained generated root; traversal, wrong names, and outside paths remain rejected.
- PROOF: the clean Developer ID candidate opened three distinct previously dataless/uncached Finder placeholders with exact verified bytes (3,830 bytes, 3,830 bytes, and 2,178,942 bytes across the 512 KiB fetch grid). Relaunch preserved all sampled identities/cache entries and every prior cursor, the namespace remained additive-only, Finder returned `ready`, and pending transfers returned to zero on the preserved authorized profile. `make check-core` passed 6/6, `make check-live-content` passed 1/1, full relevant Rust suites and 392 Swift tests passed, linked core/app packaging and installed deep-signature verification exited 0, and public tags/releases remained unchanged.
- DIAGNOSTIC: one deliberately oversized 2 GiB exploratory Finder sample hit the platform's 60-second request window; the requested cancellation was reconciled on clean startup and pending work returned to zero. It is not one of the three acceptance samples. The oldest fresh placeholder was independently gone at Telegram (`getMessage: Not Found`) and remained an item-local `not_found`, not a global namespace failure.

## 2026-07-26

### 0277 — Post-ready Telegram source faults preserve Finder’s projected namespace (BUG-260726-3gxvcu → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the live namespace loop promoted an unavailable `getChat` lookup for one newly observed, unresolved chat into an account-wide `SessionFailure::SOURCE`; Swift then mapped every failed owner callback directly to `finderContentState=failed`, even after durable date-first content had reached ready.
- FIX: unresolved chat metadata failure now drops only that bounded item-local buffer, emits a retryable `chat-metadata` degradation, and retries naturally on a later update.
