Swift records proven readiness separately from current owner progress: post-ready retryable source failures keep durable enumeration usable, expose an additive privacy-safe degradation, and recreate only that account’s namespace owner after a bounded delay; initial, authorization, storage, integrity, and nonretryable failures remain globally actionable.
- PROOF: deterministic Rust and Swift regressions exercise ready → item-local/transient source fault → recovery while preserving existing appearances, identities, cursor, and retention state. The preserved signed installed profile remained running with one authorized account, zero pending transfers, three root items, and `ready` after ordinary relaunch; the accepted comparison preserved all 11,985 prior items with three additive-only discoveries, all 3,343 cursors with zero missing/regressed rows, Mirror retention, and the exact hydrated sample. Focused/full Rust and Swift tests, strict Clippy/rustfmt, linked packaging, signed app packaging, and canonical `make check-live-content` exited 0.
- DIAGNOSTIC: the first task-local `stability-snapshot` exited 1 before database checks because a fresh evidence path had not been initialized; the first `after` attempt then truthfully exited 1 because the minimal seed lacked the public `privacy_safe` baseline. Reusing the preserved profile’s already validated public evidence fixed the harness setup without resetting credentials, content, domain, or private sample state.

### 0276 — Quiescent relaunch must retain the exact one-open sample (TASK-260721-3qm381 rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: `stability-snapshot` refreshed aggregate item/cursor state by selecting the newest verified fetchable cache row and overwrote the private sample identity, size, and digest. A newer unrelated hydration could therefore make the last relaunch pass against the wrong attachment.
- FIX: the phase now loads the prior private tuple, queries the verified cache row by that exact item identity on every quiescence poll, fails with distinct missing/size/digest categories, and refreshes only aggregate state. Regressions cover an original row plus a newer unrelated row and missing/size/hash failures. Finder read failures now use the fixed `placeholder-hydration-read-failed` category.
- PROOF: the corrected no-open snapshot reached three stable polls; relaunch preserved the exact sample and hydration, all 11,510 item identities, all 3,339 cursors, Mirror retention with Archive off, and observed one story transition. The 11-test harness suite, Python compile, scoped diff, `make check-repo`, privacy validation, installed/backup deep signatures, and public tag/release hash comparisons exited 0.
- DIAGNOSTIC: a fresh replacement hydration attempt encountered a retryable TDLib/network source failure and published zero verified cache entries; after source recovery a single retry failed with the actionable Finder hydration-read category. No further placeholder was opened, and the already accepted one-open private sample was used for the requested corrected relaunch proof.

### 0275 — Installed acceptance must prove a fresh placeholder and compare real relaunch state (TASK-260721-3qm381 rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the first task-local harness treated an already verified cache row as placeholder evidence, forced enumeration materialization false for that cached case, and labeled presence/count-of-rows checks as stable item-count/cursor progress. Signed packaging also enforced TDLib linkage only behind an opt-in flag that the canonical Make targets did not pass.
- FIX: the tracked installed-profile harness now refuses verified-cache candidates, requires Finder `dataless` before and after date-first enumeration, opens content exactly once, compares the sampled identity and full private item set, and checks every persisted cursor with the documented oldest-down/newest-up/completeness-nonregression contract. Public evidence is allow-listed; raw keys/digests stay private. Signed and notarized packaging require a linked core by default; the explicitly unsigned assembly remains the diagnostic path.
- FINDING: live metadata discovery can legitimately add items immediately after `ready`. The harness reports exact count delta, exact-set equality, and additive-only preservation separately and exits nonzero on a required false claim. After quiescence, the accepted relaunch held the full 11,182-item set exactly, preserved all 3,331 prior cursor rows with 3 additive rows and zero regressions/missing rows, and retained the one sampled hydration.
- PROOF: canonical `make package-app`, installed/backup deep signatures, 7 installed-harness regressions, 60 packaging regressions, Python compile/diff validation, and `make check-repo` all exited 0. One strict interim after-phase truthfully exited 1 while discovery added 28 items. Public tag and GitHub release hashes matched their pre-install baselines.

### 0274 — Signed live acceptance exposed packaging and transactional rename gaps (TASK-260721-3qm381 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the signed app packager accepted a core manifest with `tdjson.linked=false`, so a structurally valid Developer ID bundle could never start live content. After linked installation, a real snapshot also exposed an acyclic sibling rename chain that SQLite rejected when reconciliation upserted the blocked destination first.
- FIX: live signed packaging now requires linked TDLib; synchronous namespace construction preserves privacy-safe actionable categories; projection reconciliation tombstones stale children first and orders acyclic sibling renames from the free destination while preserving stable IDs. Fixed stage/constraint categories isolate any future live failure without source names or paths.
- PROOF: the installed app/agent/appex deeply verify under `262RZ595FP`, one authorized account/domain reaches ready, date-first text and one allowed placeholder hydration verify, and sampled identity/cursor/retention/hydration survive relaunch. Public tag/release hashes are unchanged. Focused Rust/Swift regressions, 58 packaging tests, linked core/app packaging, formatting, bytecode, diff, and installed-signature gates exited 0.

### 0273 — Live date-first acceptance is one bounded synthetic Rust/Swift gate (TASK-260721-1iop6l → to-review)
- DECISION: the accepted component suites remain the behavioral authorities; the new `live-content` acceptance suite composes their real Rust history/live/story, render, state/retention/scale, FFI hydration/policy, Swift build, and full provider/agent/companion regression legs instead of creating a parallel product simulator.
- PRIVACY: child stdout/stderr is never persisted. `live-content.json` has an exact allow-list limited to fixed scenario/fixture labels, booleans, counts, exit codes, millisecond timings, tool versions, and configured bounds. The record is capped at 64 KiB and every leg has a 900-second deadline; timeout terminates the entire child process group.
- PROOF: the real combined run passed all 7 legs in 158.2 seconds; evidence was 1,701 bytes with exact recorded/actual size agreement. The full script suite passed 279 tests. An initial suite/step naming collision caused recursive catalog expansion and was corrected by keeping `live-content` as the suite and `live-content-matrix` as its distinct step.

### 0272 — Ambiguous policy failures reconcile committed state without mutation replay (TASK-260721-2dcrkn rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: Audit-to-Mirror can commit Mirror before physical purge cleanup fails, and Archive Mode can commit before its response or follow-up status projection fails. The companion reconciled only dropped retention responses, so typed retention failures and all ambiguous Archive outcomes could leave stale pre-command state visible.
- FIX: every failed retention mutation now performs a status-only read, preserves the actionable failure, and adopts the actual committed mode plus resumable purge count. Archive response drops and typed failures perform the same read-only reconciliation and adopt the actual toggle/backfill status. No mutation is automatically replayed, and controls remain locked only until the read settles so an overlapping user mutation cannot race it.
- PROOF: regressions cover commit-then-storage-failure for Audit-to-Mirror and commit-then-drop/storage-failure for Archive. Each asserts exactly one mutation and one separate status re-read; the UI model reports Mirror with three resumable purges or Archive on with seven pending and two failed allowed items. Focused 15-test and full 388-test Swift suites, Swift build, and final strict scoped format lint pass with exit 0.

### 0271 — Destructive confirmation is consumed before sheet dismissal (TASK-260721-2dcrkn rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the SwiftUI destructive button dismissed its item-driven sheet before the asynchronous confirmation method could read the prompt. The resulting binding clear and `onDismiss` callback could remove the local gate first, suppressing the typed Audit-to-Mirror command. Archive failure copy also returned after the category and hid reported pending/failed counts.
- FIX: the destructive action now submits through the model without a pre-confirm dismissal; the model consumes the prompt before its first backend suspension, while cancel and system dismissal remain command-free. Archive failure copy composes the failure category with every available pending and failed allowed-item count.
- PROOF: a regression runs confirmation before repeated dismissal callbacks and observes exactly one typed command; the Archive fixture asserts the displayed 7 pending and 2 failed values. The focused 13-test suite, full 386-test Swift package, scoped strict Swift format lint, Swift build, and scoped diff checks pass with exit 0.

### 0270 — Production Archive status and companion recovery close review findings (TASK-260721-2dcrkn → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: Archive backfill progress existed in control fixtures but the production FFI status omitted it; retention command failures could leave the picker permanently disabled; and system dismissal of the typed-confirmation sheet did not clear its local awaiting state.
- FIX: state now derives pending and durably failed Archive candidates from the same eligibility/current-generation rules as the production worklist and exposes the latest deterministic failure category through FFI and the typed control bridge. The companion treats an ordinary command failure as retryable, keeps dropped-command recovery in a no-replay reconciling state until any later successful refresh, and clears the local confirmation gate from sheet `onDismiss` without issuing a command.
- DECISION: a failed Archive transfer remains a subset of pending work because it still requires successful backfill; failure evidence comes from the durable transfer journal, so status survives relaunch without inventing progress. Destructive recovery remains read-only: later refresh may settle the UI but never resubmits Audit-to-Mirror.
- PROOF: a real file-backed state/FFI fixture covers pending plus durable `disk_full` failure. Swift tests cover system dismissal, later-refresh reconciliation without replay, and retry after an ordinary failure. Full gramdrive-state and 385-test Swift suites, focused FFI tests, strict Clippy, Rust/Swift formatting, packaging, Swift build, and diff validation passed with exit 0.

### 0269 — Companion policy controls reread committed state and never replay destructive mutations (TASK-260721-2dcrkn → to-review)
- DECISION: Mirror/Audit and Archive Mode are separate per-account commands on the existing typed agent control socket. The companion treats the engine response as authoritative, preserves the legacy global Archive settings field only for forward/backward document compatibility, and filters policy reads to authorized account identities.
- SAFETY: Audit-to-Mirror is fail-closed without the exact non-empty agent phrase. Cancel and mismatched text make no call; an accepted phrase sends one command. If the socket drops after submission, the companion performs a status read and never replays the destructive request, while durable pending-file purges remain explicitly resumable.
- TRUTH: the current FFI status reports retention, Archive on/off, and purge count but not Archive backfill counts. The wire uses optional backfill progress and the UI says “not reported” instead of inventing zero/current; restrictions copy explicitly excludes protected, view-once, self-destruct, secret-chat, ephemeral active-story, unseen, and forbidden content.
- PROOF: typed server/client fixtures and companion tests cover exact confirmation, prospective copy, relaunch, multi-account isolation, unavailable/failure states, low disk, dropped-command reconciliation, purge resume, Archive failure/progress copy, and old/future decoding. `make package`, the 382-test Swift suite, post-test Swift build, Swift format lint, and diff validation all passed with exit 0.

### 0268 — Telegram protection is a destructive message-content boundary, not a render hint (TASK-260721-2tamdj rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: `can_be_saved=false` and protected-chat observations still serialized message text/captions/relationships/reactions/service payloads, and renderers treated `protected` as an attachment warning after emitting those fields. A later chat-protection update redacted stories but did not revoke existing message payloads, monthly render publications, provider metadata, or Archive pins.
- FIX: restricted normalization now removes every copyable body fact and media locator before persistence; per-message Audit history is purged to one body-free placeholder. Chat protection atomically clears all event payloads, permanently redacts attachment names/locators/byte facts, releases retained/live cache/blob/pin ownership, journals physical files, invalidates generated documents, and reconciles restricted provider rows. Removing protection republishes only from the empty surviving state, so old plaintext and locators cannot reappear. Markdown/NDJSON renderer v4 defensively emits only a non-content placeholder and suppresses Audit revisions whenever the current observation is protected.
- FINDING: production Archive pins originally recognized canonical attachment ids, but date-first provider attachments are appearance ids. Archive eligibility now includes direct-month attachment appearances; the protection transaction releases them without touching other accounts.
- PROOF: fixtures cover per-message `can_be_saved=false`, chat allowed→protected→allowed replay, Mirror/Audit × Archive off/on, preexisting render caches, provider/change-journal invalidation, account isolation, non-resurrection, and purge-journal ownership. Final state/render/source/126-test FFI suites, strict workspace Clippy, all-target build, diff/format checks, and `make check-core` 6/6 pass with exit 0.

### 0267 — Authoritative restrictions must enumerate detached Audit attachment owners (TASK-260721-2tamdj rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: restriction cleanup enumerated only current `attachments` rows. Audit-retained same-index versions were not explicitly released, and an attachment removed by a later edit had no current row at all, so its locator-free retained metadata and materialized-byte owner stayed invisible and intentionally protected from eviction/deletion.
- FIX: the state repository now enumerates distinct account-scoped retained canonical attachment keys by decoding their durable `ItemId`. Production cleanup merges those keys with live attachments, re-evaluates chat protection, current attachment saveability/view-once state, and restricted provider projections, then transactionally removes every retained version, journals each physical object, releases live cache/blob ownership, and purges only account-local orphan blobs.
- PROOF: production relaunch fixtures cover materialized and metadata-only same-index history under chat protection, `can_be_saved=false`, view-once, and restricted projections with Archive Mode both off and on; the detached removed-index path covers Audit/Audit+Archive, zero refetch, idempotent replay, and preservation of a shared second-account object/cache/pin. Full state and 124-test FFI suites, strict workspace Clippy, all-target build, and `make check-core` 6/6 passed. Intermediate format and Clippy gates truthfully exited 1 and 101 before their respective formatting and needless-borrow corrections.

### 0266 — Audit must retain attachments removed by index, not only same-index replacements (TASK-260721-2tamdj rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: attachment reconciliation transferred a superseded version into `retained_attachment_versions` only when an incoming row reused the same index with a new content version. An edit that omitted the old index instead deleted the canonical row directly under Audit, so later projection tombstoning could release Archive/cache ownership and leave already verified bytes evictable.
- FIX: the absent-index branch now captures the current attachment as locator-free Audit metadata and moves any matching verified cache materialization into the retained-version owner before deleting canonical metadata. Mirror continues to queue and release the object destructively. Retained rows create no Archive work and survive tombstoning, relaunch, Archive disable, and eviction scans until a typed confirmed Audit-to-Mirror transaction queues their physical purge.
- PROOF: persistent state fixtures cover materialized and metadata-only omissions under Audit and Audit+Archive, no backfill demand, eviction exclusion, relaunch, and destructive replay. The production hydration fixture additionally proves zero refetches and shared-object safety across accounts. The focused production fixture initially exposed two test-only assumptions (a missing second-account blob FK and global retained+shared byte accounting), both corrected before the full 123-test FFI suite and `make check-core` 6/6 passed.

### 0265 — Archive has a production consumer and Audit owns superseded attachment versions (TASK-260721-2tamdj rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: Archive eligibility stopped at a tested worklist with no owned-session consumer, while allowed-to-allowed attachment edits overwrote the canonical content version without first transferring or releasing its verified cache ownership. Mirror could therefore keep superseded bytes and Audit had no durable, version-addressable owner for bytes it was required to retain.
- FIX: the namespace loop now dispatches one paced Archive candidate through the existing hydration/admission/promotion path. A native `NWPathMonitor` plus periodic power/disk sampler supplies real host conditions; the Rust session starts fail-closed until that snapshot arrives, suspends on low/critical disk, metered/offline network, power saving, request spacing, pause, and flood wait, and cancels account work when its source unregisters.
- DECISION: schema v12 stores Audit-retained superseded attachment versions as locator-free metadata plus an optional verified blob/materialization owner. Audit never creates download demand from these rows. Mirror queues the superseded physical object transactionally; confirmed Audit-to-Mirror queues retained objects and removes their rows in the same crash-idempotent transaction. Reconciliation drops only missing retained byte ownership while preserving observed metadata, and cache accounting treats retained bytes as policy-pinned.
- PROOF: production-path fixtures prove Audit alone/Archive off, low disk, and restrictions make zero fetches; Archive plus Mirror/Audit fetches allowed attachments, and Audit plus Archive fetches a persistent profile story. Attachment replacement fixtures cover Mirror purge, Audit retention across relaunch, eviction exclusion, and confirmed destructive purge. State/engine/122-test FFI suites, 370-test Swift suite, package contract 0.15.0, strict Clippy, workspace build, and `make check-core` 6/6 pass with exit 0.

### 0264 — Metadata removal must capture materialized ownership before post-hoc policy scans (TASK-260721-2tamdj rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: Mirror deletion and allowed-to-expired replacement could remove or overwrite the only attachment row that identified materialized bytes before the hydrator's account scan ran. Admission and startup cleanup were correct for surviving restricted rows, but a scan cannot recover an identity or blob link already erased by the canonical mutation.
- FIX: live attachment reconciliation now receives the source observation timestamp and, in the same SQLite transaction as deletion/expiry, derives the canonical item, releases every pin origin, removes the account cache owner, journals the physical reference, clears the verified attachment blob link, and purges only account-local orphan blob metadata before applying the metadata mutation.
- INVARIANT: Audit ordinary deletion still retains already materialized allowed bytes. Expiry is authoritative and purges under Mirror, Audit, and Audit plus Archive Mode. Physical deletion remains crash-idempotent and is skipped when any other account/cache row still references the same object; the queued owner can then acknowledge without violating cross-account isolation.
- PROOF: new physical-file regressions cover Mirror deletion with and without a shared second-account owner, expiry across all retention/archive combinations, Audit ordinary-deletion retention, and production relaunch. Full state and 120-test FFI suites, strict workspace Clippy, workspace build, and `make check-core` 6/6 pass with exit 0.

### 0263 — Authoritative attachment restrictions now erase owned bytes and startup replays retention purges (TASK-260721-2tamdj rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the attachment admission resolver refused later-protected, non-saveable, view-once, and restricted content, but the authoritative update path did not remove an already materialized cache row, pin, blob link, or file. Audit-to-Mirror also persisted a crash-safe purge journal without invoking its replay from the production hydrator construction path.
- FIX: authoritative restriction cleanup now transactionally removes the affected account's cache ownership and pin, unlinks canonical attachment blobs, deletes only unreferenced blob rows, and queues physical deletion through the existing idempotent purge journal. Owned-session commits invoke cleanup, and production hydrator startup drains both retained Audit-to-Mirror work and restriction work before exposing the session.
- ISOLATION: cleanup is account-scoped at every state mutation. Physical objects remain when any cache row in any account still references their materialization, and Audit deletion remains eligible to retain already materialized allowed bytes because deletion alone is not classified as a Telegram restriction.
- PROOF: focused restriction-mode, multi-account, and production-relaunch regressions pass. The first full FFI run correctly failed one superseded explicit-repair count expectation (exit 101); after updating that fixture to startup replay semantics, all 117 FFI tests passed. `make check-core` then passed 6/6, including all-feature workspace tests, strict Clippy, architecture, and supply-chain checks (exit 0).

## 2026-07-22

### 0262 — Retention and Archive Mode are account-scoped, transactional, and crash-resumable (TASK-260721-2tamdj → to-review)
- DECISION: retention and byte ownership remain orthogonal. Mirror/Audit changes are prospective except the destructive Audit-to-Mirror invariant repair; Archive Mode alone creates persistent allowed-content pins and eager-media work. New live items join Archive coverage in their projection transaction, and an authoritative unavailable/restricted/tombstoned projection releases Archive-owned intent without overriding an explicit user pin.
- SAFETY: Audit-to-Mirror accepts an exact typed phrase bound to one account, then atomically purges superseded/deleted event payloads, deleted attachment metadata, removed profile-story metadata, orphan blobs/cache rows/pins, tombstones affected provider items, and dirties generated renders. A v11 account-scoped file-purge journal commits before cache ownership disappears; filesystem deletion and acknowledgement are safe to repeat after any crash boundary and preserve shared materializations.
- PRIVACY: Archive candidates are limited to live fetchable attachments and persistent monthly story appearances. Active stories, protected/unavailable/view-once content, and already verified materializations create no eager work; the existing source/hydration gates remain authoritative above both settings.
- PROOF: fixtures cover typed-confirmation rollback, Mirror/Audit attachment deletion, removed-story/blob purge, per-account isolation, prospective mode switching, post-toggle live Archive pinning, restriction release, low-disk suspension, durable reopen/acknowledgement, and FFI relaunch cleanup. `make check-core` passed 6/6 (all-feature workspace lint/tests, architecture and supply chain); affected state, engine, and 114-test FFI suites, formatting, strict Clippy, and workspace check all exited 0.

### 0261 — Story projection composes date-first appearances with one hydrated canonical blob (TASK-260721-1u6zc4 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the completed story repository and date-first tree were not composed into the provider projection; active snapshots also persisted allowed story facts without their typed locators. The generic `items_appearance` uniqueness rule predated location-scoped story identities and rejected the required coexistence of an active tombstone with its monthly successor.
- FIX: provider reconciliation now projects active stories only under `Active Stories`, persistent/Audit-retained stories once into their direct `YYYY-MM`, recursively tombstones removed branches, and journals only actual changes. Schema v10 preserves the ordinary one-appearance invariant while excluding `story_appearance` rows and retains an indexed canonical lookup. Active ingestion persists the allowed locator catalog.
- BYTES/POLICY: story items use the existing ranged TDLib download, refresh, promotion, and cache paths. Promotion links the verified blob to canonical `(poster_chat_id, story_id)`; an active→month open reuses the same materialization without a second cache row or fetch. Mirror/ordinary expiry and later protection purge disallowed cache rows and unshared objects; Audit removal serves only already observed bytes and never initiates a missing download. Relaunch reapplies cleanup after projection convergence.
- PRIVACY/PROOF: protected stories project restricted, locator-free, size-free placeholders; generated Markdown/NDJSON contain no protected sentinel. Lifecycle tests cover transition, normal expiry, Mirror/Audit removal, active-only Audit expiry, repeated projection/paging, journal idempotence, allowed locator refresh via non-viewing `getStory`, cache reuse, physical purge, and protection-after-materialization. Relevant state/source/engine/FFI suites, strict Clippy, all-target build, and formatting pass with exit 0.
- REVIEW REWORK: reasonless `updateStoryDeleted` correctly retained an Audit profile story's canonical blob link while changing it to inaccessible, but the post-projection cleanup still deleted the matching cache row/object because it admitted only currently fetchable stories. Cleanup now retains only a matching canonical blob for an Audit, inaccessible, removed monthly appearance; Mirror, active-only expiry, and protected states still purge. A composed regression materializes once, applies `StoryCommit::Inaccessible`, rebuilds/cleans projection, and serves the same bytes with one total source fetch. The FFI suite now has 112 passing tests; affected-crate tests, strict Clippy, workspace all-target check, formatting, and diff validation pass with exit 0.

### 0260 — Active-story discovery checkpoints new chats before dependent stories (TASK-260721-3e9bi8 rework cycle 4 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: `loadActiveStories(storyListMain)` can emit `updateNewChat` and `updateChatActiveStories` before its response, but the owned session buffered chat metadata and story reducers independently. The story commit could therefore reach SQLite before the previously ordered chat row and fail its foreign-key/storage lookup.
- FIX: the story-request composition performs a final ordered update-queue drain after the response becomes observable, then atomically applies the accumulated chat metadata/projection batch before `StoryMachine::on_response` can expose dependent story commits. No viewing, download, or mutation request was added.
- PROOF: an FFI owned-session regression starts with chat 777 absent, receives both updates while `loadActiveStories` is pending, verifies the chat checkpoint precedes the story commit, then replays the pass after a simulated relaunch. It converges to one canonical story, one active appearance, generation/page progress 2/2, and no failure. The 30-test namespace suite and 103-test FFI all-target suite pass; strict workspace Clippy, all-target workspace build, formatting, `make check` 8/8, and diff validation pass.

### 0259 — Account-wide story loading, typed locators, and profile pins close review cycle 3 (TASK-260721-3e9bi8 → to-review)
- DECISION: the owned session runs bounded `loadActiveStories(storyListMain)` calls to TDLib's documented 404 exhaustion before attaching per-chat work, so chats absent from the ordinary namespace worklist are still discovered and global failures cannot be misattributed to one chat. Account-level generation/page/complete progress is committed independently; a relaunch starts a new resumable pass, and an account namespace bump lazily creates its new scoped progress row without rewriting the retired epoch.
- DECISION: this supersedes 0256's interim locator-free model for save-permitted content. Canonical stories now own typed, byte-free locator rows for every useful photo size or video/alternative/thumbnail role, with exactly one deterministic primary source and exact `getRemoteFile` file type. The primary locator version is the canonical content version; duplicate replay replaces the same roles and never duplicates a story or blob.
- PRIVACY: captions and raw content remain excluded. Story- or chat-level protection atomically clears MIME/size, locators, and the canonical blob link; protected/unavailable rows cannot insert locators or link bytes at either repository or schema boundaries.
- APPEARANCE: first-page `pinned_story_ids` is persisted as profile-month appearance order, never canonical identity. Live updates without ordering evidence preserve it; a new first page clears and reapplies the order transactionally.
- PROOF: regressions cover an active-list-only chat, 404 exhaustion/relaunch progress, exact photo/video/thumbnail locator roles, protected erasure, duplicate replay/blob identity, pin preservation/reorder, and the existing expiry/profile/removal/crash/archive-rights cases. `make check` passes 8/8 after one independently reproduced transient runtime-close timeout; full all-target tests, all-target build, strict all-feature Clippy, formatting, migrations/query plans/schema invariants, and diff validation pass.

### 0258 — Short TDLib story pages advance without reconciling absence (TASK-260721-3e9bi8 rework cycle 2 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the shared profile/archive reducer treated `stories.len() < limit` as exhaustion even though TDLib explicitly permits short pages for performance. A short profile page could therefore purge older persistent appearances that had not been fetched, while a short archive page truncated owner/manageable backfill.
- FIX: the reducer now derives the inclusive cursor from the last raw returned story id and completes only on an empty page or a repeated/non-decreasing cursor. Every non-empty decreasing page advances regardless of length; inclusive-boundary observations remain deduplicated.
- PROOF: source regressions cover short profile continuation across crash/relaunch and short archive continuation through a second page; composed FFI coverage proves unseen profile appearances survive each non-terminal commit, relaunch uses the persisted cursor, and absence becomes authoritative only after the empty terminal page. Full source/state/FFI suites, all-target build, strict Clippy, formatting, and `make check` 8/8 pass.

### 0257 — Ready story scans recheck archive head and transient rights (TASK-260721-3e9bi8 rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: a Ready story scan preserved `archive_complete` across owned-session relaunch, so stories created and expired entirely during downtime were skipped. A failed management-right lookup was also persisted as complete, making temporary uncertainty permanent.
- FIX: each new owned session resets only Ready scans to a fresh active/profile/archive head and clears prior rights evidence before archive dispatch. In-progress scans keep their inclusive archive cursor at every committed page. `rights_unavailable` remains fail-closed and incomplete; only proven ordinary/account-unsupported states are terminal for that scan, and the next session retries exact rights classification.
- PROOF: regressions cover a downtime-only owner story discovered from `from_story_id=0`, unavailable rights followed by recovered creator rights with zero premature archive requests, distinct unavailable/ineligible durable completion, and interrupted cursor preservation after multiple page boundaries. Full TDJSON source, state, and FFI suites pass; `make check` passes 8/8, plus all-target build, strict Clippy, formatting, and diff validation.

### 0256 — Story discovery treats membership and rights as authority (TASK-260721-3e9bi8 → to-review)
- DECISION: the owned TDLib session persists one story row per `(poster_chat_id, story_id)` and separate byte-free active/month appearances. A profile observation deletes the active appearance in the same transaction; an authoritative empty active set purges only never-profiled stories, while completed profile scan generations apply Mirror/Audit removal policy.
- PRIVACY: background story dispatch is a hard metadata-request allow-list and cannot construct `openStory`, live-view lifecycle requests, `downloadFile`, or mutations. The source vocabulary deliberately has no caption/content JSON/TDLib locator/path/bytes; `can_be_forwarded=false` is redacted before that boundary, and state independently strips locator-shaped MIME/size input before storing the unavailable placeholder.
- RIGHTS/RESUME: archive pages are requested only for a regular owner's own chat or exact creator/admin `can_edit_stories` evidence. Inclusive page boundaries are deduplicated; page facts and cursor/counters share one transaction. Retryable terminal failures do not hot-loop in-session and retain cursors for relaunch requeue.
- LIMIT: pinned TDLib exposes active membership but no received expiry timestamp, so active disappearance follows authoritative membership updates and no deletion/expiry cause is invented.
- PROOF: source request/protection/rights/pagination tests, state duplicate/transition/removal/protection/crash-relaunch tests, and owned-session canonical/privacy/atomic-page tests cover the contract.

### 0254 — Pinned TDLib stories expose membership, not an exact expiry clock (TASK-260721-2e6sbq → to-review)
- FINDING: TDLib commit `022d60202e446ad1287b9fb68e687c8a0760788b` has no `expire_date` in `story` or `storyInfo`; `active_period` exists only on the outgoing `postStory` request. Active expiry must therefore follow `updateChatActiveStories` membership removal. `updateStoryDeleted` is reasonless “became inaccessible,” so deletion, expiry, privacy loss, and unposting cannot be reconstructed honestly.
- PRIVACY: the pin has no `viewStories` constructor. `openStory` explicitly opens/views a static story, while `joinLiveStory` joins an active live story; `closeStory` and `leaveGroupCall` are their UI lifecycle peers. `getGroupCall`, `getGroupCallStreams`, and `getGroupCallStreamSegment` remain explicit-live-viewer-only because discovery needs none of them and the latter two enumerate/retrieve live media. All seven are forbidden in background discovery. `downloadFile` has no documented story-view side effect but remains explicit-hydration-only.
- RIGHTS: own non-bot stories and chat creators/admins with `chatAdministratorRights.can_edit_stories` can use `getChatArchivedStories`; ordinary or unknown-right chats must not be probed and must expose an unavailable/unknown archive status instead of an empty result.
- LOCATORS: persist the role-derived `getRemoteFile.file_type`: story photo → `fileTypePhotoStory`, primary/alternative story video → `fileTypeVideoStory`, file-backed preview → `fileTypeThumbnail`; a legacy unknown role passes explicit null. `minithumbnail` has no file locator, and protected normalization erases `file_type` with every other locator field.
- CLASSIFIER: owner eligibility requires `getMe.type=userTypeRegular` and target `chat_id=getMe.id`; `userTypeBot` is known ineligible. Managed-chat lookup uses `messageSenderUser(getMe.id)` and trusts only a matching `chatMember`: creator is eligible, administrator requires `rights.can_edit_stories`, and lookup uncertainty fails closed.
- PROTECTION: `story.can_be_forwarded=false` explicitly forbids saving as well as forwarding/screenshots. Normalization must drop caption, areas, content, locators, local paths, and bytes before durable state, retaining only an honest metadata placeholder.
- LIVE PLACEHOLDER: `storyInfo.is_live=true` / `storyContentLive` remains `live_story_viewer_unavailable` metadata with zero group-call requests until a separately approved user-visible viewer owns join, streams, and leave.
- EVIDENCE: `.research/260722_tdlib-stories-capability-contract.md`; 70 pinned declarations and 25 synthetic wire fixtures locked by `.scripts/tdlib/story_contract.py` and 12 fail-closed unit tests. No TDLib client or Telegram account was invoked.

### 0253 — Provider-visible attachment names and refreshed previews invalidate stale caches (TASK-260721-3adrx2 rework cycle 4 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: attachment metadata versions were finalized before deterministic sibling collision resolution, so an existing placeholder could be renamed without advancing Finder's metadata version. Thumbnail reuse treated an equal byte length as cache identity even though preview locators intentionally refresh independently of the main content version. Enumerator cancellation fixtures also let their artificial stall expire after one second, making full-suite load decide the result.
- FIX: attachment metadata versions are now derived from stable attachment facts plus the final parent, display name, collision-resolved safe name, and availability. Preview publication reuses only byte-identical cache content and atomically replaces equal-length changes across agent relaunch. Cancellation fixtures remain blocked until the test explicitly releases them, with bounded 30-second scheduling assertions.
- PROOF: incremental collision coverage keeps the item identifier stable, advances the renamed placeholder's metadata version, and proves projection-rebuild idempotence. Thumbnail coverage changes equal-length bytes under the same item/version/dimensions after recreating storage and observes replacement. The full 370-test/63-suite Swift package passed three consecutive runs (including concurrent Rust/Clippy load); 94 FFI tests plus the combined TDJSON/FFI suites, strict Clippy, formatting, architecture, rebuilt package contract 0.13.0, post-package Swift build, and diff validation pass.

### 0252 — Thumbnail byte, format, and pixel bounds are enforced before Finder publication (TASK-260721-3adrx2 rework cycle 3 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the source byte cap covered downloadable preview files but not persisted base64 minithumbnails; animated preview formats could be downloaded even though File Provider accepts Image-I/O image data; and requested dimensions were sent to the source without validating the actual returned image pixels.
- FIX: base64 input and exact decoded length are checked against the preview cap before output allocation, with strict terminal/canonical padding. TDLib preview candidates now require known non-zero dimensions inside `ThumbnailSpec`, and MP4/WebM/TGS candidates become a zero-network no-thumbnail result. The macOS provider decodes the staged payload through Image I/O, downsamples one frame inside the requested box, re-encodes bounded PNG data, and rejects corrupt or over-cap output before the Finder completion callback.
- PROOF: Rust boundary tests cover cap, cap-plus-one, malformed base64, dimensionless/oversize candidates, and no-network non-image fallback. Swift tests verify a 320×240 source is delivered inside 64×32 and corrupt image bytes produce typed `cannotSynchronize`. Full Rust FFI/TDJSON suites, strict Clippy, formatting, crate architecture, the 370-test/63-suite Swift package, `swift build`, and diff validation pass.

### 0251 — Unknown attachment extents fail closed; File Provider task ledgers register atomically (TASK-260721-3adrx2 rework cycle 2 → to-review)
- DECISION: TDLib `expected_size` remains an estimate, never a logical-size claim. A projected attachment with no exact extent keeps `documentSize == nil`, advertises neither `allowsReading` nor a readable filesystem flag, and an ignored-capability `fetchContents` call returns retryable `serverUnreachable` without contacting the agent. Discovering and republishing exact extent during open would require a new source/engine metadata-state contract outside this File Provider task.
- FIX: content and thumbnail tasks are inserted into their in-flight dictionaries under the same lock their terminal removal takes, before immediate local refusal/no-thumbnail work can finish. Terminal callbacks observe the entry already removed. Thumbnail dimensions now clamp in `CGFloat` space before `UInt32` conversion, so any finite system request—including values above `UInt32.max`—is safely bounded.
- PROOF: new Swift regressions cover the expected-size-only capability/fetch surface with a no-IPC spy, immediate content refusal and no-thumbnail ledger cleanup, and `CGFloat.greatestFiniteMagnitude` thumbnail requests. The focused 39-test slice, full 368-test/63-suite Swift package, `swift build`, and `git diff --check` pass.

### 0250 — Thumbnail network bounds and unavailable admission survive stale provider state (TASK-260721-3adrx2 rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the thumbnail source checked its 4 MiB policy only after issuing TDLib `downloadFile` with `limit: 0`, which TDLib defines as unbounded. A missing or wrong projected size could therefore hydrate full media before the response guard rejected it. Separately, agent admission folded both durable `.restricted` and `.unavailable` into the permission-denied wire category, losing the provider's explicit unavailable result when state changed after its local snapshot.
- FIX: thumbnail requests now use `limit = max_preview_bytes + 1`; TDLib auto-cancels at that byte count, and the sentinel byte proves overflow for unknown-size locators before any local read. Known oversize previews remain zero-request. Agent admission now maps `.restricted` to `restricted` and `.unavailable` to `notFound`, which the File Provider surface maps to `fileReadNoPermission` and `noSuchItem` respectively.
- PROOF: unit and mock-runtime regressions assert the exact bounded TDLib request and reject an unknown-size oversized locator after only the sentinel prefix. Swift admission and content-fetch regressions cover the fetchable-snapshot → unavailable-at-agent race end to end. Rust FFI/source tests (93 + 222 unit tests plus integrations), strict Clippy, formatting, rebuilt package contract 0.13.0, and the freshly relinked 365-test Swift suite pass.

### 0249 — File Provider thumbnails use dedicated preview locators, never content hydration (TASK-260721-3adrx2 → to-review)
- DECISION: Finder thumbnail requests share the existing version-pinned hydration IPC framing but carry an explicit `thumbnail` purpose and bounded dimensions. The agent routes that purpose to `TdThumbnailer` over the one owned TDLib client; it does not call the full-content `FetchCoordinator` and never falls back to a media locator when the source has no preview.
- POLICY: Both extension-side admission and agent-side durable admission re-check current attachment availability before source access. Restricted placeholders fail with an explicit permission category, unavailable placeholders fail as not found/unavailable, and ineligible document representations return no thumbnail without contacting the agent. Enumeration remains a shared-state-only read.
- FIDELITY: Provider metadata tests distinguish original `messageDocument` image attachments (sender filename/MIME/exact logical size) from Telegram media representations (generated name, no claimed sender filename), while preserving the source message creation date and stable item identity.
- VERIFICATION: 93 Rust FFI tests and the full 363-test serial Swift suite pass; focused provider paging/change, metadata/fidelity/date, content-fetch cancellation/retry/relaunch, hydration-wire/server, and thumbnail bounds/policy/cancellation/retry/no-eager-fetch coverage pass. `cargo fmt --check`, strict FFI Clippy, `swift build`, `make package`, and `git diff --check` pass. Package contract remains 0.13.0; public release state was not changed.

### 0248 — Zero-byte hydration callers now own cancellable subscriptions (TASK-260721-1yp75l rework cycle 6 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: exact-zero attachments used sink-less durable demand and returned no reader handle. Multiple opens could coalesce onto one queued transfer, but either cancellation looked like the last reader and cancelled work still owned by the other opener.
- FIX: `FetchCoordinator::subscribe` gives sink-less on-demand demand a per-caller `ReaderId` in the same synchronized registry and atomic close count as ranged readers. Hydration uses it for exact-zero objects inside the existing per-content admission boundary; only the last live ranged reader or subscriber may request durable/source cancellation. Terminal transfer paths retire subscriber bookkeeping without inventing a byte sink.
- PROOF: deterministic four-worker tests pause every driver before claim, attach two zero-byte hydrations to one transfer, cancel one, and prove the other publishes the verified empty object from zero source fetches. A companion test cancels the sole zero-byte subscriber while queued and proves a cancelled journal row, no cache row, no staged ranges/reference, and no partial file after the released driver observes the terminal row. The pair passed 20 consecutive runs.
- VERIFICATION: all source/source-tdjson/engine/FFI suites, strict affected-crate all-target/all-feature Clippy, workspace all-target/all-feature build, formatting/diff checks, 29 focused Swift hydration tests, and board validation pass. Public release state was not changed.

### 0247 — Hydration completion is reserved before durable transfer publication (TASK-260721-1yp75l rework cycle 5 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: cycle-4 stored only a weak completion keyed by transfer id, but `FetchCoordinator::open` made a new transfer globally claimable before the opener looked up that completion. An already-running driver could promote first, resolve a temporary completion, and drop it; the opener then created a fresh unresolved channel over a durable `done` row and waited indefinitely.
- FIX: each opener now creates a strong causal reservation keyed by `(item, content_version)` before requesting durable work, then binds it to the returned transfer id. A producer that wins the gap adopts and resolves that exact reservation. Terminal results remain strongly addressable for a scheduled two-minute grace—twice the native idle deadline—so an already attached coalesced opener can bind after producer completion; identity-checked expiry and non-retry terminal cleanup prevent historical retention, while requeued attempts retain their causal completion across backoff.
- PROOF: deterministic four-worker tests keep an unrelated driver active, pause a second opener after its transfer commit but before binding, and let the existing driver publish first. The success case returns exact second-item bytes and exercises identity-checked cleanup; the failure case deletes staging before promotion and returns typed `Integrity` within a timeout with no cache row, blob link, or staging partial. A third regression pauses an already attached coalesced opener until its producer publishes, then proves both readers return the one exact cached object from one source fetch. The producer-first pair passed 20 consecutive runs.
- VERIFICATION: all affected source/source-tdjson/engine/FFI suites, strict all-target/all-feature Clippy, workspace all-target/all-feature build, package contract 0.13.0, formatting/diff checks, 29 focused Swift hydration tests, and the full 352-test serial Swift suite pass. Public release state was not changed.

### 0246 — Transfer completion and cache publication are causally joined (TASK-260721-1yp75l rework cycle 4 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the transfer engine durably marks a verified transfer `done` before the host promotes its staging file and commits the verified cache row. Hydration waited on a process-wide change signal, so an unrelated driver could wake a reader inside that valid window and misclassify missing cache state as integrity failure. Separately, the cache fast path checked only item availability and could serve bytes after the owning chat became protected, deleted, or left.
- FIX: a weakly retained per-transfer completion channel now publishes the exact promotion result after cache publication; `done` readers wait only on that causal channel, surface promotion failure explicitly, and ignore unrelated notifications. Driver launch happens only after the channel exists. One shared state attachment-eligibility resolver now gates both cache access and TDLib catalog lookup from item, attachment, and chat policy facts.
- PROOF: deterministic four-worker tests pause after engine completion and before cache publication, inject an unrelated notification, and verify the reader remains pending until exact bytes are published. A second test deletes staging in that window and proves an explicit integrity failure with no cache row. A composed cache regression flips an already materialized chat through protected, left, and deleted states and proves typed refusal, zero additional source fetches, and unchanged cache-access accounting.
- VERIFICATION: the affected four-crate normal parallel suite passed three consecutive runs; the publication-window regression passed 20 consecutive runs; strict all-target/all-feature Clippy, workspace all-target/all-feature build, formatting/diff checks, and the 29-test Swift hydration slice pass. Public release state was not changed.

### 0245 — Last-reader cancellation is atomic with new hydration admission (TASK-260721-1yp75l rework cycle 3 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: cycle-2 serialized `open → cancellation reset → driver launch`, but last-reader close still ran outside that same per-content admission. A new reader could attach after the zero-reader observation and then be cancelled by the old request, or create/reset a fresh source generation before the old request called `RoutingSource::cancel`, redirecting cancellation onto the new TDLib fetch.
- FIX: `begin_cancel_reader` now owns one short synchronous admission section for reader close, zero-reader decision, durable cancel request, old source-generation cancellation, and driver eligibility. A new open either attaches before the close and prevents cancellation, or waits until the abandoned generation is durably marked and its exact signal is cancelled. Terminal journal waiting, source awaits, retries, delivery, and promotion remain outside the lock; zero-byte admissions use the same boundary.
- PROOF: a deterministic four-worker regression pauses first immediately after the last close and again after old-generation source cancellation while a replacement open is queued. It proves no reader/transfer can cross either checkpoint, then verifies the abandoned transfer alone is cancelled with no ranges/reference/staging, the replacement generation alone materializes exact bytes, one old source future is dropped, and verified cache publication occurs once. The race passed 20 consecutive runs.
- VERIFICATION: all source/source-tdjson/engine/FFI tests pass; strict all-target/all-feature Clippy, workspace build, formatting/diff checks, 50 focused Swift hydration/provider tests, and repeated fast-materialization coverage pass. Public release state was not changed.

### 0244 — Hydration admission binds cancellation to the launched generation (TASK-260721-1yp75l rework cycle 2 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: `FetchCoordinator::open`, cancellation reset, and driver launch were separate operations. Two simultaneous first opens could let one driver capture signal S1, then let the other opener remove S1; last-reader cancellation would cancel replacement S2 while the live TDLib fetch still observed S1.
- FIX: a weakly retained per-`(item, content_version)` admission registry now serializes only the short synchronous `open → reset new generation → launch driver` step. Network awaits, retry sleeps, unrelated items, reader delivery, and promotion remain outside that lock; historical content keys do not accumulate in memory.
- PROOF: a deterministic four-worker regression holds the cancellation registry between first open and reset, proves the second opener cannot attach across that boundary, then releases both readers and verifies one routed-source drop, a terminal cancelled journal row, empty staged ranges/reference, no cache row, and no staging file. The identical race runs again with the prior cancelled signal still resident; the test passed 20 consecutive process runs. The TD downloader integration still proves abandoned source futures emit `cancelDownloadFile`.
- VERIFICATION: all source/source-tdjson/engine/FFI tests pass; strict all-target/all-feature Clippy, workspace build, formatting, diff validation, and the 29-test Swift hydration slice pass. Public release state was not changed.

### 0243 — Hydration concurrency and refresh evidence now fail closed (TASK-260721-1yp75l rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the first composition held one async mutex across `FetchCoordinator::open`, TDLib source awaits, promotion and retry sleeps. Lower-layer coalescing existed but exported concurrent opens could not reach it, and one retry-delayed item blocked unrelated work. Locator refresh also compared stable identity and exact extent only for `Some/Some`, accepting loss of previously pinned evidence.
- FIX: cloned fetch drivers now share an internally synchronized reader registry while each owns a short-lived state connection and staging/promotion host. No global mutex crosses source awaits or backoff; same-item readers attach atomically, independent transfers run concurrently, and cancelling one reader only raises durable/source cancellation when it was the last reader. Queue notifications are generation-preserving watch channels, so fast completion cannot be lost.
- FAIL-CLOSED: when stable unique id or exact extent was previously known, refreshed `getMessage` and state persistence require the same value; `None` is a version conflict just like a changed value. A fresh transfer resets only its own prior cancellation generation, while an already-running TDLib fetch retains the old cancellation signal and emits `cancelDownloadFile` on last-reader cancellation.
- PROOF: composed tests cover two live readers/one fetch/both results, one-reader cancellation with the other surviving, and unrelated hydration completing inside another transfer's two-second retry delay. Source-machine, TD downloader integration and state-catalog tests cover known-to-absent identity and extent. Affected Rust suites, strict Clippy, workspace build, package contract 0.13.0, focused 29-test Swift hydration and full 352-test serial Swift suite pass.

### 0242 — On-demand hydration is composed inside the owned TDLib session (TASK-260721-1yp75l → to-review)
- COMPOSITION: one data-root Hydrator now owns the state-backed FetchCatalog, cross-account source router, fetch/transfer coordinator, filesystem staging, verifier and atomic cache promoter. Each NamespaceSession registers exactly its own TdClient and unregisters it before client shutdown; the production Swift AgentLifecycle always hosts the FFI-backed HydrationServer.
- FAIL-CLOSED: durable item, attachment and chat policy gates reject protected, can-be-saved=false, view-once, unavailable/deleted and absent content before downloadFile. getMessage refresh may update only the remote locator; changing message/file identity, stable unique id, saveability or pinned extent is a version/restriction failure.
- CRASH/CANCEL: startup reconciliation returns interrupted work to the durable queue with ranges and staging handle intact. Client cancellation drops the TDLib fetch (which sends cancelDownloadFile), durably marks the journal cancelled, removes both recorded and pre-first-byte staging, and never creates a cache row.
- PROOF: composition tests cover exact-byte promotion, verified-cache reuse, pre-network state gates, locator identity, cancellation/no partial and relaunch resume. Existing engine suites cover aligned ranges, coalescing, retry/flood wait, resume, pin/version gates and atomic promotion; source tests cover TDLib range geometry, stale refresh and cancelDownloadFile. Native packaging generated contract 0.13.0 and the focused 29-test Swift hydration/lifecycle suite passes.

### 0241 — Attachment versions trust stable identity and fail closed without it (TASK-260721-ddqgxa rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the first projection hashed the complete normalized `AttachmentDescriptor`, so a TDLib local file-id, remote locator, thumbnail locator, or inline preview refresh advanced the main attachment `content_version` even when Telegram's stable content identity and bytes were unchanged.
- FIX: content-version schema v2 hashes Telegram's stable remote unique id plus stable byte/media facts and excludes every main/preview locator and preview payload in that normal branch. If the unique id is absent, canonical `(scope, chat, message, ordinal)` identity plus TDLib's current main local/remote locators forms a conservative generation signal; preview routes and payloads remain excluded.
- BLOB SAFETY: attachment upsert preserves a verified blob exactly when the derived content version is unchanged. Stable-identity locator refreshes and fallback preview-only refreshes preserve it; a stable identity change or a possible fallback main-content generation change advances the version and clears the old link.
- PROOF: owned-session fixtures retain item/attachment versions and verified bytes across stable-identity main/preview locator refresh and fallback preview-only refresh. They invalidate the version and blob for both a genuine stable-identity replacement and a same-key, equal-metadata possible replacement without `remote.unique_id`. Full workspace tests, strict Clippy, all-target build, formatting, architecture, traceability, diff, and board validation pass.

### 0240 — Attachment projection preserves provenance and identity-derived names (TASK-260721-ddqgxa → to-review)
- COMPOSITION: the owned TDLib history/live transaction now maps each accepted current `MessageRecord` into durable attachment facts before reconciling the provider tree. Schema v7 persists TDLib's numeric local file id separately from remote locator and stable unique id; current attachment rows reconcile edits while canonical attachment identity remains `(scope, chat, message, ordinal)`.
- FIDELITY: logical kind, Telegram representation, fidelity, sender source-name presence, MIME, exact size, timestamp, locators, saveability, and availability stay independent. Image/video MIME `messageDocument` records remain `original_document`; processed Telegram media discards transport filenames and uses generated names. TDLib `expected_size` is not treated as exact.
- FAIL-CLOSED: restricted/view-once/expired/locatorless media persists metadata-only or unavailable records without fetchable locators or previews, so Finder and monthly exports can describe absence without fabricating recovery.
- NAMING: direct-month attachment appearances use the persisted account timezone for `YYYY-MM-DD HH-mm-ss`, sanitize the original document leaf or generate a truthful Telegram-media leaf, and resolve equal names from canonical identity. Opposite-order ingestion fixtures produce the same message-to-name mapping.
- PROOF: source fixtures cover original-document versus Telegram-transcoded media and unavailable cases; state fixtures cover current projection reconciliation and locators; the owned-session integration fixture covers timezone projection, provider metadata, collision stability, and exact canonical identity.

### 0212 — File Provider date-first pages are bounded and restart-safe (TASK-260721-1v0o4e → to-review)
- PAGING: UniFFI contract 0.12.0 adds a provider-facing `children_page` result capped at 256 live records with one-row lookahead. The continuation anchor is accepted only when its durable item belongs to the same parent; a tombstoned anchor remains valid, while a missing/foreign anchor maps to File Provider's explicit `pageExpired` restart. The native page envelope also binds account, namespace epoch, journal instance, and parent, so it survives an ordinary extension relaunch but expires after identity/database replacement.
- DEADLINES: item and change enumeration now share exactly-once completion gates. A watchdog bounds a stalled local snapshot, enumerator invalidation cancels both paths, and late reads cannot emit a second callback. Authorization, source-offline, storage, missing-container, page-expired, and sync-anchor-expired outcomes map to distinct recoverable File Provider errors.
- SIGNALING: relay startup refreshes the root once to cover authorization installed while no observer was alive. Ordinary history/render commits signal only the working-set journal, whose coalesced rows enumerate only affected generated appearances; deep publications no longer trigger an unrelated root re-enumeration.
- PROOF: real Rust state tests pin date-first first-page metadata, truthful generated versions/sizes/dates, container-bound pagination, tombstoned anchors, and stable identities across reopen. Swift tests pin hard caps, cursor relaunch/expiry, auth/storage/offline errors, listing/change timeout and cancellation races, and affected-generated-item-only delivery. Repository acceptance passes 8/8; the 352-test Apple suite, 148-test File Provider suite, Rust FFI tests/build, package 0.12.0, and Swift/Kotlin binding smoke pass.

### 0117 — render policy changes use a monotonic generation (TASK-260721-1dzolg rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the chat event watermark does not advance when retention mode or the persisted display timezone changes, even though either policy can change monthly bytes, membership, and paths. Keying immutable staging and content versions only by event watermark therefore collided on Mirror → Audit → Mirror round trips, and a generic account upsert could mutate timezone without repartitioning the tree.
- FIX: schema v6 adds a per-account monotonic render generation. A single read snapshot now pins generation, retention mode, display timezone, normalized messages, and event watermark; both render formats and immutable stage paths include that policy provenance. Publication rereads the generation and policy in its write transaction and rejects a stale render. Existing-account upserts cannot change timezone; the dedicated timezone transition increments generation and is committed with tree reconciliation and generated-document invalidation.
- IDENTITY: provider catalog, render-state, and item rows are appearance-owned; canonical document ids remain logical shared job keys only. Renderer documentation now matches the production ownership model.
- PROOF: owned-session regressions exercise Mirror → Audit → Mirror at one message watermark, including destructive superseded-payload purge, and UTC → Asia/Tbilisi movement of a boundary message from June to July without changing its absolute source timestamp. Repository acceptance passes 8/8, including all-feature tests, strict Clippy, formatting, architecture, dependency policy, traceability, and script tests.

### 0116 — monthly rendering joined the owned namespace path (TASK-260721-1dzolg rework → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the first implementation proved projection, planning, composition, and publication only as isolated helpers. `NamespaceSession` committed normalized history/live changes without invoking them, while planner render rows used canonical generated-document ids that are not provider tree positions. A caller-supplied publication catalog could also omit an otherwise complete live view.
- FIX: history/live commits now reconcile direct `YYYY-MM` appearance subtrees and mark every affected appearance dirty in the same SQLite transaction as normalized state and sync progress. The owned session drains that durable worklist, decodes normalized `MessageRecord` payloads, composes and stages one monthly pair, then publishes the complete catalog loaded inside the publication transaction. Canonical ids group logical jobs only; all durable render state belongs to appearance rows.
- REPLAY: projection reconciliation preserves published file facts and renderer metadata versions. Identical history replay is byte-, version-, cache-path-, and provider-journal-quiet. New or resurrected view appearances start dirty so membership changes cannot expose stale generated files.
- PROOF: the production-path fixture uses Main and Archive appearances and exercises July insert, identical replay, August insert, July edit, and July deletion. It asserts direct `chat.json` / `Active Stories` / `YYYY-MM` shape, four-row atomic pair publication, equal appearance watermarks, and unchanged August bytes/version/path/journal state during July changes.
- VERIFICATION: all model/state/render/engine/FFI tests pass; strict all-target Clippy is clean; workspace all-target check, crate architecture, dependency policy, 202-requirement traceability, formatting/diff, and board validation pass. `cargo deny` retains only the known duplicate-`hashbrown` warning.

### 0215 — monthly timeline pairs publish from one watermark (TASK-260721-1dzolg → to-review)
- PIPELINE: one read transaction pins the normalized month rows and chat event watermark; Markdown and NDJSON compose from that shared snapshot, stage beneath one schema/renderer/watermark-qualified immutable directory, and every live appearance, render row, cache locator, and provider journal signal advances in one SQLite transaction. Publication rejects incomplete format pairs, wrong identities, mismatched provenance, or staged-byte drift.
- INVALIDATION: event watermark intervals resolve to distinct source send instants, then the persisted IANA account timezone maps only those instants to direct `YYYY-MM` Markdown/NDJSON pairs. The publication race query is bounded to the same UTC month range, so an edit/delete/insert in another month neither dirties nor rebuilds the published pair.
- DETERMINISM: the bundled timezone database removes host-tzdb variation while absolute source epoch milliseconds remain unchanged in NDJSON. Audit replay retains superseded revisions plus explicit Telegram representation/fidelity facts; identical state at the same watermark produces byte-identical files and the same staged paths.
- PERFORMANCE/SECURITY: the monthly appearance catalog starts from the exact canonical month identity and uses appearance/child indexes rather than scanning all generated items. `cargo deny` required explicit all-target allowances for Jiff's non-pointer-atomic portability probes; those build scripts are not compiled for the supported macOS target and do not generate timezone data or access the network.
- VERIFICATION: full model/state/render/engine suites, focused replay/invalidation/publication tests, large-account query-plan gate, strict Clippy, workspace all-target check, formatting, dependency policy, and 202-requirement traceability validation pass.

### 0007 — edit intake uses a durable crawl-generation fence (TASK-260721-yrcjlo rework → to-review)
- CRASH SAFETY: a tracked durable-chat `updateMessageContent` / `updateMessageEdited` now atomically removes the complete cursor window and records retryable privacy-safe `live-edit-pending` progress before the targeted refresh exists only in `LiveMachine` memory. A crash in any TDLib response-wait path therefore relaunches a full `getChatHistory` crawl from zero instead of trusting stale content.
- GENERATION FENCE: an unrelated new-message/delete commit or a page response from a crawl planned before the edit may persist normalized idempotent rows, but cannot restore the invalidated window or clear the recovery reason. The next scheduler claim is built from the absent window and then clears the fence to `syncing`; this prevents the subtle response-arrives-before-`drive_live_steps` crash gap.
- FIXTURE: a complete durable chat receives an edit while an unrelated TDLib request remains pending, then accepts an unrelated live message and a stale in-flight history commit before crashing without calling any recovery helper or consuming a live step. Relaunch proves a zero-anchored crawl, edited-message convergence, and replay without duplicate effects.
- VERIFICATION: repository acceptance 8/8, focused/full FFI+source+state tests, strict Clippy, workspace all-target build, Apple build/tests 2/2, Swift/Kotlin binding smoke, formatting, and diff checks pass.

## 2026-07-21

### 2348 — tracked live staging and saturated priority hints are hard-bounded (TASK-260721-yrcjlo rework → to-review)
- BOUNDS: the owned session now drains normalized observations/deletions to atomic commits on every TDLib update-consumption path, including authorization, snapshot, gap resolution, and delayed content responses. `LiveMachine` independently caps tracked ready changes at 256/chat and 4,096/account and distinct edit refreshes at the same bounds; counters make the check independent of account cardinality.
- RECOVERY: overflowing targeted edit identities reports one typed `RecoveryRequired`, clears the bounded targeted queue, and atomically replaces the chat cursor with an incomplete/no-window state plus privacy-safe retryable `live-refresh-overflow` progress. A crash/relaunch therefore anchors a full `getChatHistory` crawl from zero; already observed changes and deletions stay durable and idempotent.
- PRIORITY: a saturated provider hint queue admits a new visible chat by evicting the oldest lowest-priority pending hint, and dequeues foreground-first while pressure mode lasts. Below saturation it remains FIFO so requested → visible → background lifecycle edges are not collapsed or reordered. The full Apple gate caught the first over-coalescing attempt; the corrected queue preserves both behaviors.
- VERIFICATION: delayed-response fixture (599 live observations, deletion, edit overflow, crash/relaunch), per-chat/account-wide source-machine bounds, deterministic Swift saturation tests, workspace gate 8/8, Apple build/tests 2/2 (344 tests), all-target workspace build, Swift/Kotlin binding smoke, diff check, and board validation pass.

### 2325 — review gaps closed at the provider/readiness boundary (TASK-260721-yrcjlo rework → to-review)
- IPC: provider metadata contract v2 carries only the owning Telegram chat id; opening a chat enqueues `requested`, enumeration enqueues `visible`, and enumerator invalidation enqueues `background` through a bounded fire-and-forget client on the existing agent control socket. The server routes the hint to `AgentLifecycle` and its one owned `NamespaceSession`; File Provider performs no TDLib call and never waits for the socket/history operation. UniFFI contract is now 0.11.0.
- ORDERING: the control server intentionally handles separate connections concurrently, so fire-and-forget connections alone can reorder a rapid visible/background pair. The private priority-client worker now waits for each one-line command acknowledgement before opening the next connection; only that utility worker waits, while File Provider callbacks still return after bounded enqueue.
- BOUNDS: `LiveMachine` caps unresolved readiness traffic at 256 chats, 256 ordered operations per chat, and 4,096 operations account-wide. Per-chat overflow reports `recovery_required` and immediately bridges from the durable newest cursor after resolution; identity-set overflow reports account-wide resync so the complete snapshot plus resumable crawl recovers present state.
- RELAUNCH: the namespace opens its durable scope and seeds live tracking for every eligible stored chat before authorization readiness. Known-chat observations/deletions therefore commit during readiness instead of entering the unresolved queue; crash/relaunch tests pin that deletion behavior. Unknown-chat edit/delete limitations remain exactly the approved contract: data deleted before first observation and unseen revisions are unrecoverable.
- VERIFICATION: bounded overflow/bridge, sustained multi-chat readiness, provider requested/visible/background routing, real control-socket delivery, and lifecycle ownership tests pass alongside the full 341-test Apple suite and focused Rust live/namespace/state suites.

### 2310 — history and live updates composed into the single owned session (TASK-260721-yrcjlo → to-review)
- OWNERSHIP: `NamespaceSession` remains the sole TDLib owner for an account and now composes the existing `CrawlMachine` and `LiveMachine` only after root/chat metadata readiness. Provider calls enqueue visible/requested priority without performing TDLib I/O; work stays newest-first, one page at a time, and page boundaries are the preemption/cancellation boundary. No content path issues `downloadFile`.
- ATOMICITY: normalized `MessageRecord` vocabulary is versioned JSON. Each history page and ordered live batch writes message events/current state, merged oldest/newest window facts, completion, and privacy-safe progress in one transaction. Schema v5 seeds an incomplete window and pending/protected progress for every canonical chat, including future inserts, so relaunch and large-account backlog selection need no unbounded outer join.
- RECOVERY: live updates are buffered from authorization onward, duplicate/overlap effects remain idempotent, live gaps bridge before cursor movement, and bounded flood-wait/error/cancellation outcomes become retryable stable categories rather than raw Telegram detail or indefinite activity. Protected/deleted untracked chats are discarded before content persistence.
- NATIVE BOUNDARY: the agent lifecycle routes foreground priority only to its owned account session. Existing auth, folder/catalog, control, menu, File Provider enumeration, and quit ownership remain on their prior seams.
- BINDINGS: UniFFI objects already generate Kotlin `AutoCloseable.close()`, so exporting an application method named `close` created conflicting Kotlin overloads. Auth and namespace teardown now export as `shutdown`; the Swift agent's lifecycle-facing `close` seam is unchanged and delegates to it. Both Swift and Kotlin generated-consumer smoke tests cover contract 0.10.0.

### 2200 — live legacy layouts and false processed-media claims closed (TASK-260721-1hm7dx rework)
- FIX: schema v4 and the typed item repository now admit `year_dir` / `media_dir` only as deleted migration tombstones. The v3 atomic copy stamps those legacy rows deleted before the new table constraint applies, preserving stable identity while making a mixed live layout unrepresentable.
- FIX: one model-layer attachment validator now governs state writes/reads and both render inputs. Known Telegram-processed representations carry no sender source name and only `telegram_variant` or `metadata_only` fidelity; original documents and `unknown_legacy` rows have their own truthful pairings. SQLite independently enforces the same decision table, and defensive FFI reads reject corrupted rows instead of exposing them.
- ANOMALY: large query-plan and transfer-concurrency fixtures still built live Year/Media directories even though product fixtures were date-first. Both now use direct month directories; this is why repository guards must be exercised by the full workspace, not only contract-focused tests.
- VERIFICATION: full workspace tests, strict workspace clippy, all-target workspace build, formatting/diff checks, 10,000 naming fixed-point property cases, 202-requirement traceability validation, and regenerated Swift UniFFI compile/runtime smoke pass.

### 2138 — date-first contract review regressions closed (TASK-260721-1hm7dx → to-review)
- FIX: schema v4 again enforces `canonical_item_id` and `view_kind` as an all-or-none appearance pair. `items_appearance` is partial only on `canonical_item_id IS NOT NULL`, so the required canonical-to-appearances lookup uses the index instead of scanning `items`.
- FIX: atomic rebuilds now run `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` inside the migration transaction before schema history and `user_version` are stamped. A deterministic orphan-chat migration proves the violating writes, history row, and version all roll back while foreign-key enforcement is restored.
- FIX: `sanitize` recognizes an already safe, edge-canonical component as its fixed point. This closes the known file-name drift where truncation exposed a short dot-tail that a second pass reinterpreted as an extension; the regression is pinned with a compact deterministic case and 10,000 property cases.
- VERIFICATION: full Rust workspace tests, strict workspace clippy, all-target workspace build, formatting, diff checks, 202-requirement traceability validation, and regenerated Swift UniFFI compile/runtime smoke all pass.

### 2117 — versioned date-first live-content contract implemented (TASK-260721-1hm7dx → to-review)
- CONTRACT: identity format v1 grows additively with direct month (`0x0c`), Active Stories (`0x0d`), canonical story (`0x0e`), and story-appearance (`0x11`) tags; existing identity encodings remain byte-for-byte pinned. The live projection is only `chat.json` + `Active Stories` + direct `YYYY-MM/{Messages.md,Messages.ndjson,attachments,profile stories}`. Legacy year/media identities remain parseable solely so schema-v3 rows can become tombstones without losing identity.
- MIGRATION: schema v4 atomically persists account display timezone, orthogonal attachment kind/Telegram-representation/fidelity/source-name/MIME/exact-size/availability facts, canonical stories, and byte-free appearances. Existing authorized v3 rows keep account/chat/message/item identities; legacy year/media and whole-chat NDJSON rows are retired, direct months and both bounded documents are created, attachments are reparented and date-prefixed, and an in-transaction foreign-key check gates version/history stamping. Legacy accounts default explicitly to persisted `UTC`; no historical timezone is guessed.
- STORIES/FIDELITY: canonical story identity is `(poster_chat_id, story_id)` and owns the sole optional blob link. Active→month deletes the active appearance in the same transaction; a persistent appearance blocks active resurrection. Protected or non-forwardable stories cannot link bytes.
