Original document representations may retain source name/MIME/exact bytes; Telegram media variants carry generated names and explicit non-original fidelity.
- VERSIONING: NDJSON and Markdown renderer schemas/implementations are v2; Markdown attachment links now resolve directly to month siblings. UniFFI contract is 0.9.0 with date-first/story kinds, account timezone, record vocabulary version, and attachment fidelity metadata.
- VERIFICATION: model identity/tree/naming goldens and properties, real schema-v3 migration fixture, state repository tests, render v2 goldens, FFI tests, generated Swift compile/runtime smoke, all workspace tests, strict workspace clippy, workspace all-target build, formatting, diff checks, and 202-requirement traceability validation pass.

### 2004 — live date-first content decomposed around the real integration seam (EPIC-260721-1gyt2s → to-review)
- FINDING: the completed core already contains the append-only message event log, current-message projection, per-account Mirror/Audit and Archive flags, render watermarks, attachment rows, resumable history/live machines, transfer/cache engine, TDLib downloader, agent hydration IPC, and bounded File Provider enumeration. The missing work is composition, not new parallel implementations of those primitives.
- ANOMALY: the persisted/provider item vocabulary still encodes the rejected legacy `year_dir` / `media_dir` shape and lacks the approved account display timezone, Telegram representation/fidelity fields, and canonical story/appearance identities. A forward versioned contract migration is therefore the first critical-path task; wiring history or stories directly onto schema v3 would force mixed layouts and later identity churn.
- PLAN: 14 developer-ready tasks across four implementation stories, with 26 dependency edges. The critical story path is timeline/history → attachment hydration → stories/retention → automated and signed installed acceptance. TDLib story-rights research is intentionally unblocked in parallel with the contract migration and forbids any live-account calls.
- CONTRACT: every executable task carries the exact owner-approved date-first content contract plus the runtime component diagram; story/retention tasks also carry the explicit active/profile/retention state diagram. Architecture preserves one agent-owned TDLib session, read-only deadline-bounded provider callbacks, metadata-first enumeration, on-demand bytes, and restriction checks before network content access.
- ARTIFACTS: `EPIC-260721-1gyt2s_runtime-components.puml`, `EPIC-260721-1gyt2s_story-retention-state.puml`, the canonical board-generated execution plan, and the rendered phase graph are attached as epic outcome resources.

### 1701 — live Telegram namespace composed and Finder populated (BUG-260721-3tilmj → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the owned macOS agent installed only source-free roots and never composed the existing TDLib snapshot/folder machines, normalized repositories, or virtual tree. A second latent issue made a real `loadChats` request deadlock when update bursts filled the bounded channel before its response was consumed; the provider change relay was also never retained by the companion and signaled only the working set, so Finder could retain stale root labels.
- FIX: the lifecycle now owns one metadata-only namespace session per authorized account, persists canonical chats, ordered list/folder memberships and restart checkpoints in schema v3, and projects stable virtual appearances under exactly `Chats`, `Archive`, and `Folders`. Snapshot response waiting drains updates concurrently to preserve bounded backpressure. The companion retains per-domain change relays and signals both the working set and root container. Initial sync never requests message history or media.
- PRIVACY BOUNDARY: installed verification records only product-owned fixed labels, counts, booleans, timings, contract/schema versions, and signing status. It records no chat or folder names, account identity, messages, credentials, tokens, history, or media.
- VERIFICATION: Rust formatting, clippy with warnings denied, and all model/state/TDLib/FFI tests pass; all 336 Swift tests in 61 suites pass. A fresh Developer ID signed app package passed deep/strict signature validation and was installed over the already authorized profile without resetting account or domain state. The namespace became ready in 2 seconds with one account, one domain, 2,168 canonical chats, 2,175 stable appearances, zero duplicate canonical records, three visible fixed Finder roots, and 961 live `Chats` appearances. Quit terminated both processes and relaunch restored readiness in 1 second. The local package remains intentionally unnotarized, and public release state was not changed.

### 1506 — LSUIElement cold/reopen window routing repaired (BUG-260721-1f48xp → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the persistent menu-bar label requested a scene only for incomplete onboarding, so an authenticated cold launch created no window. The app had no `applicationShouldHandleReopen` bridge, making LaunchServices reopen a no-op after the last window closed.
- FIX: an AppKit delegate publishes durable presentation generations consumed by the always-instantiated label; one action router selects the singleton main/onboarding `Window` id and is shared by launch, reopen, menu-bar Open, and menu-bar Set Up. Observation of one generation is coalesced, while each explicit reopen raises the same singleton scene.
- PLATFORM DECISION: an `LSUIElement` process remained behind the current app under supported activation calls. The first explicit window request therefore promotes the process to regular activation policy and keeps it foreground-capable until Quit; demoting on close made later supported self-activation unreliable. Closing still leaves the menu-bar app and its single child agent running, and Quit terminates both.
- REVIEW REWORK: the first activation controller continued ordering and activating its window every 50 ms for the full retry budget, so it could steal focus back after the user selected Finder. Retry work now stops as soon as a normal key-capable window is active, and each new explicit request invalidates callbacks from the prior generation. A deterministic regression covers post-success focus preservation and another covers overlapping requests.
- VERIFICATION: 333 Swift tests in 61 suites, 57 packaging tests, release companion build, Developer ID app/DMG packaging, deep/strict codesign, diff whitespace, and board validation pass. Installed LaunchServices checks proved cold launch, zero-window reopen, singleton reuse, both menu actions, and Quit. Finder remained frontmost both 150 ms and 1.1 seconds after an explicit post-presentation switch. Count-only probes preserved one authorized account, one domain, and the fixed three-item Finder first page; no account or Telegram content was recorded and public release state was unchanged.

### 1346 — File Provider entry recursion and empty authorized root repaired (BUG-260721-qkv1ga → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the hand-assembled extension was a normal Swift executable whose `main.swift` called `NSExtensionMain`. The installed process sample showed `NSExtensionMain → GramDriveFileProviderExtensionApp_main → NSExtensionMain` recursion before `makeEnumerator`, so Finder could never receive a completion. Independently, authorization persisted only the account root; the live authorized database had zero root children and the agent never repaired that legacy shape.
- FIX: the extension Mach-O entry is now linked directly to `_NSExtensionMain`, matching Xcode's App Extension product type. Fresh authorization and coordinator startup transactionally upsert the three source-free virtual roots (`Main`, `Archive`, `Telegram Folders`); identical relaunch upserts are journal-quiet. Enumeration remains keyset-paged at at most 256 items and now has an 8-second exactly-once timeout/cancellation gate with retryable provider errors.
- HEALTH/UI: agent health v2 projects authorization plus `waitingForAuthorization/preparing/ready/failed` Finder-content readiness and a privacy-safe retry action. Companion account/sync/domain status consumes those real readings instead of staying at “not reported” or “connecting”.
- VERIFICATION: Rust workspace tests, clippy with warnings denied, 326 Swift tests, and 57 app-packaging tests pass. A Developer ID signed build was installed without changing App Group/account/domain state; black-box launch and relaunch each showed one authorized account, one domain, one app with one owned agent, three non-duplicated first-page folders, and a 4 ms enumeration. The attached Finder crop contains only the fixed product-owned names; no account identity, chat title, message, credential, or token was captured. Public release state was not changed.

## 2026-07-20

### 2135 — authorized account now creates a usable File Provider domain (BUG-260720-gnfmji → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: the containing app ran its only `DomainStartupReconcile` from `App.init`, before the companion's agent-readiness barrier, and never ran it again after authorization persisted the account row. Registration failures were log-only. The onboarding success step independently polled unwired initial-sync health forever and treated the CloudStorage container as an acceptable Finder destination, so it could show an indefinite spinner with no GramDrive location and no retry.
- FIX: launch now projects only the safe `authorized` marker from agent health and runs the existing add-only, stable-identifier reconciler after readiness. Onboarding runs the same actor-coalesced operation after authorization, publishes `idle/settingUp/ready/failed`, gates Done on `ready`, and exposes Retry on failure. `SystemDomainRegistrar` resolves the domain through `NSFileProviderManager`; because macOS can transiently return the CloudStorage container for the root item, the app boundedly reconciles that result with the surfaced `GramDrive*` child and never reports the parent as success. Open in Finder uses that actual child URL.
- REGRESSION PROOF: tests cover first registration, failure/retry, a settled repeated pass, overlapping-call coalescing, authorized launch, process relaunch, completion gating, and exact-root reveal. Focused 48/12 and full 319/60 Swift tests pass; repository and Apple gates pass.
- INSTALLED PROOF: Developer ID packaging and deep/strict app + DMG verification pass (notarization intentionally not run). A signed installed copy preserved the existing App Group/account data, produced exactly one GramDrive CloudStorage root, retained one app plus one owned agent across repeated activation and relaunch, and the real menu-bar Open in Finder action targeted that root. Evidence records only booleans/counts/hashes/signature identifiers—no account identifiers, names, credentials, tokens, messages, or chat content.

### 1945 — companion-owned direct-session agent cold start (BUG-260720-2rvt9r → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE: `AgentEnsurer` was reachable only from control operations. Launching the `LSUIElement` companion instantiated the menu-bar shell and Welcome window without any status/control operation, so with Launch at Login off and the transient acceptance supervisor removed the app stayed at one GUI process, zero agents, and absent health/control readiness.
- FIX: the always-instantiated menu-bar launch label now awaits live status readiness before presenting Welcome. Live health and control paths share an actor-coalesced `AgentEnsurer`, so concurrent launch/menu activation joins one start attempt. `BundledAgentStarter` retains one direct child, coalesces repeated starts, explicitly runs the same packaged `Contents/MacOS/gramdrive-agent`, and terminates only its owned child on normal application shutdown; the `SMAppService` Launch at Login path and preference are unchanged.
- REGRESSION PROOF: Swift tests cover concurrent cold status reads producing one start, repeated direct starts retaining one PID, normal stop, and a fresh relaunch PID. The full Apple suite passes 314 tests in 58 suites; `make check` passes 8/8 and `make check-apple` passes 2/2.
- INSTALLED PROOF: a Developer ID signed 0.1.1 build replaced `/Applications/GramDrive.app`; the immediately previous signed app is recoverable at `.temp/BUG-260720-2rvt9r/installed-backup/GramDrive.app`. With settings absent (Launch at Login default off), no app/agent/transient service, cold launch produced exactly one app plus one bundle-local child agent, both sockets and control status were ready, Welcome was present, five repeated activations preserved both PIDs, quit removed both processes, and relaunch produced a fresh healthy pair. Evidence contains only operational fields and account counts; no credentials or Telegram account data were read or changed.

### 1902 — developer rework: auth replacement transitions are coalesced (BUG-260720-30mqp6 → to-review)
- REVIEW FINDING CONFIRMED: `AuthorizationViewModel.begin()` awaited the prior session's delayed close before setting `isSubmitting`, and `reset()` cleared that flag during the transition. A second QR-to-phone or Restart activation could therefore enter the same close barrier and later construct/start a second replacement session.
- FIX: begin, submit, and cancel now share the model-owned busy gate. Begin/cancel publish busy before their first suspension and use `defer` to release it; reset no longer creates a false-idle window. Duplicate activation is coalesced inside the view model, independent of SwiftUI button disabling.
- REGRESSION PROOF: the delayed-close model test invokes `begin()` again after cancel acknowledgement but before release, verifies busy remains published, and proves exactly one replacement is constructed and started while a third fixture is untouched.
- VERIFICATION: Authorization suites 11/11; `make check` 8/8; `make check-apple` 2/2; Developer ID app/DMG packaging passed deep/strict signing and entitlement checks (`spctl` rejection expected for the intentionally unnotarized local build); installed-copy black-box auth smoke passed against synthetic sockets with delayed release and length-only password observations. The original `/Applications/GramDrive.app`, onboarding preference, and processes were restored.

### 1842 — developer rework: QR-to-phone replacement waits for FFI slot release (BUG-260720-30mqp6 → to-review)
- REVIEW FINDING CONFIRMED: accepting `cancel` and even observing TDLib `Closed` were not proof that another sign-in could start. Rust emitted `Closed` from the auth pump immediately before the pump returned, while the pump-owned single-sign-in `ScopeGuard` dropped only on return; the companion could construct the phone session inside that gap and receive `InvalidArgument`/a dropped control channel.
- FIX: `AuthSession.wait_closed()` now uses a retained Tokio watch signal published only after the pump returns and its guard drops. `CoreAuthSession` keeps the hosted state stream/control connection alive until that barrier. `LiveAuthorizationSession.cancel()` waits for terminal channel completion, and `AuthorizationViewModel` retains its sole state consumer until cancellation and stream completion before creating the replacement session.
- REGRESSION PROOF: Rust closes a session, awaits `wait_closed`, then reacquires the same data-root sign-in scope immediately with no poll/retry. The Swift model double acknowledges cancel but withholds terminal release and proves the second session factory is not called early. The installed signed-app smoke now emits `closing`/`closed`, holds a one-second post-state release delay, and proves phone fallback does not connect during it.
- VERIFICATION: targeted Authorization suites 11/11; `make check` 8/8; `make check-apple` 2/2; TDLib-linked `make package`; Developer ID `make package-app` passed deep/strict signing and entitlement checks (`spctl` rejection expected for the intentionally unnotarized local build); delayed-release installed smoke passed with synthetic inputs only. Original `/Applications/GramDrive.app`, onboarding preference, and processes were restored; the temporary installed smoke bundle was moved back into task evidence.

### 1805 — developer: native QR + secure 2FA input recovery (BUG-260720-30mqp6 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE (secret-free reproduction): `AuthorizationView.waitQrConfirmation` rendered TDLib's rotating `tg://` link as selectable text instead of pixels. The password branch had a native `SecureField`, but no focus state or Return handler, while onboarding installed a disabled footer button as the window's default Return action; the dynamically inserted password field therefore had neither reliable first-responder placement nor a submit path from Return.
- FIX: Core Image now generates a sharp QR with a four-module quiet zone and no raw-link text; the image has a VoiceOver label/hint. Phone/code/password inputs have field-specific `@FocusState`, dynamic-state focus after the native control joins the responder chain, `defaultFocus`, accessible labels, and `onSubmit`. Invalid-password recovery restores focus for correction/retry; password state is cleared when leaving the step. Onboarding no longer claims the default Return shortcut while sign-in awaits input.
- DECISION: official TDLib docs say the QR link updates frequently, but `setAuthenticationPhoneNumber`/`requestQrCodeAuthentication` are not valid from `authorizationStateWaitOtherDeviceConfirmation`. The QR screen therefore states automatic refresh and implements "Use phone number instead" by closing the current control session before opening a fresh phone-capable session—no invalid wire input or special-case core state rule.
- REGRESSION PROOF: Vision decodes the generated synthetic QR back to its exact payload. An AppKit-hosted SwiftUI test verifies the real secure field receives first focus, full-string insertion, deletion correction, Return submission, invalid-password display, focused retry, and a second submission while recording only input lengths. A model test proves QR fallback sends `cancel` before the fresh session reaches phone entry.
- RELEASE PROOF: `make check` 8/8; `make check-apple` 2/2; signed arm64 `make package-app` passed deep/strict codesign and entitlement verification (`spctl` rejection expected for the intentionally unnotarized local build). Installed `/Applications/GramDrive-AuthSmoke.app` black-box smoke used synthetic local health/control sockets—no Telegram/Keychain/human secrets—and verified accessible QR + phone fallback, focused/editable password, Return rejection, focused retry, button acceptance, and signed-in state. Original app/onboarding preference restored and original agent relaunched afterward.

### 1725 — developer: v0.1.1 installed-app auth and menu recovery (BUG-260720-i8vz85 → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE (reproduced from the signed/notarized v0.1.1 in `/Applications`): direct spawn found and launched the correctly signed embedded `Contents/MacOS/gramdrive-agent`, but `AgentLifecycle.start()` published `health.sock` before it created `control.sock`. The companion treated the successful health read as readiness and raced the first auth connection into a missing control listener; the agent remained healthy while Welcome reported a daemon connection failure.
- FIX: control now binds before health is published, making health a truthful whole-agent readiness barrier. The companion also boundedly retries only pre-request `agentUnavailable` connection failures; protocol errors and possibly accepted requests are never replayed. Bundled-agent path and launch-plist `BundleProgram` are regression-checked against the same `Contents/MacOS/gramdrive-agent` packaging contract.
- MENU: menu commands route through one testable seam. Open GramDrive and Set Up GramDrive create the intended SwiftUI scene and explicitly activate/order its window for the `LSUIElement` shell; Open in Finder retains the real drive revealer. Installed black-box checks visibly confirmed Account, Welcome, and Finder/CloudStorage destinations.
- RELEASE PROOF: clean-state installed build reached the real Telegram phone-number UI on first launch and again after terminating/relaunching the agent, without removing App Group data. `make check` 8/8, `make check-apple` 2/2 (309 Swift tests), signed arm64 app/DMG assembly, deep strict codesign, shared App Group entitlements, embedded launch metadata, mounted-DMG verification, and installed UI smoke passed. The local dirty artifact was not submitted to Apple notarization (`spctl` rejection is expected); the live Telegram boundary stops before entering a real phone/code.

### 1650 — developer: first-launch onboarding flow (TASK-260720-31nw0w → to-review)
- MILESTONE: Welcome/onboarding wizard for the companion shell (SwiftUI, macOS 14+) — v0.1.0 acceptance feedback ("no window, no guidance after launch"). Steps Welcome → Sign In → Choose Defaults → You're All Set.
- SCOPE: new `apple/GramDriveSupport/Sources/GramDriveCompanion/Onboarding/{OnboardingCompletionStore,DriveLocation,OnboardingViewModel}.swift`, `Views/{OnboardingView,MenuBarStatusView}.swift`; edited `CompanionViewModel.swift` (owns `onboarding`, defaulted `driveLocation`/`onboardingStore` params) and `GramDriveCompanionMain/CompanionMain.swift` (compact menu-bar content, onboarding `Window`, first-launch present, Help re-run). Tests `OnboardingViewModelTests.swift` + `OnboardingSeamTests.swift`.
- DECISION: onboarding drives the SAME live auth/settings/status sub-view-models the shell uses — no parallel sign-in/settings paths, so state is consistent when onboarding ends. Sign-in step embeds the existing `AuthorizationView` and gates advance on `authorization.isAuthorized`.
- DECISION: drive "Open in Finder" location resolved by scanning `~/Library/CloudStorage` for a `GramDrive*` folder (fallback: the container) rather than depending on `NSFileProviderManager` — keeps `GramDriveCompanion` free of FileProvider specifics and unit-testable (`CloudStorageDriveLocation` + injected revealer).

- DECISION: completion is a persisted `UserDefaults` flag recorded on finish/skip only; a bare window red-close does NOT record it, so an accidental close re-nudges next launch instead of stranding a user who set nothing up. Re-runnable from Help ▸ GramDrive Setup Guide + the compact panel.
- NOTE: first-launch auto-open rides the always-present `MenuBarExtra` label's `.task` (the one reliably-instantiated view at launch in an `LSUIElement` shell); Help + compact panel are guaranteed manual fallbacks. Executable window-glue is not unit-testable here; the presentation *decision* (`OnboardingViewModel.isPresented`/restart/finish/skip) is.

### 1600 — reviewer: control-channel rework accepted (BUG-260720-3i74u1 → done)
- VERDICT: ACCEPTED. All 3 confirmed majors + the should-fix + the minors from the 0338 verdict are correctly resolved; no redesign of the accepted control channel. Independently re-verified, not taken on faith.
- MAJOR 1 (Drop→close): `impl Drop for AuthSession` calls the idempotent `close()`; the pump owns the slot `ScopeGuard` and unwinds when the client close ends the update stream. `close()` is non-blocking, never runs on the pump thread (the pump holds `Arc<SessionShared>`, not the handle) — no deadlock. Test `dropping_a_session_without_close_frees_the_sign_in_slot` passes.
- MAJOR 2 (finalize scope): `persist_account` takes `ScopeGuard::acquire(data_dir, real_account)` as its FIRST action, before store-key/wipe/rename/row-write; contention returns `finalize-account-busy` with zero mutation. Scopes are a non-blocking global `HashSet` (acquire fails fast, never blocks) so holding slot+account keys together cannot deadlock. Happy path still reaches `Complete` (no spurious busy) — verified. Test `finalization_fails_safe_when_the_account_scope_is_held` passes.
- MAJOR 3 (security): confirmed the swift tool has NO read path left — only `SecItemDelete`+`SecItemAdd` (write) and a side-effect-free `--check`; `readItem`/`--exec` gone; `trustedPaths` starts `["/usr/bin/security"]` and appends only `--agent`/`--app` product binaries, never `arguments[0]`. The team-signed exfiltration primitive is eliminated, not merely narrowed.
- SHOULD-FIX (otool): `assert_no_absolute_runtime_refs` reads shipped Mach-Os back and fails on any staged/Homebrew absolute load. Directly exercised by `RuntimeEmbeddingTest` clean + dirty (surviving staged ref) + brew + signing-order cases — the exact silent `-change` no-op the 0338 verdict called out.
- MINORS: Debug redaction, `Starting` on pump thread, canonicalized scope key, bounded terminate waits, smoke `failed`-event + `mask_phone`/`mask_account`, provision `--check`-before-delete — all present and correct. OnceLock deferral (one-shot `claim()` + `start()` consuming halves) is a sound documented judgment, not a gap.
- VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY: `make check` 8/8 exit 0 (re-run); `make check-apple` 2/2 exit 0 (re-run); `cargo test -p gramdrive-ffi` 47 passed / 0 failed incl. all four new tests. Live Telegram E2E correctly NOT attempted — owner-owned final hop per the 2026-07-20 decision and tdlib/td#3361 (0400); AC sentence 1 (every agent-ownable hop) is fully proven, sentence 2 (real code accepted, persistence) is the owner's release-build step. Checklist item 3's persistence sub-clause stays owner-owned; "Implementation matches AC" now satisfied for the agent's scope.

### 1530 — developer: control-channel rework — 3 majors + should-fix + minors (BUG-260720-3i74u1 → to-review)
- FIX (major): `AuthSession` now `impl Drop → self.close()` (`crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/auth.rs`) — a dropped handle no longer strands the pump thread or holds the slot `ScopeGuard`; idempotent close ends the update stream and unwinds the pump. Test `dropping_a_session_without_close_frees_the_sign_in_slot`.
- FIX (major): `persist_account` now acquires the REAL account's `ScopeGuard` before any mutation (auth.rs) — a concurrent probe/remove of the same id fails the finalize fail-safe (`finalize-account-busy`) instead of racing the wipe/rename. Test `finalization_fails_safe_when_the_account_scope_is_held`.
- FIX (major, security): removed the `--exec` read+exec mode and `readItem` from `provision-telegram-credentials.swift`, and dropped the tool's self-trust from the item ACL (`trustedPaths` no longer includes `CommandLine.arguments[0]`) — the compiled tool has no standing read authority; only product binaries + `/usr/bin/security` remain trusted. Write/provision path unchanged.
- FIX (should): `build_app_bundle.py` `assert_no_absolute_runtime_refs` reads shipped Mach-Os back after embed and fails on any absolute staged/Homebrew load (the silent `-change` no-op). Verified live: `make package-app` PASSED with it active; `otool -L` all `@rpath`/system.
- FIX (minors): `AuthCommand` manual Debug redacts code/password; `Starting` emitted on the pump thread not the constructor; `ScopeGuard::acquire` canonicalizes `data_dir`; `removal.rs` terminate waits bounded by the outer deadline; smoke handles `failed` during submit-wait + `mask_phone`/`mask_account` on all stdout; provision runs the signed tool's new side-effect-free `--check` before deleting keychain items. New tests: `RuntimeEmbeddingTest` (7), FFI `cancel_is_accepted_in_the_unsupported_state` + `finalization_reports_failed_when_the_identity_read_fails`.
- DECISION (minor, documented not fixed): `shared_runtime` OnceLock caching a transient `TdRuntime::start` failure is left as-is — `RealTdJson::claim()` is one-shot and `TdRuntime::start` consumes the sender/receiver halves, so retry needs a `gramdrive-source-tdjson` API change the rework scope forbids; `start` only fails on thread-spawn (fatal). Flagged for a future source-crate pass.
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8 (incl. 4 new FFI + 7 new build-script tests), core restage PASSED (real_tdjson build compiles, contract 0.6.0 unchanged), `swift test` 2/2, `make package-app` PASSED + signs. Live Telegram E2E NOT run (external blocker per 0400 + owner decision; owner closes it on the release build).

### 0338 — reviewer: control channel verdict — changes requested (BUG-260720-3i74u1 → to-dev)
- VERDICT: implementation quality high, architecture fit clean (third narrow IPC channel per DEC-006, DEC-003/DEC-007 upheld, arch check green) — but 3 confirmed majors route to-dev before close.
- FINDING (major, confirmed in source): `AuthSession` has no `Drop` impl (`crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/auth.rs` — only `ScopeGuard` and a test helper implement Drop); a host dropping the object without `close()` leaks the pump thread and holds the sign-in slot scope forever, blocking all future sign-ins in-process. Swift paths do call `close()` today; fix is defense-in-depth `Drop → close()`.
- FINDING (major, confirmed in source): `persist_account` (auth.rs:919-951) overwrites the real account's vault key and wipes/renames its storage while holding only the SIGN_IN_SLOT scope guard — a concurrent `probe_authorization`/`remove_account` on the same account id races the finalization (e.g. user hits Repair while sign-in finalizes). Acquire the real account's scope in finalization.
- FINDING (major, security): `.scripts/keychain/provision-telegram-credentials.swift` `--exec` mode is a promptless secret-exfiltration primitive — team-signed, self-trusted in the items' ACL, zero callers in the repo; any local process can defeat both keychain gates through it. Remove the mode.
- FINDING (hardening): `build_app_bundle.py` `embed_runtime_libraries` `-change` fixup silently no-ops if the core artifact was relocated since staging; THIS build verified correct (`otool -L`: `@rpath/libtdjson.dylib` + `@executable_path/../Frameworks` rpath in shipped agent+app) — add a post-embed `otool -L` assertion (no absolute staged/homebrew refs) so a future relocation can't ship a build-machine-only bundle.
- NOTE (verified independently this review): swift test 275/49 green; make check 8/8; packaged bundle signed (262RZ595FP, hardened runtime), libtdjson staged; `notWired` unreachable from live backend (enum + preview support only); tdlib/td#3361 maintainer quotes verified verbatim via GitHub API; live E2E evidence real (36× PHONE_CODE_INVALID in agent8.log, test-DC IPs). External blocker stands as recorded at 0400 — status is to-dev (actionable rework), not blocked; the human-only test-account bootstrap remains for the next cycle.
- SCOPE (minors for the same rework pass, may trail): OnceLock caches a transient `TdRuntime::start` failure forever (auth.rs:536-547); `AuthCommand` derives Debug with plaintext code/password; smoke ignores `failed` while awaiting submit-result + logs operator phone unmasked; provisioning driver deletes old keychain items before the tool proves runnable; no unit coverage for new bundle-embed logic; scope key uncanonicalized.

### 0400 — Telegram retired the shared-test-number auto-code; unattended E2E sign-in externally impossible (BUG-260720-3i74u1)
- FINDING (exhaustively verified): sign-in on the REAL test DC gets `400 PHONE_CODE_INVALID` for every 99966XYYYY number and every pattern code (X*5 and X*6), on DC1 and DC2, random and popular suffixes alike. Proven at three layers: full product stack (packaged agent over the control channel), and a raw ctypes probe over the staged `libtdjson.dylib` bypassing our code entirely — server reports `authenticationCodeTypeSms length 5`, rejects both patterns. `lsof` at `wait-code` confirmed the session on **149.154.167.40:443 = test DC2** (not production), `use_test_dc` plumbing intact end to end.
- ROOT CAUSE (external): TDLib maintainer levlam, tdlib/td#3361 (2025-06): "You need to use a real phone number to log in. **The test phone numbers don't work anymore for regular users**" + "You need to create the account using an official mobile app first." Community mirrors: gram-js/gramjs#734 (archived unresolved), @mtproto/core docs. TASK-260716-1iypv4's standing assumption "test accounts provisioned programmatically, no human-owned account required" is dead server-side.
- FIX (path forward, implemented): smoke gained `--phone +NUMBER` operator mode — one interactive sign-in with a dedicated test-DC account (created once via an official app in test mode), code from stdin, `--keep` implied so the authorized session persists; restart/repair legs and future re-runs over the kept container stay unattended. Pattern mode kept for the day Telegram restores it.
- NOTE (what the failed runs DID prove live, from the shipped bundle against the real test DC): agent auto-start, silent keychain reads, `authStart` streaming `starting → configuring → wait-phone-number`, phone accepted by the real server, DC migration, `wait-code` with real `codeInfo` over the typed wire, sequence-correlated submits, the server's real rejection classified `invalid-code` through FFI → agent → wire → client, session slot reuse across attempts, agent stable throughout. The ONLY unproven hop is Telegram accepting a code — human-only now.

### 0330 — keychain partition list blocks the signed agent from `security`-created items (BUG-260720-3i74u1)
- ROOT CAUSE: E2E auth smoke hung 90s with zero events after `authStart` — `sample` showed the agent blocked in `KeychainSecretVault.apiCredentials()` → `SecItemCopyMatching` → securityd `ClientSession::decrypt` mach call, i.e. waiting on an interactive consent prompt. File-keychain items carry TWO gates: the trusted-app ACL AND the partition list (creator's code-signing partition). Items created by the `security` CLI are partition-locked to `apple-tool:`; a Developer ID binary always prompts, ACL or not (`add-generic-password -T` alone proved insufficient — retested, still blocked). Changing an existing item's partition list needs the keychain password (human-only).
- FIX: `.scripts/keychain/provision_telegram_credentials.py` + `provision-telegram-credentials.swift` — compiles and Developer ID-signs a tiny tool, recreates the `gramdrive-telegram` items FROM a team-signed binary (partition = `teamid:262RZ595FP`) with a trusted-app ACL naming packaged `gramdrive-agent`/`GramDrive`. Verified: authStart now streams `starting → configuring → wait-phone-number` unattended in seconds.
- NOTE (tradeoff): after re-provisioning, `security find-generic-password -w` itself prompts (apple-tool no longer in the partition). Re-runs of the provisioning script must pass `GRAMDRIVE_API_ID`/`GRAMDRIVE_API_HASH` env.
- NOTE (product gap, out of this bug's scope): a clean end-user machine has no `gramdrive-telegram` items at all — sign-in would fail typed with `authRequired: credentials not provisioned`. How api_id/api_hash reach end users (embedded at build vs provisioned) is an open product decision; keychain provisioning is the documented dev/CI convention (TASK-260716-1iypv4).

### 0320 — agent died of SIGPIPE from TDLib network sockets (BUG-260720-3i74u1)
- ROOT CAUSE: first E2E smoke run killed the agent with exit -13 (SIGPIPE) mid-auth-flow. The agent's own IPC channels set SO_NOSIGPIPE per socket, but TDLib's network sockets inside libtdjson carry the process default disposition — a test-DC peer resetting mid-write killed the whole process.
- FIX: `signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN)` process-wide at agent start (`AgentMain.runAgent`, before any engine work) so a dead peer surfaces as EPIPE to the writer that hit it. Regression probe added to `.scripts/smoke/run_agent_lifecycle_smoke.py` (step 2b: `kill -PIPE` → agent alive, health still answers) — green.

### 0145 — reviewer: attestation plan-gate fix accepted, BLOCKED on release gate (BUG-260720-116eli → blocked)
- VERDICT: code ACCEPTED — correct, complete, fits architecture. Re-verified independently: 24/24 provenance unittests, `actionlint` clean, job perms carry `attestations: write` + `id-token: write` (so the preflight probe can actually reach the endpoint). Fail-closed confirmed: degrades ONLY on the 2-substring signature; every other outcome leaves attestation enabled so the real attest step still hard-fails. Manifest + job summary record the gap both ways, scrub-safe. No unrelated steps touched.
- BLOCKED (not done): the two operational AC clauses — release run green end-to-end + a v0.1.0 GitHub Release with the dmg asset — are a HUMAN-ONLY gated op no agent role can run. The fix is uncommitted (role does not auto-commit); force-moving `v0.1.0` triggers a REAL signed+notarized public release; and it sits behind the mandatory `environment: release` owner-approval gate (POL-8/DEC-020, release.yml:16-21,72). Marking `done` would falsely assert the release exists.
- NEXT (human): commit+merge the fix → `git tag -f v0.1.0 <sha> && git push -f origin v0.1.0` → approve the `environment: release` gate → confirm green run + `attestation: unavailable (private-repo plan)` in the summary + v0.1.0 carries the dmg. Then closes `done`, no code rework.

### 0130 — release.yml: plan-aware attestation preflight (BUG-260720-116eli → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE (run 29703775352 log): private `relux-works/tgfs` lacks the org-plan entitlement for GitHub artifact attestation, so `actions/attest-build-provenance` hard-failed at "Failed to persist attestation: Feature not available for the relux-works organization … upgrade the billing plan, or make this repository public" — AFTER sign+notarize+staple, before `gh release create` ever ran.
- FINDING (empirically verified against this repo via `gh api`, both a `repo`-scoped PAT and the org gate): on `POST /repos/{o}/{r}/attestations` the **org-plan/entitlement check fires BEFORE body validation** → POSTing a deliberately invalid `bundle` creates nothing and cleanly distinguishes unavailable (returns the exact plan message) from available (returns a bundle-validation error). This is the crux the whole fix hangs on: a `uses:` action's error text can't be captured to classify, so you can't catch-and-degrade — you PREFLIGHT.
- FIX (`release.yml`): new step `attest_preflight` POSTs the invalid-bundle probe, degrades ONLY on the narrow two-substring signature (`Feature not available` AND `upgrade the billing plan`) → sets output `attestation_available`. Attest step (`id: attest`) gated `if: …== 'true'` — unchanged action still HARD-FAILS on real errors; skipped (not failed) when unavailable so the job continues. New job-summary step records the decision both ways. Provenance step passes `--attestation-status`.
- FIX (`build_release_provenance.py`): new `--attestation-status {available,unavailable,unknown}` (default unknown = local dry run) → manifest `attestation` block: unavailable → `{available:false, status:"unavailable (private-repo plan)", note}`; available → `{available:true, status:"attested"}`. Note wording kept clear of the credential-scrub leak-word/secret-material patterns (test-guarded).
- DECISION: degrade narrowly, fail-open on everything else — a validation error / transport failure / 5xx leaves attestation ENABLED so real errors still fail the release (task AC). Any non-obvious integrity gap is recorded in manifest + job summary, never silently dropped; notarization + CHECKSUMS/RELEASE-CHECKSUMS + SBOM remain the integrity story. Resumes with zero config once the plan upgrades or the repo goes public.
- GATE: 24/24 provenance unittests (5 new `AttestationStatusTest`); `run_automated.py --suite repo` 2/2; `actionlint` 0 errors (shellcheck 0.11.0 linted the `run:` scripts); CLI smoke (real cargo+git) both variants → correct block, scrub passed.
- RISK: preflight assumes entitlement-before-validation ordering; if GitHub reversed it the probe would mis-say "available" → hard-fail at the real attest step (= today's behavior, red run) — never a silently-worse artifact. Documented in the step comment.
- LIMIT: tag re-point of v0.1.0 → fix commit + green-run confirmation NOT executed autonomously — needs the fix committed/merged (role does not auto-commit), force-moves a tag to trigger a real signed+notarized public release, and is `environment: release` owner-gated (POL-8/DEC-020). Handed to review to run through that gate.

### 0049 — release.yml: missing Developer ID G2 CA broke signing on relux (BUG-260720-29dn2v → to-review)
- ROOT CAUSE (confirmed from run 29702885260 log): `.p12` leaf imports ("1 identity imported") but `security find-identity -v -p codesigning` lists **0 valid** Developer ID Application identities → `grep -c` exits 1 (fatal under `set -euo pipefail`). `relux` lacks the Apple **"Developer ID Certification Authority" (G2)** intermediate that hosted GitHub runners preinstall; without it the leaf never chains to the (system-trusted) Apple Root CA, so `-v` treats it invalid. This is why the migrated runner failed where hosted never would.
- FIX (release.yml import step, after the `.p12` import, before the kept find-identity check): `curl --fail` the public `https://www.apple.com/certificateauthority/DeveloperIDG2CA.cer`, verify pinned sha256 `f16cd3c54c7f83cea4bf1a3e6a0819c8aaa8e4a1528fd144715f350643d2df3a` via `shasum -a 256 --check --status` (**fail CLOSED** on mismatch), `security import` into the SAME throwaway keychain (no `-T` — public cert, no key; no trust override — anchor stays OS Apple Root CA). `.cer` rm'd in-step + in the `always()` cleanup. No residual cert/secret beyond the run (throwaway keychain deleted).
- DECISION: pin-by-sha256 not pin-by-URL-trust; a swapped/MITM'd CA never reaches the keychain. G2 (not G1) — modern Relux Works Developer ID leaf chains through the OU=G2 intermediate (subject `Developer ID Certification Authority, OU=G2`, issuer Apple Root CA, valid 2021→2031).
- GATE: `actionlint` 1.7.12 CLEAN (all workflows, incl. internal shellcheck of `run:`); `gitleaks --no-git` on release.yml → no leaks (sha256 does not trip generic-api-key; ci.yml `GITLEAKS_SHA256` is precedent); local scratch-keychain proved `1 certificate imported`. `keychain-sim.sh` on relux extended with CA coverage → **SIM OK** + `CA import: G2 intermediate present` + `CA pin: fails closed on mismatch` + zero residue (default/search list restored, keychain GONE). Sim synced to `relux:~/gramdrive-ci/keychain-sim.sh`.
- LIMIT: sim's dummy self-signed identity is NOT issued by G2, so it cannot assert `find-identity -v >=1` — only the live run with the real `.p12` proves that. NOT executed autonomously: tag re-point + live public signed release is outward-facing/credential-minting + `environment: release` owner-gated; handed to review with an exact runbook (delete+retag+push v0.1.0 → approve env → watch to green). Review the diff BEFORE pushing the tag (correct order for a credential-touching workflow).

### 0014 — Self-hosted runner migration: recon + provisioning findings (TASK-260719-1dwaj8)
- FINDING: GitHub org billing blocks BOTH hosted macOS and Linux minutes (secret-scan on ubuntu-24.04 failed at start with the billing error, run 29700024728) — so secret-scan moved to the self-hosted mac too, gitleaks darwin_x64 asset, same 8.30.1 pin + sha256.
- FINDING: local `/Applications/Xcode_26_5.app` is **arm64-only** (Mach-O arm64, LSMinimumSystemVersion 26.2) — the task's rsync-to-Intel fallback is dead on arrival. Resolved without stop-the-line: relux already has `/Applications/Xcode.app` = **Xcode 26.2 (17C52), universal x86_64+arm64, minOS 15.6**; `xcodebuild -showsdks` works via `DEVELOPER_DIR` (runner .env), no license prompt, no sudo.
- FINDING: relux CLT python3 is 3.9 — too old for the gate entrypoint (`datetime.UTC` needs 3.11+; the earlier preflight spawn died on exactly this). Provisioned python-build-standalone cpython 3.12.13 (pinned + sha256) into `~/gramdrive-ci/bin/python3`.
- ANOMALY (keychain, measured on relux): `security delete-keychain` deletes the file but leaves a **dangling search-list entry**; `security list-keychains -s` with zero args is a **silent no-op**; `security default-keychain -s` on a never-explicitly-set search list makes the list follow the new default. Consequence: a grep-filter cleanup can silently leave throwaway-keychain residue on a persistent runner.
- FIX: release.yml records default keychain + full search list VERBATIM in the naming step (before any modification) and restores both in the always() cleanup; proven by a dummy-p12 lifecycle sim on relux (`~/gramdrive-ci/keychain-sim.sh`): keychain GONE, default RESTORED, search list RESTORED, zero residue.
- DECISION: TDLib arm64 artifact cannot be cross-built on the Intel host (brew OpenSSL is x86_64-only there) — took the task's cache-seed path: pinned arm64 artifact rsync'd from this host into `~/gramdrive-ci/cache/tdlib-28775d200a3a0386/out`; native-ci proves it with file(1) arm64 + `make tdlib-smoke-link` (cross-link, no execution) and fails actionably on a cold miss.
- SCOPE: runner `relux-gramdrive` (labels self-hosted/macOS/X64/gramdrive-mac) as LaunchAgent via svc.sh, survives stop/start, online. Toolchain: rustup 1.91.0 + aarch64-apple-darwin target, cmake 4.3.3 universal, gitleaks 8.30.1, gperf 3.0.3 (OS), python 3.12.13 — all no-sudo under `~/gramdrive-ci`, every download pinned + checksum-verified (`~/gramdrive-ci/provision.sh`).

## 2026-07-19

### 2320 — macOS native acceptance harness built (TASK-260715-3oe2nr → to-review)
- SCOPE: new `.scripts/acceptance/run_native_macos.py` + `.scripts/tests/test_run_native_macos.py` (30 tests) + `.scripts/acceptance/README.md`; `Makefile` (accept-macos, accept-macos-runsheet); `README.md` tools row.
- DECISION: harness is **human-in-the-loop and never self-passes a scenario**. One scenario catalog (10 flows: registration/enumeration/hydrate/cancel/pin/update/restart/repair/upgrade/remove) drives run-sheet + evidence form + machine probes (no drift). Preflight = v1 matrix (macOS 14+ arm64, POL-5/DEC-017) + located signed GramDrive.app + codesign/spctl/fileproviderctl/App Group. Probes: machine-assert only where robust (domain registered, sig valid), else evidence capture (`fileproviderctl dump`, `com.reluxworks.gramdrive` unified log, codesign, stat). Emits `runsheet.md`/`evidence-template.md`/`summary.json` to `.temp/acceptance/<run-id>/` (NFR-052). `result` is `prepared`, never `passed`; `human_verdict: pending`.
- DECISION (avoided forced fit): did NOT fake Finder/hydration/Telegram to make a green harness. Real blockers to full automation: needs signed installed build + Telegram test account + person at Finder, and **TDLib is not yet linked into gramdrive-agent** (apple-app/README) so hydration has no unattended path. Kept it a separate script (not a run_automated gate step) — a gate that can't pass unattended would be a forced fit; mirrors package/tdlib/smoke precedent.
- FINDING: only real os_log stream under subsystem `com.reluxworks.gramdrive` is category `file-provider-domains` (CompanionMain.swift); HydrationFailure `category:` values are IPC-payload enums, NOT log categories. So domain-log probe = that category for registration/restart/update/repair/remove; hydrate/cancel use subsystem-wide predicate.
- FINDING: `fileproviderctl dump` hangs on a host with no registered GramDrive domain → added 60s per-probe timeout + execution-level dedup (identical argv runs once). Live run on macOS 26.5/arm64: preflight correctly `ready=false` because only local `GramDrive.app` is the **unsigned dev assembly** in `.temp/app-packaging/` (fails codesign --deep --strict) — honest finding, not a bug.
- GATE: `make check-repo` → traceability ok + scripts ok (2/2, new test auto-included); 30/30 self-tests. No Rust/Swift changed → builds unaffected. No Python linter configured in repo/CI.
- STATUS: to-review. Deferred HUMAN step: run `make accept-macos` on a matrix Mac with signed build + Telegram test account, fill evidence-template, attach run dir to release task (do-not-self-close per checklist item 3).

### 2140 — shared core CI stood up (TASK-260715-3faqmr → to-review)
- SCOPE: new `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (2 jobs) + `.gitleaks.toml` + `.gitleaksignore`; `secret-scan` step and `security` suite added to `.scripts/acceptance/run_automated.py` (+4 tests, 174→178); `make check-security`; README "Continuous integration" section.
- DESIGN: barycenter pattern — every job is `run_automated.py --suite <x> --require-clean --run-id ci-<x>`, never an ad-hoc command list. `rust-core` (macos-15 arm64, POL-5 host) runs `--suite all` = the DoD checklist's fmt/clippy/tests/deny(POL-6)/architecture/traceability + script self-tests; `secret-scan` (ubuntu-24.04) runs `--suite security`. Two required checks. Each uploads `.temp/acceptance/<run-id>` (`if: always()`, `if-no-files-found: error`, 14d) → NFR-052 attributability.
- DECISION (secret scanning goes through the runner, not YAML): added it as its own `security` suite so it is runnable pre-push (`make check-security`) and provenance-tracked — the repo's single-entrypoint contract. Kept OUT of `all`/`make check` because it is the only gitleaks-dependent gate; folding it in would break the everyday local gate for anyone without gitleaks. gitleaks runs `git` mode (history, not just worktree — a deleted-but-committed secret still ships in clones) with `--redact` (AC: no secret in logs). One confirmed false positive (a board checklist line "key roundtrip, missing/corrupt key…") pinned by fingerprint in `.gitleaksignore`, not by weakening a rule.
- DECISION (pinning for "cache cannot alter results"): toolchain via rust-toolchain.toml, deps via Cargo.lock, cargo-deny 0.20.2 + gitleaks 8.30.1 by exact version (gitleaks also sha256-verified `551f6fc8…`), every action by commit SHA. Swatinem/rust-cache keyed on toolchain+lockfile → a hit only holds artifacts from identical inputs; cache changes speed, never verdict. `permissions: contents: read` workflow-wide; no job consumes a secret.
- EVIDENCE (this host): `actionlint -shellcheck` exit 0; `--suite all` 8/8 + `--suite security` 1/1; runner self-tests 178/178; gitleaks 60 commits / 0 leaks. Required-check branch protection on `main` is the one repo-admin step CI YAML cannot self-grant (documented).

### 2110 — macOS signing/entitlements/packaging pipeline (TASK-260715-1dk9ik → to-review)
- SCOPE: new `.scripts/apple-app/build_app_bundle.py` + `README.md`; `.scripts/tests/test_build_app_bundle.py` (41 faked-subprocess tests, run by repo gate); `apple/GramDriveSupport/Package.swift` + new `Sources/GramDriveFileProviderExtensionApp/main.swift` (appex entry); `Makefile` (`package-app`, `package-app-notarize`); `.spec/platform-requirements.md` (agent identifier row). Boundary: this owns the LOCAL reusable script + make target (barycenter one-entrypoint); the tag-triggered release.yml that invokes it is TASK-260715-3bhbkv.
- MILESTONE: real end-to-end PROVEN on this host (Xcode 26.5, Developer ID Application Relux Works LLC 262RZ595FP). `codesign --verify --deep --strict` exit 0; `flags=0x10000(runtime)` on app+agent+appex; notarization submission `e012f0ae-...` → Accepted; `stapler validate` ok; `spctl --assess --type exec` → accepted, source=Notarized Developer ID. AC met and exceeded.
- DECISION (appex entry): SwiftPM emits no `.appex`/`NSExtensionMain`. Added `GramDriveFileProviderExtensionApp` executable target whose `main.swift` calls `NSExtensionMain` (`@_silgen_name`, exported from Foundation.tbd — verified) and touches `GramDriveFileProviderExtension.self` to defeat dead-strip. `nm` confirms `_NSExtensionMain` referenced + `_OBJC_CLASS_$__TtC21GramDriveFileProvider30GramDriveFileProviderExtension` present. Info.plist `NSExtensionPrincipalClass` = `GramDriveFileProvider.GramDriveFileProviderExtension` (Swift-mangled runtime name of the defining module; class is not `@objc(...)`-renamed, so no annotation needed). `swift test` still 252/252.
- DECISION (entitlements, Developer ID, hardened runtime, macOS 14+): app + agent = app-groups only, UNSANDBOXED; appex = app-sandbox=true + app-groups (macOS File Provider extensions run sandboxed; the App Group is how a sandboxed appex reaches durable state + the agent hydration socket). All three via `--options runtime` + `--timestamp`; NO `get-task-allow` (SwiftPM's debug default sets it true → fails notarization; verify step dumps+asserts it is absent). App Group = team-prefixed `262RZ595FP.com.reluxworks.gramdrive` (no portal reg / no provisioning profile under Developer ID). Agent bundle id `com.reluxworks.gramdrive.agent` set via `codesign --identifier` (loose Mach-O, no Info.plist) — closes LOGBOOK 172 "packaging story should add the agent row".
- FINDING (reproducibility, honest): a Developer ID signature embeds a trusted timestamp that varies per signature BY DESIGN, so the signed bytes are not byte-reproducible. Manifest claims `attributable: true`, `byte_identical: false` — NFR-052 asks for attributability (commit/toolchain/core-version/cdhash/checksums), which the manifest + CHECKSUMS.sha256 provide; it does not ask a signature to be byte-identical.

Also the deferred live domain-registration proof (LOGBOOK 141) is now unblocked (a real signed bundle embedding the appex exists) but functional domain-load belongs to acceptance TASK-260715-3oe2nr.
- NO CREDENTIALS: identity resolved from keychain (name/team recorded, never key); notarization via `gramdrive-notary` profile (submission id/status recorded). Self-tests assert manifest carries no PRIVATE KEY / p12 / password / AuthKey strings.

### 2035 — REVIEW-01: offline pin/eviction macOS surface ACCEPTED (TASK-260715-3s461k → done)
- VERDICT: accepted → `done`. AC met for the SYNC-053 macOS-surface scope; architecture fits; gates green.
- GATES (reviewer re-ran independently): `cargo test -p gramdrive-ffi` 29/29 (2 new pin tests incl. reopen-durability + unpin), `swift test` 252/252 across 47 suites (8 new content-policy tests incl. exhaustive kind×availability×pin matrix), `make check` 8/8.
- ARCHITECTURE: read-only `StateRole::Provider` surface — no pin write path leaked; `pin` folded from `ReadTxn::pin` as same-snapshot lookup on all four reads; additive contract 0.4.0→0.5.0 with `#[uniffi(default=None)]`. The eager-parent / explicitly-lazy-child model is self-consistent with NSFileProviderContentPolicy inheritance and correctly respects engine backfill pacing.
- FINDING CONFIRMED (verified by reading `cache/pin.rs`): engine `pin`/`unpin` write the `pins` table + fold the cache row but never bump `metadata_version` or journal a change → pin changes reach the working set only on restart/re-enumeration, not live. Correctly OUT of this read-only surface's scope (a provider must not bump `metadata_version`); it's TASK-260715-11abx8 core territory. Developer stopped at the boundary and flagged rather than hacking — right call. RECOMMENDATION for coordinator: open an engine follow-up to journal a `metadata_version` bump on pin/unpin so Finder reflects pin changes without a restart.
- NIT (non-blocking): logbook/notes cite "DEC-006 keeps the extension read-only," but DEC-006 is the no-TDLib-in-iOS decision; read-only actually traces to `StateRole::Provider` / NFR-014 / DOM-008. Code unaffected.
- EVIDENCE: board resource `TASK-260715-3s461k_review-r1.md`.

- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/shared_state.rs` (FFI read surface), `crates/gramdrive-ffi/src/api.rs` (contract bump), `apple/.../GramDriveFileProvider/FileProviderItem.swift` (mapping) + item/enumerator tests.

DEC-006 keeps the extension read-only, so the deliverable is projecting durable core pin → `NSFileProviderContentPolicy`, not writing state back.

So a pin/unpin never enters the working-set change feed, and the system re-reads the new `contentPolicy` only on restart/full re-enumeration, not live. Durability across restart holds (re-derived from durable state); live propagation needs the engine to bump `metadata_version` on pin change. Flagged for the engine/coordinator owner.

### 1947 — REVIEW-03: round-3 determinism rework ACCEPTED (TASK-260715-kkglhx → done)
- VERDICT: accepted → `done`. Closes the flaky-suite defect from review-02. AC/architecture were accepted in review-01/02 and product code is unchanged this round; only determinism was outstanding.

Pre-fix flake ran ~28%, so 0/20 ≈ 0.72²⁰ ≈ 0.16% if still live — decisive. `make check` 8/8. Logs `.temp/TASK-260715-kkglhx/review-r3/`.

Lock-file write (`SingleInstanceLock.swift:55`) and stderr writes aren't peer sockets. Production agent ignores only SIGTERM/SIGINT (`AgentMain.swift:126`), so it relies solely on per-socket `SO_NOSIGPIPE`, which is complete. The test-only `signal(SIGPIPE,SIG_IGN)` hardens only the multi-role test process and converts any stray EPIPE into a *catchable* error, not a hidden one.

Fix 1 (`HydrationConnection` guard) and Fix 2 (`catch UnixSocketError → serverUnreachable`, ContentFetcher.swift:208-220, DriveError sibling-catch passthrough preserved) both intact.
