### 2015 — REWORK-R3: flaky-suite determinism, 5 flakes killed test-only (TASK-260715-kkglhx → to-review)
- FIX (busyBound, `HydrationServerTests.swift`): now catches `HydrationFailure(.busy)` OR `is UnixSocketError`/`is HydrationTransportError` — the refuse-before-read races the client write; any other error still fails.
- FINDING (SIGPIPE, key): the review's per-socket `SO_NOSIGPIPE` at `HydrationServerTests.swift:315` is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT. Audited every socket `write`/`send` in product + tests — all already `SO_NOSIGPIPE`-guarded on their fd (client `HydrationClient.swift:119`, server `:205`, health `HealthChannel.swift:107`, scripted server `HydrationChannelTests.swift:63`, + fixed 315). Yet the process still died by signal 13 under load, from the client/server *refuse-races-write* path, not line 315. `lldb` perturbed timing enough to hide it 40/40. The reviewer's "confirm while fixing" hedge held.
- FIX (SIGPIPE): added process-wide `signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN)`, installed idempotently via each socket-writing suite's `init()` in both test modules. `write` still returns EPIPE (same errno `SO_NOSIGPIPE` yields) so error handling incl. the busyBound catch is unchanged. Production untouched (keeps per-socket `SO_NOSIGPIPE`); guard hardens only the shared test process hosting client+server+raw sockets under 240-way parallelism.
- FINDING (registry race): `hydrationRegistersInTheLedger` sampled `registry.pendingCount==0` synchronously, but server calls `registry.end` (`HydrationServer.swift:299`) AFTER `writeEvent(.done)` (`:285`) — the event that unblocks the client — so `hydrate` returns before cleanup. Product correct. FIX: bounded poll (≤2.5s) for the drain.
- FINDING (fd-reuse): `cancelWhileLive` + `finishRetires` (`HydrationChannelTests.swift`) asserted `fcntl(closedFd,F_GETFD)==-1` — racy, a parallel suite reuses the freed fd number. Contradicted the suite's OWN doc-comment ("reuse can't be forced portably; pin the state machine"). FIX (reuse-immune): `finishRetires` uses a `socketpair` + peer-EOF to prove `finish()` closed the fd without reading the number back; `cancelWhileLive` keeps cancel→EOF + swaps the racy check for the no-double-close observable. Accepted Fix-1 product code (`HydrationConnection`) untouched.
- DECISION (scope): (c)+(d) exceed the two named fixes but are the same parallel-runner-timing flake class this task targets, test-only, no product/server changes — kept, and disclosed to review.
- GATES: `swift test` 30/30 plain + 40/40 under NCPU-1 busy-loop load (harshest) = 95 full-suite runs post-fix, 0 fail / 0 SIGPIPE. `make check` 8/8 (`.temp/acceptance/local-all`). `swift build --build-tests` clean bar pre-existing `Progress:@retroactive` warning.
- STATUS: `to-review`. Evidence: board resource `TASK-260715-kkglhx_rework-r3-results.md`; logs `.temp/TASK-260715-kkglhx/`.

### 1915 — REVIEW-02: rework fixes accepted, blocked on pre-existing swift-test flake (TASK-260715-kkglhx → to-dev)
- VERDICT: changes requested. Both review-01 surgical fixes independently verified CORRECT and accepted — Fix 1 `HydrationConnection` fd-ownership/`finish()` closed-flag (mirrors server, race-safe, 3 state-machine tests) and Fix 2 `ContentFetcher` `UnixSocketError → serverUnreachable` (wire-scoped, DriveError passthrough intact, 7 parametrized tests). `make check` 8/8; `swift build` clean.
- ANOMALY (flaky/crashy suite): `swift test` is NOT deterministically green — ~28% of full-suite runs fail under parallel load (measured 5/25 + 2/25 over two loops). Isolated `busyBound` is 0/20; needs parallel timing.
- ROOT CAUSE (one class): server refuses `busy`/malformed *before reading the request* (`HydrationServer.swift:227-234`, `admit` precedes `readRequest`), so `refuse()`→`finish()` closes the socket while the client is still writing → client `send()` gets EPIPE.
- FINDING (test bug #1, ~20%): `busyBound` (`HydrationServerTests.swift:277`) only `catch let failure as HydrationFailure`, so the client's `UnixSocketError.failed("write",EPIPE=32)` escapes as a recorded issue. NOT a product bug — `ContentFetcher` maps exactly this to `serverUnreachable` via Fix 2. Over-narrow expectation. Fix: accept EITHER `.busy` OR a transport/socket error.
- FINDING (test bug #2, ~8%): SIGPIPE (signal 13) kills the whole test process (no `.ips` — signal, not Mach exception). Only unprotected raw-socket `write` in the tree is `HydrationServerTests.swift:315` (`malformedRequestRefused`) on an fd with no `SO_NOSIGPIPE`. Fix: set `SO_NOSIGPIPE` before writing (macOS has no `MSG_NOSIGNAL`).
- FINDING (provenance): pre-existing — rework touched only `HydrationClient.swift` + `ContentFetcher.swift`; `send()`/`SO_NOSIGPIPE` ordering unchanged and the refusal race predates it. review-01's 239/239 and rework-results' "244/244 stable across looped runs" both got lucky; not reproducible under load.
- NOTE (lesson): a wire refusal that closes before consuming the request line is inherently racy on the client write; unit tests over that path must tolerate a transport error, and every raw test socket that writes needs `SO_NOSIGPIPE`.
- STATUS: `to-dev` for test-only hardening (do NOT touch accepted product code or server design); re-loop `swift test` ≥20× + `make check`, then `to-review`. Evidence: board resource `TASK-260715-kkglhx_review-02.md`.

### 1912 — REWORK: content fetch review-01 fixes applied (TASK-260715-kkglhx → to-review)
- FIX (fd-reuse race): `HydrationClient.swift` — `HydrationConnection` now owns the descriptor. New `finish()` closes it once under the lock and retires it (`descriptor=nil`, `closed=true`); `cancel()` gained the `!closed` guard, so a post-`finish` cancel is a no-op (mirror of the server `Connection.finish()` closed-flag pattern). `exchange()`: socket → fcntl → adopt (closes the fd itself on refusal) → `defer { connection.finish() }`. During the exchange `cancel()` still shuts the live fd down (wire cancel). `HydrationConnection` made `internal` for testability.
- FIX (NFR-030 gap): `ContentFetcher.performFetch` gained `catch let socketError as UnixSocketError → NSFileProviderError(.serverUnreachable)`, scoped to the wire. DriveError storage passthrough from `liveFile` untouched (initial call is outside the `do`; restart-branch call throws from a sibling `catch`) and `CancellationError` still propagates.
- FINDING (untestable-race): the fd-reuse race can't be forced portably — Swift Testing runs suites in parallel and fd numbers are process-global, so a `socketpair`+`victim.contains(sacrifice)` reuse-victim test flaked (~1-in-N: another suite grabbed the freed number first). Replaced with deterministic state-machine tests on `HydrationConnection` (`HydrationConnectionTests`). Lesson: don't assert on specific reclaimed fd numbers in a parallel runner.
- TESTS: `ContentFetcherTests` parametrized `rawSocketFaultMapsToServerUnreachable` (EPIPE/ECONNRESET/EINTR/EMFILE/EPERM/ENOTCONN) + unrepresentable-path; new `ScriptedHydration.enqueueSocketFailure`. `HydrationConnectionTests`: adopt-after-cancel refuses, cancel-while-live → peer EOF + close-once, finish-then-cancel no-op.
- GATES: `swift test` 244/244 in 46 suites (stable across repeated + looped runs); `make check` 8/8 (`.temp/acceptance/local-all`); `swift build` clean bar the pre-existing `Progress: @retroactive Sendable` warning.
- STATUS: `to-review`. Evidence: board resource `TASK-260715-kkglhx_rework-results.md`.

### 1836 — REVIEW: content fetch CHANGES REQUESTED (TASK-260715-kkglhx → to-dev)
- VERDICT: changes requested — two defects in the new code; everything else accepted (DEC-006/POL-4/PRD-043/version-race/bounded-memory design and the two documented deferrals all hold up under line-by-line read).
- FINDING (race): `HydrationClient.swift` — `exchange()` closes the socket in `defer { close(fd) }` but the fd is never cleared from `HydrationConnection`; `cancel()` can fire until `withTaskCancellationHandler` returns, i.e. after the close, and then `shutdown()`s a stale fd *number* that another thread may have reused (another concurrent fetch's socket, scratch I/O, XPC). Server side already guards exactly this (`Connection.finish()` sets `closed` before `close`; `shutdownWire()` checks it) — the client lacks the mirror guard. Realistic trigger: `invalidate()`/user cancel landing as a fetch completes.
- FINDING (NFR-030 gap): `ContentFetcher.performFetch` maps `HydrationFailure` + `HydrationTransportError` but raw `UnixSocketError.failed` leaks unmapped to the system (EPIPE on send when the agent dies post-accept, EINTR/ECONNRESET on read, `socket()`/`connect` edge errnos) instead of `serverUnreachable`. Fix must not disturb the deliberate, test-pinned `DriveError` storage passthrough in `liveFile`.
- GATES (independently re-run by reviewer): `swift test` 239/239 in 45 suites; `make check` 8/8; smoke logs verified in `.temp/TASK-260715-kkglhx/`. Server tests confirmed to exercise the real client against the real socket (not mock theater).
- STATUS: routed `to-dev`; evidence in board resource `TASK-260715-kkglhx_review-01.md`. After the two fixes: re-run gates, back `to-review`.

### 1826 — File Provider content fetch over a new agent hydration channel (TASK-260715-kkglhx → to-review)
- MILESTONE: `fetchContents` is real. The bridge: extension `ContentFetcher` → `AgentHydrationClient` → `agent/hydration.sock` → `HydrationServer` → `ContentHydrating` seam (the engine bridge's socket). Bytes never cross the socket: `done` names the engine-staged file in the shared container; the extension APFS-clones it into the provider scratch dir, verifies the byte count, and only then hands the URL to the system (PRD-043). `swift test` 239/239 (+45); `make check` 8/8; both smokes PASSED.
- FINDING (design-forcing): the FFI exports NO fetch/transfer surface (contract 0.4.0 = snapshot reads only; writes are deliberately absent per DEC-006), and the agent hosts no engine yet — so "bridge to the shared transfer engine" cannot mean an in-extension engine call. The extension-side bridge is IPC to the coordinator process, matching PLAT-MAC-002's narrow-native-service shape; the engine-backed `ContentHydrating` lands with the FFI fetch export (no board task for that export yet — flagged to coordinator).
- DECISION (honest absence over stub): `AgentConfiguration.hydrator` is optional; with none wired the hydration endpoint is not offered at all, so a fetching extension gets connect-refused → `serverUnreachable` — the truthful "companion cannot hydrate" state. No production code fakes bytes.
- DECISION (wire shape mirrors the health channel's boundedness): one request line per connection (16 KiB cap), progress/done/failure event lines back (64 KiB cap), cancel = client closes (server EOF-monitor fires the FFI `CancellationToken`). Hydrations register in `TransferRegistry`, so shutdown drains them through the same grace-then-cancel machinery as every transfer.
- DECISION (version races, SYNC-042 provider-side): fetch pins the metadata snapshot's `contentVersion`; server admission refuses a stale pin with `versionConflict`; the extension restarts once against a fresh snapshot and only when the store actually moved — an unmoved snapshot fails `serverUnreachable` instead of spinning on the same stale token. A `done` whose token differs from the pin is treated as the same conflict. A *requested* stale `NSFileProviderItemVersion` is served as current (Telegram keeps no history; documented provider fallback).
- DECISION (POL-4 twice): the extension refuses restricted/unavailable with `fileReadNoPermission` before any IPC (tests pin zero agent contact), and the server's store-backed admission independently refuses non-fetchable availability — the engine is never asked for bytes it must never fetch.
- FINDING (Swift 6 strict concurrency): `NSLock.lock()` is unavailable in async contexts — every locked step on an async path needs a sync helper (pattern already in `TransferRegistry`); `Foundation.Progress` needed `@retroactive @unchecked Sendable` (documented thread-safe, SDK unannotated); a non-Sendable `NSFileProviderItemVersion` captured by a `Task` closure trips region analysis — dropped from the task entirely since the version needs no branch.
- SCOPE: `apple/GramDriveSupport`: `Sources/GramDriveSupport/{HydrationContract,HydrationClient,UnixSocketAddress(moved from AgentCore, now public)}.swift`, `Sources/GramDriveAgentCore/{HydrationServer(new),AgentLifecycle,AgentRuntimeLayout,HealthChannel}.swift`, `Sources/GramDriveFileProvider/{ContentFetcher(new),FileProviderExtension}.swift`, 4 new test files (+2 updated), README.
- NOTE: extension-side concurrency bound = FIFO `FetchGate` (4) below the server's bound (8), so the `busy` refusal is unreachable from a well-behaved extension; queued fetches stay individually cancellable, and `invalidate()` cancels all in flight.

### 1747 — Enumerators + durable change anchors over a new core change journal (TASK-260715-rhcnhc → to-review)
- MILESTONE: full enumeration surface lands — `GramDriveEnumerator` (keyset paged listing per SYNC-003/NFR-021, journal-anchored `enumerateChanges`, working set as the domain-wide change feed), `EnumerationPageCursor`/`EnumerationSyncAnchor` codecs, `ChangeSignalRelay` (doorbell → `signalEnumerator(for: .workingSet)` behind the `WorkingSetSignaling` seam), `enumerator(for:request:)` wired on the extension. `swift test` 194/194; `make check` 8/8; `make smoke-shared-state` PASSED on the repacked artifact.
- FINDING (design-forcing): no durable change vocabulary existed — `data_version` is connection-relative by contract (never persist), and the only journal was the *message* event log. NSFileProvider sync anchors survive extension restarts, so without core support every restart would answer `.syncAnchorExpired` and re-import whole domains. Durable change enumeration therefore required a core addition, not a Swift workaround.
- DECISION (schema v2 — first real migration): `item_changes` coalesced journal (one row per item at its latest `AUTOINCREMENT` seq — bounded by item count; issuance never rewinds, proven via `sqlite_sequence` so cascades can't lower the high-water mark) + `item_change_journal.instance_id` (`randomblob(16)` at migration time) naming the database life, so an anchor surviving quarantine-and-reseed expires explicitly instead of pointing at unrelated sequences. Journaling lives inside the three item write paths (`upsert_item`/`update_item_content`/`tombstone_item`), which are provably the only `items` writers.
- DECISION (no-op discipline is required, not an optimization): engine re-baseline after restart re-pushes identical rows (SYNC-021 replay); a blind journal would replay the whole tree at the provider boundary on every agent restart. `upsert_item` now compares the stored provider-visible row (exactly the ON CONFLICT column set) and skips write+journal when identical; content republish and tombstone-of-tombstone likewise quiet.
- DECISION (anchor validity is bound in, not assumed): anchors carry {codec version, account, namespace epoch, journal instance, sequence}; expiry on any mismatch or a never-issued sequence. Pages carry {codec version, container, last id} — a replayed page can never anchor one directory's listing inside another (foreign → `.pageExpired`). Recovery is always the platform's explicit path, never a silently wrong diff.
- FINDING (concurrency semantics): keyset pages never duplicate and never resurrect behind the anchor; an item landing *behind* a served page during listing is invisible to the listing but replayed by change enumeration from the anchor minted before listing began — pinned by scripted mid-enumeration insert/rename/tombstone tests (`GramDriveEnumeratorTests`), with core paging/journal semantics pinned Rust-side (`repo_item_changes.rs`, FFI tests).
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state` (schema/v2.sql, SCHEMA_VERSION 2, migrate registry + fixture test, repo/item_changes.rs, items.rs write paths, tests), `crates/gramdrive-ffi` (`ItemChange`, `ChangeJournalState`, `item_changes_since`, `change_journal_state`; contract 0.3.0 → 0.4.0 additive), repacked `GramDriveCore`, `apple/GramDriveSupport/Sources/GramDriveFileProvider/{GramDriveEnumerator,EnumerationPageCursor,EnumerationSyncAnchor,ChangeSignalRelay,FileProviderExtension}.swift` + 4 new test files.
- NOTE: `ChangeSignalRelay` is built and tested but not yet hosted — wiring it into the companion/agent alongside domain registration belongs with the engine-host/content stories; probe-on-start covers rings missed while down, so late hosting loses nothing durable.

### 1704 — Core items mapped to NSFileProviderItem (TASK-260715-i3mp9x → to-review)
- MILESTONE: `GramDriveFileProviderItem` (pure, total map of `ItemMetadata` + account root id → `NSFileProviderItem`) plus `ItemIdentifierMapping` and a real `item(for:)`/`resolveItem(for:)` on the extension. Read-only per DEC-007/SYNC-060; POL-4 restricted/unavailable content withholds bytes; POL-3 tombstone + unknown id → `noSuchItem`; storage failure passes through. `swift test` 170/170; `make smoke-shared-state` PASSED with new 2c mapping assertions over the seeded tree.
- FINDING (platform, non-obvious): on macOS `NSFileProviderItemCapabilitiesAllowsContentEnumerating == AllowsReading` (both bit `1<<0`) and `AllowsAddingSubItems == AllowsWriting` (both `1<<1`) — they are *aliases*, not distinct bits (`FileProvider.framework/Headers/NSFileProviderItem.h`). So a directory's "enumerate" and a file's "read" are the same bit, and `.contains(.allowsReading)` is true for any enumerable folder. The read-only invariant is "bit 0 only, never bit 1"; a capability check must read intent from `isDirectory`, not from the enumerating/reading pair.
- FINDING: core caps version tokens at 256 bytes (`gramdrive-model MAX_VERSION_TOKEN_BYTES`) but `NSFileProviderItemVersion` requires each component ≤ 128 bytes — a real mismatch. Long tokens fold to their SHA-256 digest (32 bytes, equality-preserving). Absent content version → `0x00` sentinel, which is collision-proof because `validate_token` forbids ASCII control bytes in any real token.
- FINDING: `UTType(mimeType:)` synthesizes a *dynamic* (`dyn…`) type for an unrecognized MIME instead of returning nil, so a bare non-nil check lets a useless dynamic MIME type shadow a good filename-extension match. Content-type resolution must prefer a *declared* (`isDeclared`) type — MIME first, then extension — before accepting any dynamic type; `.data` is the last resort.
- SCOPE: `apple/GramDriveSupport/Sources/GramDriveFileProvider/{FileProviderItem,ItemIdentifierMapping,FileProviderExtension}.swift`, `Tests/GramDriveFileProviderTests/FileProviderItemTests.swift`, `Sources/SharedStateSmoke/main.swift`, `.scripts/smoke/run_shared_state_smoke.py`.
- NOTE: mapping is pure so it is fully unit-tested from hand-built fixtures (DEC-006 keeps writes off the FFI — tests cannot seed a store); the seeded-store proof is the shared-state smoke's `domains` mode.

### 1641 — REVIEW (rework): domain removal & repair ACCEPTED (TASK-260715-gnat2x → done; STORY-260715-2pe5sa → done)
- VERDICT: accepted. The Option-A rework resolves all four blocking points from the 1626 review: (1) `CompanionMain.init()` reverted to add-only `DomainStartupReconcile.run()` (SYNC-070) — no launch-time `DomainRepair` caller remains; (2) `DomainRepair.run()` wired behind an explicit `.commands` menu action "Repair File Provider Domains…" (SYNC-071), off-main; (3) the spurious-empty vector is closed by `TotalTeardownPolicy.refuse` (default): when `desired.isEmpty && !plan.strays.isEmpty` repair withholds every removal and reports `withheldStrays`/`withheldTotalTeardown` — verified the guard runs after the (no-op) adds/renames loop so there is no partial side effect; (4) docs reconciled and `DomainStartupReconcile` is a live caller again (no dead code, no self-contradicting safety doc).
- VERIFIED (design, not just diff): `desiredDomains(for: [])` returns `[]` so an empty read makes every registered domain a stray with empty adds/renames — exactly the guarded case; `wasSettled` is correctly `false` for a withheld teardown; no destructive path is reachable from the UI (menu action calls `run()` with default `.refuse`; `.allow` is never wired to a click); corruption still fails closed (throws on provider open → `.failed`).
- GATES (independently re-run by reviewer, not trusted from notes): `swift test` 140/140 in 29 suites (was 136; +4 destructive-branch tests `refusesTotalTeardown`, `allowedTotalTeardownRemovesAll`, `straysRemovedWhenAnAccountRemains`, `runRefusesTotalTeardown`); `make check` 8/8.
- NON-BLOCKING (no rework): the menu action only logs (withheld-teardown / preserved-location surfacing belongs to the companion-app story's view models, correctly deferred); the guard is scoped to empty-desired only (a hypothetical *partial* spurious read still cleans N−1 as strays, but only under an explicit user repair, never at launch — matches the review's exact ask). Evidence: `TASK-260715-gnat2x_review-verdict-accepted.md`.

### 1637 — REWORK (Option A): launch add-only, repair user-triggered, total-teardown guarded (TASK-260715-gnat2x → to-review)
- FIX (launch reverted to add-only, SYNC-070): `CompanionMain.init()` runs `DomainStartupReconcile.run()` again — add/rename only, never removes. Comment restates the invariant (auto-teardown at launch is the failure mode). Resolves the self-contradiction with `DomainRepair.swift` rationale (now consistent) and the dead-code finding (`DomainStartupReconcile` is the live launch caller again).
- FIX (repair behind explicit user action, SYNC-071): `CompanionMain` adds a `.commands { CommandGroup(after: .appInfo) { Button "Repair File Provider Domains…" } }` → new `GramDriveCompanionApp.repairFileProviderDomains()` runs `DomainRepair.run()` off-main. App-side domain repair (PLAT-MAC-001) is distinct from the engine-side `RepairViewModel` (repairs the *source* via the agent control channel).
- FIX (total-teardown guard): new `TotalTeardownPolicy{refuse(default),allow}` on `DomainRepair.repair(...)`/`repair(store:)`/`run(dataRoot:)`/`run()`. When `desired.isEmpty && !plan.strays.isEmpty && .refuse`, repair withholds ALL stray removals (removes nothing) and reports them via new `DomainRepairOutcome.withheldStrays`/`withheldTotalTeardown`. Closes the spurious-empty vector the review flagged (empty account list is a normal non-throwing answer, so fail-closed-on-throw never covered total teardown). Guard is narrow — a genuine orphan alongside a live account (`desired` non-empty) is still cleaned. Genuine last-account logout removes its domain via targeted `DomainRemoval`, not by repair inferring teardown from emptiness.
- DECISION (Option A over B): matches the SYNC-070/071 spec split and the retained doc; removes the dead-code + doc-contradiction problems for free. Guard added on top of A as defense-in-depth so even a user-triggered repair cannot wipe every domain from a spurious-empty read without explicit confirmation.
- DOCS: `DomainRepair.swift` rationale reconciled; README `DomainStartupReconcile`/`DomainRepair` rows + prose rewritten (launch=add-only; repair=user-triggered+guarded); verification bullet updated.
- STATUS: `swift build` clean; `swift test` 140/140 in 29 suites (+4 destructive-branch tests: `refusesTotalTeardown`, `allowedTotalTeardownRemovesAll`, `straysRemovedWhenAnAccountRemains`, `runRefusesTotalTeardown`; `failureReportsFailed` now passes `.allow` to reach the failing remover); `make check` 8/8. ### 1626 — REVIEW: domain removal & repair CHANGES REQUESTED (TASK-260715-gnat2x → to-dev)
- VERDICT: changes requested. Removal/repair *primitives* accepted (clean remover-vs-registrar seam split, correct disposition→mode mapping, idempotent per-account removal, adds-before-stray-removal, interruption-at-each-step + re-registration-after-crash tested, ownership boundary respected — engine-side SEC-004 halves left to agent behind `notWired` channel, no forced fit). Gates independently re-run green: `swift test` 136/136 in 29 suites; `make check` 8/8 (Rust core only — the whole changeset is Swift, so `swift test` is the real gate).
- REGRESSION (launch wiring reverses a documented safety invariant): `CompanionMain.init()` switched add-only `DomainStartupReconcile.run()` → full `DomainRepair.run()` (`CompanionMain.swift:42`), so stray removal now runs automatically every launch — directly contradicting the retained rationale at `DomainRepair.swift:64-68` ("repair is user-triggered … automatically destroying Finder state on every launch is the failure mode the split guards against").
- FINDING (fails-closed claim is false for empty reads): the launch comment + `results.md` claim repair "only ever tears down genuine orphans" and "fails closed". Rust `accounts()` (`shared_state.rs`) treats an empty list as "a normal answer, not an error", so a spurious-empty read — App Group ID change on upgrade (PLAT-004 scope) or a state-dir reset — makes EVERY registered domain a stray and auto-repair tears them all down. Only the *throwing* case fails closed; corruption throws on provider open (safe), but empty does not. Spec SYNC-070 (auto reconcile) vs SYNC-071 (user-triggered repair) collapsed into one auto path.
- FINDING (test gap): the destructive branch (empty desired set + non-empty registered set) is untested — `emptyRepairSettles`/`emptyContainer` both have zero registered domains. AC requires flows be "covered".
- NOTE (dead code): `DomainStartupReconcile` has no production caller after the switch (README:88 still frames it as "the add-only building block").
- DECISION (routing → to-dev, not blocked): both remediations are autonomous. Option A — revert launch to add-only reconcile, wire `DomainRepair` behind an explicit user action (matches spec + doc). Option B — guard the everything-is-a-stray case + add a test + fix the contradicting doc. Evidence: `TASK-260715-gnat2x_review-verdict.md`.

### 1620 — File Provider domain removal & repair implemented (TASK-260715-gnat2x → to-review)
- MILESTONE: the domain-lifecycle *teardown* half exists in `GramDriveFileProvider` — `DomainRemover`/`SystemDomainRemover` (the removal seam, deliberately separate from `DomainRegistrar` which still has no remove, so "registration never destroys Finder state" stays structural), `DomainDataDisposition`, `DomainRemoval` (targeted idempotent per-account unregister), `DomainRepair` (reconcile + stray resolution + never-throwing `run()` entries).
- DECISION (preserve-or-delete = the domain removal mode, not an engine concern): `DomainDataDisposition.deleteLocalData` → `NSFileProviderManager.DomainRemovalMode.removeAll`; `.preserveDownloads` → `.preserveDownloadedUserData` (system moves the user's files aside, returns the location). The third platform mode `preserveDirtyUserData` is omitted on purpose — read-only V1 (PLAT-MAC-004) never has dirty user data. This is the SEC-004 "provider registrations" cleanup step; "local data" = the Finder-materialized copies, exactly what the removal mode governs.
- DECISION (interruption safety by ordering, not transactions): removal = engine drops the canonical account row FIRST, then the app removes the domain; a crash between leaves a stray, never a domain re-registered for a gone account. Repair applies adds/renames BEFORE stray removal, so an interrupted pass converges from either side and a completed pass re-runs to a settled no-op. Repair FAILS CLOSED — removes strays only after successfully reading the canonical rows, so an unreadable store touches nothing.
- DECISION (launch pass upgraded add-only → full repair): `CompanionMain` now runs `DomainRepair.run()` at launch instead of `DomainStartupReconcile.run()`. This is SYNC-070 startup recovery: a Finder root orphaned by an interrupted logout self-heals next launch, so "logout leaves no broken Finder root" holds with no user action. Safe because the ONLY domains repair tears down are genuine orphans (a registered domain implies a row once existed; a stray means the row was deleted), and stray downloads are preserved by default. This completes the split 3s44pc deliberately deferred here (strays were reported-never-touched, removal seam withheld from the reconciler).
- FINDING (ownership boundary, no forced fit): domain management is app-side (PLAT-MAC-001) and the Swift `SharedStateStore` is read-only, so this task does NOT delete account rows. The engine-side SEC-004 halves (server logout, trace-free on-disk wipe) live in the agent (coordinator role) behind the control channel, which is `notWired` in this build (separate story). No stub/flag was added to fake the engine side; the removal/repair UI trigger + preserve/delete toggle belong to the companion-app story's view models (they also need that channel to be end-to-end).
- DOCS: `apple/GramDriveSupport/README.md` gains a removal/repair types table + an Uninstall cleanup subsection (PLAT-004/SEC-004): macOS does NOT unregister a provider's domains when the app is deleted, so clean uninstall is *remove accounts first, then delete the app*; leftovers are reconciled by reinstall + repair (no cleanup is possible once the app that embeds the extension is gone).
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8 (`.temp/TASK-260715-gnat2x/make-check-01.log`); `swift build` all targets clean; `swift test` 136/136 in 29 suites (17 new across 3 suites — disposition→mode mapping, targeted-removal idempotence/disposition, repair re-registration/stray-cleanup/idempotence/interruption-mid-add/interruption-mid-stray-removal + `run()` shared-state paths). Results: `TASK-260715-gnat2x_results.md`. Nothing committed (no-auto-commit policy).

### 1600 — File Provider domain registration implemented (TASK-260715-3s44pc → to-review)
- MILESTONE: the File Provider domain layer exists — `GramDriveFileProvider`, a new library of `apple/GramDriveSupport` (PLAT-MAC-001): stable per-account domain identity, idempotent reconciler over a registrar seam, thin `NSFileProviderReplicatedExtension` skeleton wired to shared state, launch-time reconcile in the companion shell.
- DECISION (domain identity = pure function of account id only): identifier `account-<id>` — never display name, auth state, or namespace epoch — so reauthorization and namespace bumps can never move or recreate a domain (Finder state is keyed to the identifier). Parse-back is strict by round-trip (`account-007` refused), so a foreign/corrupted domain identifier can never alias a real account. Naming per POL-7: one account → exactly "GramDrive"; several → "GramDrive — <name>", collisions append the id; blank names fall back to "Account <id>". Deterministic and total.
- DECISION (registrar seam has NO remove operation): registration converges toward the desired set touching only what differs (adds + display-name renames via the platform's upserting `add`); registered domains no account explains are *reported* as strays, never touched. Removal/repair is TASK-260715-gnat2x's; a seam without `remove` makes "registration never destroys Finder state" structural rather than disciplined. Duplicate install and restart are the same proof: re-running the pass plans nothing.
- CHANGE (FFI contract 0.2.0 → 0.3.0, additive): `SharedStateStore.accounts()` / `account(account_id)` snapshot reads — identity, source kind, display name, auth state, namespace epoch, and the account root's item id (derived in Rust via `ItemKey::Canonical(CanonicalKey::Account(..))`, so hosts never learn the identifier scheme). Never secret material (`secret_ref` stays behind the boundary). No writes added — DEC-006's no-writes-over-FFI rule intact; both bindings-smoke consumers updated to assert 0.3.0.
- DECISION (extension skeleton refuses honestly, wired for real): `accountContext()` = parse domain id → open shared state as `.provider` → resolve account + root item id; callbacks answer `NSFileProviderError(.noSuchItem)` when the domain maps to no configured account, `CocoaError(.featureUnsupported)` for a resolvable domain until enumeration/content stories land, storage failures pass through untranslated. No TDLib by construction: the target links only GramDriveSupport + core bindings (DEC-006).
- FINDING (platform constraint, documented not forced): `NSFileProviderManager` domain management resolves the extension from the calling app's bundle → the launch-time reconcile lives in the companion shell (the app that will embed the .appex), off-main, never failing startup; live proof belongs to signing/packaging (TASK-260715-1dk9ik). `SystemDomainRegistrar` is the only untested-by-unit surface, everything above the seam is tested against fakes.
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8 (provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`); `cargo test -p gramdrive-ffi` 26 green (3 new account-read tests); `swift test` 118/118 in 25 suites (28 new across 5 domain suites); `make smoke-bindings` PASSED (0.3.0 asserted Swift+Kotlin); `make smoke-shared-state` PASSED with new `domains` step (separate provider process: seeded account → domain `account-7`/"GramDrive" → extension type resolves the same root item the Rust seeder printed); `make smoke-agent-lifecycle` PASSED (regression). Results: `TASK-260715-3s44pc_results.md`. Nothing committed (no-auto-commit policy).

### 1150 — macOS companion agent lifecycle implemented (TASK-260715-1yx9ly → to-review)
- MILESTONE: the engine-hosting coordinator process exists — `GramDriveAgentCore` library + `gramdrive-agent` executable as new products of `apple/GramDriveSupport` (PLAT-MAC-002/-005). Lifecycle `launching → recovering → running → draining → stopped`; startup order lock → shared state (coordinator, quarantine+retry on corruption) → DriveCore → health endpoint → power observation; SIGTERM/SIGINT → drain → exit 0 (what launchd sends on unload/logout/update).
- DECISION (health IPC = UNIX socket, not XPC mach service): mach registration needs the signed bundled launchd plist — unprovable in tests/smoke; socket lives in the App Group container (exactly the granted surface). Bounded hard: one endpoint, zero request vocabulary (connect → one JSON snapshot → EOF), 1 MiB cap, timeouts. Transport swappable later without touching payload/lifecycle. `Sources/GramDriveAgentCore/HealthChannel.swift`.
- FINDING (sun_path trap): group-container socket paths exceed `sockaddr_un`'s 103-byte budget → direct bind/connect fails. Fixed with serialized `chdir`-relative bind/connect (cwd restored via held fd), tested with a beyond-limit path. `UnixSocketAddress.swift`. Relevant to any future UDS in the container.
- DECISION (single instance via flock): kernel drops a dead holder's lock with its last descriptor — crash recovery needs no stale-lock heuristics, and a dead agent can never lock out a successor. Proven in smoke: SIGKILL → successor healthy immediately.
- DECISION (launch-at-login split): `SMAppService.agent(plistName:)` resolves against the *caller's* bundle → registration is app-shell-side by platform constraint; `LaunchAtLoginPolicy.reconcile` (idempotent, approval surfaced never retried) + `SMAppServiceAgentLoginItem` land here for TASK-260715-13pxnu to call. Agent honors preference by reporting it (health), never self-registers; preference default OFF (user opt-in). Plist name `com.reluxworks.gramdrive.agent.plist` per DEC-019 derivation — packaging story should add the agent row to the identifier table.
- FINDING (Swift 6 trap): `NSLock.lock()` is unavailable in async contexts — locked sections of async paths (drain, shutdown) must live in sync helper funcs. Also `#expect(try …)` inside throwing closures fails to compile in this toolchain (works in plain throwing funcs) — assign first, then expect.
- FIX (drain semantics): `TransferRegistry` — admission refused once draining; grace period → cancel survivors through their FFI `CancellationToken` → bounded wait; `abandoned` counted and reported, never hidden. Ledger is process-local by design (durable transfer state is the engine's) → crash cannot duplicate work from the agent side.
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8 (provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`); `swift test` 50/50 (39 new); `make smoke-agent-lifecycle` PASSED (health/pid/pending over socket, second agent exit 2, SIGTERM drain cancelled=1 + socket removed, SIGKILL→successor healthy); `make smoke-shared-state` PASSED (regression after Package.swift change). Results: `TASK-260715-1yx9ly_results.md`. Nothing committed. Real wall clock 0955 +04 at write; placed on top as newest per parallel-session skew convention.

### 1110 — Apple shared durable state coordination implemented (TASK-260715-gnsa2s → to-review)
- MILESTONE: first Apple-native product source in the repo — `apple/GramDriveSupport` Swift package (App Group `262RZ595FP.com.reluxworks.gramdrive` resolution per DEC-019, data root `Library/Application Support/GramDrive` inside the container, role-based open, Darwin-notify change doorbell). FFI contract bumped 0.1.0 → 0.2.0 (additive): `shared_state.rs` — `shared_state_layout`, `SharedStateStore` (Coordinator/Provider roles, snapshot reads `item`/`children`/`child_by_name`, `data_version`, `schema_version`), `quarantine_corrupt_state`.
- DECISION (no writes over FFI): durable state is written by the engine in its host process; foreign processes only read. A write surface at the boundary would invite the extension to mutate engine-owned state (DEC-006 thin extension). The smoke's writer is a Rust process (`gramdrive-ffi/examples/shared_state_seed.rs`) — the product's real write shape, not a smoke-only API.
- DECISION (coordinator-only recovery, detection ≠ destruction): `gramdrive-state/src/recovery.rs` — probe (`PRAGMA quick_check`, corrupt only on `SQLITE_CORRUPT`/`SQLITE_NOTADB`) is separate from quarantine, which re-probes and declines healthy files; only the Coordinator role may quarantine (two concurrent recoverers could quarantine each other's fresh files). Move order shm→wal→db-last so a crash mid-quarantine leaves a re-probeable file and never a stale `-wal` beside a fresh database.
- DECISION (cache placement): managed content cache lives under the data root (`<root>/cache`), NOT `Library/Caches` — the engine's quota accounting owns that space and a system cache purge would invalidate it behind the accounting's back.
- FINDING (change signal): `PRAGMA data_version` is connection-relative — moves exactly on foreign commits, meaningless across handles/processes; exposed as the probe the Darwin doorbell pairs with (doorbell = advisory "check now", database = truth). Proven cross-process by the smoke watcher: doorbell + moved data_version + re-read across a Rust foreign commit.
- FINDING (test traps hit): Darwin notification names are host-global → parallel Swift tests observing one name hear each other (confirmation counted 2 for 1 post); fixed with an internal name-scoped seam + unique names per test. Swift stdio block-buffers pipes → `setbuf(stdout, nil)` needed before the harness could see `WATCH-READY`. Both worth remembering for the agent/extension tasks.
- FIX (AC evidence, real processes): `gramdrive-state/tests/multiprocess.rs` re-execs the test binary — 3 writer processes × 25 SYNC-022 batches + 75 serialized counter bumps with a live observer holding cursor-behind-state; crash writer SIGKILLed mid-stream ×3 rounds, after every kill `quick_check` ok and messages == exactly the cursor-sealed batches. Plus `tests/recovery.rs` (7 tests), FFI unit tests (10 new), Swift Testing suite (11), and `make smoke-shared-state` (2 concurrent Swift readers byte-identical through the packaged XCFramework).
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8 green (provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`); `make smoke-bindings` PASSED (contract 0.2.0 asserted in both consumers); `make smoke-shared-state` PASSED. TASK-260715-gnsa2s → to-review.

### 0945 — Metadata-first local backfill scheduler implemented (TASK-260715-mua1ng → to-review)
- FIX (engine): new `crates/gramdrive-engine/src/backfill/` — `BackfillScheduler` (sans-IO, clock-free, `now_ms` threaded like the transfer machine) + pure account-global pacer (`pace.rs`). `plan_next` yields one history action/call ordered Visible>Requested>Background (never media); `media_policy` is the separate Archive-Mode eager-media gate; `note_dispatch`/`note_flood_wait`/`set_paused`/`observe` are the durable mutations/read. Provider-neutral: no `Value`/`TdError`/`CrawlMachine`.
- FIX (state): new `backfill_control` table (per-scope pause + `next_request_at_ms` spacer + `flood_wait_until_ms`; template = `change_cursors`) + `repo/backfill.rs` (`BackfillControlRecord`, `backfill_control`/`put_backfill_control`) + `query_plans` entry (PK point read) + `tests/repo_backfill.rs`.
- DECISION (metadata-first enforced two ways): `plan_next` structurally cannot return media; `media_policy` returns `Suspended{MetadataPending}` while `backfill_backlog` is non-empty. Quota-exemption honored by NOT consulting the cache quota — media gates only on physical disk (`DiskState::Low/Critical`) + device conditions.
- DECISION (flood-wait budget): reuses the source machine's own per-request `attempt` (passed into `note_flood_wait`) rather than a durable fault counter — the durable row holds only deadlines + pause. A later short flood wait never shortens an earlier long one (`.max`).
- FINDING (durability proof): file-backed restart test (`pause_and_flood_wait_survive_a_restart`) reopens the same sqlite file and confirms a reopen resumes neither paused work nor a violated flood wait (POL-8 account safety).
- SCOPE: scheduler decides only — it does not enumerate media attachments, enqueue transfers, or drive `CrawlMachine`; that host/FFI wiring is a later thin seam. Boundary-clean (no `cfg(target_os)`; engine arch check green).
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8 green (provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`). New tests: 5 pace unit + 17 engine integration + 4 state integration, all green. TASK-260715-mua1ng → to-review.

### 0900 — Metadata-first local backfill scheduler: design (TASK-260715-mua1ng, development)
- FINDING: no scheduler/pacer/backfill-loop exists in `crates/` yet. tdjson provides sans-IO machines (`CrawlMachine`/`LiveMachine`/`SnapshotMachine`/`UpdateMachine`/`FolderCatalogMachine`) that emit `Backoff{retry_after_secs, attempt}` obligations and expect a composing caller to own the clock, pacing, and flood-wait budget. This task IS that missing composing policy.
- DECISION (placement = engine, provider-neutral): tdjson `Cargo.toml` states "product code never links state from here — the composing caller (engine/ffi) owns that wiring." Per the established "engine owns retry/scheduling policy; sources only classify" split (see `transfer/retry.rs`), the scheduler is provider-neutral policy in `gramdrive-engine` (deps: model/source/state/render; no `Value`/`TdError`/`CrawlMachine`). tdjson glue that maps neutral actions → `CrawlMachine::set_priority` is later host/ffi wiring, out of this task's product scope.
- DECISION (durable substrate): new per-account-scope `backfill_control` table in `gramdrive-state` (template = `change_cursors`): `paused`, `next_request_at_ms` (account-global request spacer), `flood_wait_until_ms` (durable flood-wait deadline — restart safety is account-critical, POL-8). Flood-wait budget cap uses the machine's own per-request `attempt` (passed in), not a durable counter. Per-chat history progress + backlog ordering already durable via existing `chat_sync_state` / `backfill_backlog`.
- DECISION (metadata-first + no eager mobile media): `plan_next` returns ONLY history actions (`AdvanceHistory{chat, priority}`) / `Wait` / `Paused` / `Idle` — never media. Visible > Requested > Background priority; Background gated off metered/power-saving; Visible/Requested (foreground) proceed even when constrained. Eager media is a SEPARATE `media_policy` gate (Eager vs Suspended{reason}) that suspends while any metadata backlog remains (enforces metadata-first), on low/critical disk (POL-2 warning), when metered/offline/power-saving, or when account Archive-Mode off. Quota-exemption honored by NOT consulting the cache quota (only physical disk).
- SCOPE (planned): `gramdrive-state` — `src/schema/v1.sql` (+`backfill_control`), `src/repo/backfill.rs` (new), `repo/mod.rs`, `tests/query_plans.rs`, `tests/repo_backfill.rs`. `gramdrive-engine` — `src/backfill/` (new module: pacer + scheduler), `src/lib.rs`, `tests/backfill_scheduler.rs`. All sans-IO/deterministic, `now_ms` threaded like the transfer machine.

### 0806 — REVIEW: TDLib thumbnail/preview source ACCEPTED (TASK-260715-3nl3mu → done)
- VERDICT: accepted. All AC clauses (bounded / correctly typed / restriction-aware / never force full media) verified against code and independently re-run gates: `make check` 8/8 (fresh run, provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`), 32 lib + 12 integration + 2 conformance thumbnail cases green, clippy `-D warnings` clean.
- FINDING (never full media, AC): the download only ever carries the preview `file_id` (701), never the media `file_id` (700) — `serving_responder` in `tests/thumbnail_source.rs` asserts this on every request, and `max_preview_bytes` is an independent post-response backstop. Verified real, not a test-only claim.
- FINDING (POL-4 defense-in-depth): restriction refusal holds at TWO independent layers — the normalizer fails closed (`previews()`/`photo_attachment()` in `message.rs` force `(thumbnail, minithumbnail) = (None, None)` for non-fetchable attachments, with its own test), and `ThumbnailMachine`'s POL-4 gate refuses with `Restricted` before any network. The authoritative conformance "every door" case (`shape.rs`) now runs the REAL adapter (was the embedded fake) and certifies zero-request refusal.
- FINDING (placeholder is downstream, not the source's job): POL-4's "unavailable placeholder" is rendered by the presentation layer from a `Restricted` error; the source MUST refuse (the conformance suite forbids "answered or reported missing"). Returning `Restricted` is the correct source-layer behavior — confirmed against `DriveSource::thumbnail`'s doc contract and `shape.rs:216-226`.
- OBSERVATION (non-blocking): `inline_covers` serves the blurred minithumbnail directly for boxes ≤ its declared dims (Telegram caps minithumbnails ~40px) — a bounded POL-2 round-trip saving; a minor blur-vs-sharp tradeoff only at very small boxes, defensible. No in-adapter `getMessage` refresh for previews (surfaces `StaleReference` instead) is a documented, correct scope boundary.
- SCOPE: read-only review; no product code modified. Verdict evidence attached (`TASK-260715-3nl3mu_review.md`). TASK-260715-3nl3mu → done.

### 0742 — TDLib thumbnail/preview source implemented (TASK-260715-3nl3mu → to-review)
- FIX: new `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/thumbnail.rs` — the `DriveSource::thumbnail` side of the tdjson source: sans-IO `ThumbnailMachine` (POL-4 gate, preview selection, base64 decode, download request, response validation, byte cap) + thin `TdThumbnailer` driver (`thumbnail()` has the trait method's exact signature). New `ThumbnailCatalog` seam: ItemId → `Option<ThumbnailTarget{availability, downloadable, inline}}>`; `ThumbnailTarget::from_descriptor` is the single projection from a normalized `AttachmentDescriptor`.
- DECISION (never full media, POL-2 "distinct from full-content hydration"): serves the TDLib *thumbnail* file_id (photo's smallest stored size, or a video/document `thumbnail` member) — a different file_id than the media — via whole-file synchronous `downloadFile{offset:0, limit:0, synchronous:true}`. Inline minithumbnail is a zero-network fallback, and is served directly when the requested `ThumbnailSpec` box is small enough that the tiny blur already fills it (`inline_covers`). Integration test asserts the download carries the preview id (701), never the media id (700).
- DECISION (bounded, AC): `ThumbnailConfig::max_preview_bytes` (default 4 MiB) caps twice — a known oversize preview is skipped before any request (falls back to inline/None), and a download response reporting a size past the cap is refused (`Internal`), so a mis-projected file_id can never become a full-media download.
- DECISION (base64 in-crate): minithumbnail `data` is standard base64; `message.rs` deliberately kept it a string to stay dependency-free. `decode_base64` is a ~30-line in-crate RFC-4648 decoder (padded+unpadded) — "free of a base64 dependency" honored as no external crate, not no decoding.
- DECISION (reference refresh — deliberate scope boundary): unlike `download.rs`, this adapter does NOT run an in-adapter `getMessage` refresh. A thumbnail is a secondary best-effort preview whose reference rides on the owning message's own refresh path; a stale preview reference surfaces as `StaleReference` (retryable-after-refresh) and the caller re-resolves via the catalog. Keeps the refresh protocol in one place. Not required by the AC or the required test surface.
- DECISION (shared helpers): made `download.rs` `FileLocks`/`CancelGuard`/`read_exact_at`/`classify_runtime_error`/`is_stale_reference` `pub(crate)` and reused them — per-file serialization and cancel-on-abandon behave identically across both adapters, no duplication.
- FIX (conformance): `tests/fetch_conformance.rs` now routes `thumbnail()` through the real `TdThumbnailer` (was the embedded fake), so the POL-4 "every door" case (shape.rs `restricted.is-refused-through-every-door`) certifies the real adapter — refused with `Restricted`, zero requests. Suite runs whole with zero skips.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/thumbnail.rs` (new), `src/lib.rs` (module + exports + docs), `src/download.rs` (pub(crate) helpers), `tests/thumbnail_source.rs` (new, 12 cases: photo/video/document classes, restricted, view-once, missing→None, unknown-item→None, inline fallback, flood, stale-ref, cancel, serialization), `tests/fetch_conformance.rs`, crate `README.md`.
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8 green (provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`); 44 lib+integration thumbnail cases pass. TASK-260715-3nl3mu → to-review.

- VERDICT: accepted. All four AC verified against code + independently re-run gates: `make check` 8/8 (fresh run, provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`) and `make tdjson-smoke` green against the staged artifact.
- FINDING (reviewer verification): claims cross-checked against `.spec/sync-and-filesystem-semantics.md` SYNC-040..046 and POL-4 — all hold. `TdDownloader::fetch` signature match proven by compilation: `RangedTdjsonSource::fetch` in `tests/fetch_conformance.rs:119` delegates to it verbatim as a `DriveSource` impl. POL-4 zero-request pin is real (`tests/file_download.rs:341` runs with no responder — any network call panics the mock). Conformance ran with zero skips; harness honestly names its fake-enumeration split.
