### 0709 — TDLib ranged download adapter implemented (TASK-260715-1onbmf → to-review)
- FIX: new `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/download.rs` — the `DriveSource::fetch` side of the tdjson source: sans-IO `DownloadMachine` (gates, protocol, classification, delivery geometry) + `TdDownloader` driver (`fetch()` has `DriveSource::fetch`'s exact signature, for the future full adapter to delegate to). New `FetchCatalog` seam: ItemId → `FileTarget{file_id, chat/message, availability, size, version}`; the state layer's projection supplies it at composition.
- DECISION (protocol): synchronous `downloadFile{offset, limit, priority, synchronous:true}` per fetch — the engine already grids fetches to 512 KiB chunks (SYNC-041), so per-range sync download is the right grain and `updateFile` push updates are deliberately not consumed (delivery itself is the progress signal per SYNC-046; `progress()` mirrors accounting for NFR-033). Bytes then read directly from `file.local.path` in bounded slices (default 256 KiB, no whole-file buffering) — the documented TDLib streaming pattern; `readFilePart` avoided (would need a base64 dep, crate deliberately has none).
- DECISION (per-file lock): TDLib keeps ONE download conversation per file — a second `downloadFile` with different offset/limit displaces a synchronous wait. Concurrent fetches of one file serialize on an internal async per-file lock; mock-only tests would never catch this, so it is load-bearing for the real backend.
- DECISION (temp-file ownership): TDLib owns everything under its files directory; adapter opens read-only, never moves/renames/deletes (deletion = `deleteFile` request, cache policy's job). Handoff to the engine is bytes into the sink, never a path.
- DECISION (refresh protocol): `FILE_REFERENCE_*` rejection → `getMessage` refresh (TDLib re-learns the reference for the same file_id) → verify refreshed attachment still names pinned content (`remote_unique_id` drift ⇒ VersionConflict; restricted ⇒ Restricted; gone ⇒ NotFound) → surface `StaleReference`. No in-call retry: the conformance contract expects call #1 to fail StaleReference and the caller's retry to succeed (SYNC-045; identity never moves).
- DECISION (version verification): pin checked at the gate AND re-resolved before every delivered slice (`observe_entry`) — TDLib file_ids name immutable content, so the re-check closes the catalog-level race; conformance `losing-a-race-never-completes` runs against it for real.
- FIX (cancellation): drop-at-await + a self-waking yield per delivered slice; a `CancelGuard` fires `cancelDownloadFile{only_if_pending:false}` when a fetch is abandoned mid-download (SYNC-043 network half). Sink `Stop` ⇒ `Cancelled`.
- SCOPE: `download.rs` (+35 unit tests), `tests/file_download.rs` (16 driver tests over runtime+mock, incl. race cadence + abandon-fires-cancel), `tests/fetch_conformance.rs` — the SYNC-002 conformance suite runs whole: fetch through the real adapter over mock tdjson + real temp files, enumeration via the testkit fake (its conformance testkit already proves); zero skips. `Cargo.toml` gains `gramdrive-source` (allow-listed) + dev `gramdrive-testkit`.
- STATUS: to-review. `make check` 8/8 green after the unrelated BUG-260718-17hzcx lottery hit (entry below).

### 0705 — Latent sanitize() idempotence bug found by proptest lottery (BUG-260718-17hzcx)
- REGRESSION: `naming_properties::sanitize_is_idempotent` (gramdrive-model) failed during TASK-260715-1onbmf `make check` on a fresh random proptest seed — unrelated to the task's diff (naming untouched; code predates it, b8d9b2b).
- ROOT CAUSE: `prepare()` decides the extension split on the *untruncated* name; `compose()` then truncates the stem and unconditionally trims trailing dots. A tail 1 byte over `MAX_EXTENSION_BYTES` (17) is not split on pass 1, truncation shortens it, pass 2 *does* split it, and `trim_edges` eats the stem's now-trailing dot: `…CP..😀…` → `…CP.😀…`. `sanitize(sanitize(x)) != sanitize(x)` (`naming.rs` `compose`/`split_extension`/`trim_edges`).
- FINDING: deterministic repro = proptest regression `cc ce9a3f44…` (full seed + shrunken input recorded in BUG-260718-17hzcx). The local regressions-file append was deliberately NOT committed with the task, to keep the diff scoped and the shared gate green; whoever fixes the bug should commit the entry alongside the fix.
- STATUS: pending — BUG-260718-17hzcx (under STORY-260715-3qxar5) carries the analysis and fix directions (fixpoint loop vs truncation-stable split decision).

### 0700 — REVIEW: POL-3 edit/delete retention mapping ACCEPTED (TASK-260715-37nhe5 → done)
- VERDICT: accepted. All three AC verified against code + independently re-run gates. (1) Fixtures deterministic (8/8 `repo_retention` green, in-memory + explicit timestamps). (2) Privacy honored — Mirror purge uses the schema's single sanctioned UPDATE (`payload`+`payload_schema` → NULL together, all other cols untouched), satisfying the `message_events_append_only` trigger exactly; rows never deleted so `event_seq` watermarks never rewind. (3) Cache eviction separate — `set_retention_mode` touches only `accounts`/`message_events`/`render_state`.
- FINDING (reviewer verification): traced `apply_revision` replay/stale-edit logic — it compares only against the *current* revision payload, which is retained in both modes, so purging prior revisions cannot break SYNC-021 idempotence (pinned by `mirror_edit_chain` stale re-observation). Audit→Mirror sweep keeps exactly `latest_event_seq` of live (`is_deleted=0`) messages; `purged_events=2` arithmetically correct. Render invalidation correctly scopes to `kind='generated_doc'` (excludes `order_doc`, which carries no retention header).
- NOTE (non-blocking, forward dependency): Mirror live-purge produces NULL-payload event rows; no production reader of `events_after` exists yet and the event API already models payload as `Option` ("absent for purged events"), so a future render-input builder must tolerate superseded NULL payloads on full re-render. `set_retention_mode`/`upsert_account` have no production callers yet (repo scaffolding ahead of engine wiring).
- GATES (reviewer re-run): `make check-core` 6/6 + `make check-repo` 2/2 green; full workspace suite green, no regressions.

### 0626 — Attachment metadata + capability mapping implemented (TASK-260715-23arcu → to-review)
- FINDING: message normalization (TASK-260715-1ynmct, done) already produced `AttachmentDescriptor` with kind/locators/mime/size/dims/availability. The real gaps for this task were: thumbnail descriptor (absent), deterministic safe filename (absent), and identity binding + album provenance (deferred to "the consumer"). This task fills all three.
- FIX: `message.rs` — new `ThumbnailDescriptor` (downloadable preview: format, dims, file locator) + `Minithumbnail` (inline blurred JPEG, base64 as tdjson delivers — kept dep-free, no base64 crate) on `AttachmentDescriptor`. Extracted per kind: `thumbnail`/`minithumbnail` for video/animation/document/video_note/sticker, `album_cover_thumbnail`/`album_cover_minithumbnail` for audio, smallest `photoSize` for photos (single-size photo → no distinct thumbnail), none for voice notes. `ThumbnailFormat` degrades unknown `@type` to `Unknown` (same degrade-don't-omit rule as the rest of the normalizer).
- FIX: new `attachment.rs` mapping module — `MappedAttachment` binds the stable `AttachmentKey` identity (account scope + chat + message + ordinal 0), the deterministic safe name (`gramdrive_model::naming::sanitize(File)`), album_id provenance, and verbatim `can_be_saved`. `map_message_attachments(record, scope)` + `map_attachment(...)`. Nameless media gets a stable kind+MIME default (`photo.jpg`, `voice.ogg`, sticker webp/tgs/webm by MIME); sibling collisions are the media dir's job (`resolve_siblings`), not settled here.
- DECISION (POL-4 fail-closed): thumbnails AND minithumbnails are captured ONLY for `Fetchable` attachments. Restricted (`can_be_saved=false`) and ViewOnce (self-destruct/secret) carry no preview bytes or locators at all — a non-fetchable attachment is a pure placeholder. This is conservative (withholds data, never adds ToS risk), so it does not trip the POL-8 escalation. Note for TASK-260715-3nl3mu (thumbnail-source, blocked by this): Telegram's own clients DO show minithumbnails for protected content; relaxing this to expose restricted-content minithumbnails would be an owner decision (DEC row), not a code call.
- DECISION: v1 = at most one attachment per message, ordinal 0. Telegram albums are many one-attachment messages sharing `media_album_id`; distinct identities come from distinct message ids, shared `album_id` is the provenance. `map_message_attachments` returns a Vec (0/1) so future multi-attachment content needs no caller change.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/{message.rs,attachment.rs,lib.rs}`; new `tests/attachment_metadata.rs` (6 fixtures) + 7 new `message::tests` + 8 `attachment::tests`. Lives in source-tdjson (deps model+source per crates/README allow list); no new dependency, no README table change (data-mapping modules documented via rustdoc, per the `message` module's established convention).
- STATUS: to-review. `make check` 8/8 green (provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`).

### 0610 — POL-3 edit/delete retention mapping implemented (TASK-260715-37nhe5 → to-review)
- FINDING: the event-log plumbing (append-only `message_events`, `messages` projection, watermark render-invalidation) already existed and was retention-agnostic — it always kept every payload. Retention only affected *rendering* (gramdrive-render projects Mirror/Audit at render time). The gap POL-3 mandates for privacy is the *storage-level* content purge; schema even documented "the single sanctioned UPDATE is the Mirror-mode content purge" but nothing performed it.
- FIX: `apply_message_changes` (`repo/changes.rs`) now reads `accounts.retention_mode` once/batch and, in Mirror, purges superseded content via payload-NULL UPDATE — prior revisions on edit (keep current), all revisions on delete. Audit retains. New private `purge_message_content(chat, msg, keep)`. Event *rows* never removed → `event_seq` watermarks never rewind, replay stays recognizable.
- DECISION: the current revision of a live message keeps its payload in *both* modes — it is the projection join target and the SYNC-021 replay comparand. Only *superseded* content (older revisions, deleted messages) is purgeable. This is what makes idempotence survive the purge.
- FIX: new `WriteTxn::set_retention_mode` (`repo/accounts.rs`) is the sole mid-life mutator: Audit→Mirror runs a retroactive sweep (`event_seq NOT IN (latest_event_seq of live messages)`), marks *all* account generated docs dirty. `upsert_account` no longer flips `retention_mode` on conflict — a silent flip would leave purged-but-still-rendered content. Returns `RetentionChange{previous,current,purged_events,invalidated_docs}`.
- DECISION: a mode switch invalidates *every* account doc in *both* directions because the `retention_mode` token is written into each document header (`render/*/render.rs`), so any switch changes the bytes even where the message set is unchanged.
- DECISION: delete-for-everyone vs delete-for-me collapse to one observed `Deleted` — TDLib's `updateDeleteMessages` carries no revoke-scope flag (`live.rs` counts `is_permanent && !from_cache`); the archive mirrors this account's view where both are permanent. No separate path, no claim about which. Pinned by test.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/{changes,accounts,mod}.rs`; new `tests/repo_retention.rs` (8 tests). Cache eviction untouched (POL-2, separate axis).
- STATUS: to-review. `make check-core` 6/6 + `make check-repo` 2/2 green; all pre-existing state/engine/tdjson suites (apply under default Mirror) still green — none asserted prior-revision payload presence.

### 0535 — REVIEW: ordered live update loop ACCEPTED (TASK-260715-10p5zp → done)
- VERDICT: accepted. All three AC verified against the code and independently re-run gates: cursor advance rides only the commit whose records justify it (`live.rs` — `advance_newest` set solely on bridge connect / verified extension, applied in one txn by the caller); gaps recover before publication (Unverified→Bridging→Verified; pre-connection pages commit under unchanged cursor, pinned by `gaps_recover_before_the_cursor_is_published` with per-commit coverage invariant); duplicates idempotent (`duplicate_and_out_of_order_updates_are_idempotent` full-replay fixed point).
- FINDING (reviewer verification): bridge fold logic is a faithful mirror of the crawl's `Phase::CatchUp` (`history.rs:726-752`) incl. the connect-on-`page_oldest <= newest` rule and empty-page connect; `parse_bridge_page` (`live.rs:929`) duplicates `parse_entries` (`history.rs:805`) verbatim (~50 lines) rather than sharing — deliberate mirror, acceptable, but a future refactor could make the crawl's parser `pub(crate)` and parameterize the error prefix.
- FINDING (crash-boundary reasoning is sound): durable state mutates only at commit application, so the every-commit-boundary kill in `restart_at_every_commit_boundary_converges_exactly` covers every distinct durable checkpoint; intra-commit atomicity is the state layer's own tested contract (SYNC-022).
- NOTE: board notes claim 25 unit tests; actual count in `live::tests` is 24 — immaterial, all green. Independently re-ran `make check` (8/8, provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`) and targeted `live::`/`live_updates` suites (24 + 8 pass).
- STATUS: done. No code changes requested.

### 0528 — MILESTONE: ordered live message update loop with gap bridges (TASK-260715-10p5zp → to-review)
- MILESTONE: deterministic sans-IO `LiveMachine` in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` (`src/live.rs`) — TDLib message pushes (`updateNewMessage`/`updateMessageSendSucceeded`, edit signals, permanent `updateDeleteMessages`) fold into ordered per-chat `LiveCommit`s over `normalize_message`, each carrying the cursor advance it justifies (SYNC-022/023). `make check` 8/8 green; 25 unit tests + `tests/live_updates.rs` (8 integration suites incl. every-commit-boundary crash/replay, gap recovery, crawl/live interplay, runtime round-trip).
- DECISION (cursor advance is a MERGE instruction, not a window write): `LiveCommit.advance_newest` means "raise stored `chat_sync_state.newest` to at least this id"; the caller merges against the STORED row in the applying transaction, keeping stored `oldest`/`history_complete`. Symmetrically the crawl side merges min/max. This is what makes concurrent backfill + live safe: crawl moves oldest down while live moves newest up, and a crawl page committed after a live advance can never regress it. Pinned by `in_progress_backfill_and_live_updates_never_lose_state` (message arrives mid-backfill; final window [1,13], one event per message).
- DECISION (live loop NEVER establishes a window): a chat with no committed window gets boundary-free commits forever — records persist (idempotent, SYNC-021), cursor stays absent until a crawl Anchors it. Establishing `[N,N]` from a live message over an empty-complete chat would recreate the anchor-gap orphaning class (see 0450/0457): stale `history_complete=true` over a window that never covered the offline arrivals. Rows outside the window are safe by design; only the cursor claims contiguity.
- DECISION (gap bridge = the crawl's catch-up protocol, live-triggered): per-chat session boundary Unverified→Bridging→Verified/Frozen. First live message above committed `newest` opens a `getChatHistory` descent from 0 (same page-contract validation as the crawl, SYNC-003 at message granularity); pre-connection pages commit records under the UNCHANGED cursor; the connecting page's commit carries the advance to max(bridge top, live top). Crash mid-bridge → old cursor survives → next session re-bridges over idempotent replay. Failed bridge (chat-level TDLib rejection / page-contract lie) → `Frozen` + one `LiveStep::Degraded{UnavailableReason}`: records keep flowing boundary-free, the cursor never lies, the next crawl run owns recovery.
- DECISION (edits re-fetch, never merge partials): `updateMessageContent` (content, no edit time) and `updateMessageEdited` (edit time, no content) would be a torn write if merged; both collapse into one coalesced `getMessage` refresh → full renormalized snapshot → ordinary observation. The state layer classifies observed-vs-edited and guards stale rewind + post-deletion resurrection (already in `apply_message_changes`). Rejected refresh (message gone) → counted `refreshes_rejected`, never silent; the real deletion arrives as its own update.
- DECISION (`updateMessageInteractionInfo` deliberately NOT consumed v1): reaction/view tallies tick continuously; folding each into the append-only `message_events` log (POL-3) is pathological growth. Reactions refresh on any re-observation (edit refresh, history re-fetch). Documented boundary in module docs.
- FINDING (TDLib delete/send semantics the loop encodes): `updateDeleteMessages` counts only when `is_permanent && !from_cache` (from_cache = cache eviction, not deletion); a message still carrying `sending_state` has a PROVISIONAL id and is skipped outright — the final server-id message arrives via `updateMessageSendSucceeded` and ingests as an ordinary new message (no temp-id rows, no window corruption).
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/live.rs` (new), `tests/live_updates.rs` (new), `src/lib.rs` + crate `README.md` (module row/re-exports). Reuses `history::UnavailableReason` and `error::retryable_after`; no state-schema change. Untracked chats buffer in arrival order + report once (`LiveStep::Unresolved`) → caller upserts canonical row (FK) then `track_chat` replays.
- STATUS: to-review. Composing-caller contract: plan `LiveChat{chat_id, newest}` from `chat_sync_state`, feed `on_update` from the client's `UpdateStream`, drive `next_step`/`on_response`, persist each commit + merged cursor in one transaction, stamp `observed_at_ms` at commit time (SYNC-073).

### 0505 — REWORK REVIEW: anchor-gap fix ACCEPTED (TASK-260715-26dnp6 → done)
- VERDICT: accepted. Both rework items (verdict 0450) satisfied. Fix at `history.rs:722` is minimal and correct — `Phase::Anchor` runs only when `window == None`, so the empty-chat durable output (`{window: None, history_complete: true}`) is the sole plan shape reaching that branch with a stale flag; resetting to `false` there can never falsely clear a genuinely-complete windowed chat (those enter CatchUp). Regression pinned by the every-commit-boundary fixture flavor + a focused unit test.
- INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION (reviewer, no product-code change): reverted *only* the one-line fix in an isolated worktree (working-tree crate synced in) → both new tests FAIL, integration reproduces the exact orphaning `left [9,10,11,12,13]` vs `right [1..13]` at `stop_after=1` (the anchor commit boundary); fix restored → green. `make check` 8/8 green on the real checkout (114 unit + 8 integration crawl suites). Scope respected — no redesign, no existing assertion weakened.
- ARTIFACT: `TASK-260715-26dnp6_rework-review-verdict.md`.

### 0457 — REWORK: anchor fold resets history_complete — silent gap closed (TASK-260715-26dnp6 → to-review)
- FIX: `CrawlMachine::on_page` `Phase::Anchor` non-empty branch (`crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/history.rs:722`) now sets `chat.history_complete = false` when installing the fresh `[min,max]` window. A non-empty anchor page proves history exists below it, so completeness must be re-proven by an empty backward answer — exactly as for a never-crawled chat. One line, no redesign; everything else in the crawl accepted as-is (verdict 0450).
- REGRESSION PINNED: added `resume_of_a_grown_empty_complete_chat_resumes_exactly` to `tests/history_crawl.rs` — the empty-complete→active flavor of the every-commit-boundary interruption suite: seed `{window: None, history_complete: true}` (the machine's own empty-chat output, applied through the real commit path), grow the chat by >page_size messages during downtime, then kill/resume at every commit boundary and assert gap-free convergence (full id set, one event per message, window `[first,last]`, `history_complete` only after the empty answer). Also a focused unit test `anchor_over_a_carried_complete_flag_resets_it`.
- VERIFIED: reverting only the one-line fix makes both new tests fail — the integration test reproduces the review's exact orphaning (`stored ids [9,10,11,12,13]` vs expected `[1..13]`), confirming the fixture pins the class shut, not just the symptom. Restored → `make check` 8/8 green (format, clippy `-D warnings`, workspace tests incl. 16 unit + 8 integration crawl suites, architecture, supply-chain, traceability, scripts).
- STATUS: to-review. Artifacts: `TASK-260715-26dnp6_rework-results.md`.

### 0450 — REVIEW: anchor phase keeps stale history_complete — silent permanent gap (TASK-260715-26dnp6 → to-dev)
- FINDING (confirmed by standalone repro, no product-code change): `CrawlMachine::on_page` `Phase::Anchor` non-empty branch (`crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/history.rs:709`) installs the fresh `[min,max]` window but never resets `chat.history_complete` carried in from the plan.
- ROOT CAUSE: the machine's own durable output for an empty chat is `{window: None, history_complete: true}`; resuming that row re-enters Anchor with `history_complete=true`, so the first anchor commit over >page_size downtime arrivals persists a FALSE completion fact while older ids are unfetched.
- REGRESSION PATH: interrupt at exactly that commit boundary → next resume's catch-up connects → `history_complete` branch → `Phase::Complete`, backward never runs → older messages permanently orphaned, silently (no `Unavailable`, no error). Violates SYNC-021 / story DoD "no missing messages".
- FINDING (test-coverage hole): `restart_at_every_commit_boundary_resumes_exactly` only starts from a never-crawled chat; the downtime suite resumes a windowed chat; no fixture resumes the empty-complete flavor — the one plan shape where Anchor runs with `history_complete=true`.
- STATUS: to-dev. Requested fix: reset `history_complete=false` in the Anchor `Some` branch + add the empty-complete→active flavor to the every-commit-boundary interruption suite. Everything else accepted (verdict: board resource `TASK-260715-26dnp6_review-verdict.md`, repro `TASK-260715-26dnp6_anchor-gap-repro.rs`); reviewer re-ran `make check` 8/8 green.

### 0442 — MILESTONE: resumable per-chat history crawl (TASK-260715-26dnp6 → to-review)
- MILESTONE: deterministic sans-IO `CrawlMachine` in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` (`src/history.rs`) — resumable `getChatHistory` paging into `normalize_message` records with durable per-chat cursors and bounded priority scheduling (SYNC-021). `make check` 8/8 green; 15 unit tests + `tests/history_crawl.rs` (7 integration suites: every-commit-boundary interrupt/resume, downtime catch-up, flood wait, per-chat unavailability, priority boost, runtime round-trip). Only request type issued: `getChatHistory` (TGC-21); never media (SYNC-020).
- DECISION (no opaque resume token — `chat_sync_state` IS the cursor): unlike the snapshot's `ChangeCursor` token, history progress is structured per-chat state the state layer already owns: `[oldest, newest]` window + `history_complete` (`chat_sync_state`, schema v1). Every answered page → exactly one `HistoryCommit` carrying records + window facts; caller persists both in one `WriteTxn` (SYNC-022). Resume = read rows back into `CrawlPlan{ChatCrawl{window, history_complete}}`. SYNC-004 scoping is the row key's job.
- DECISION (three phases keep the window contiguous): Anchor (no window: one page from 0 → initial `[min,max]`), Catch-up (window exists: page from 0 down until an id ≤ committed `newest` connects, THEN newest jumps to the first page's top — unconnected catch-up pages commit records under the UNCHANGED window, so a crash mid-catch-up refetches them and idempotent replay skips; contiguity of the durable window is never broken), Backward (page from `oldest` until empty answer → `history_complete`). Catch-up is what hands the ordered update loop (TASK-260715-10p5zp) a reconnected newest boundary; the loop only extends it live.
- DECISION (per-chat blast radius): non-retryable TDLib rejections (left channel, CHANNEL_PRIVATE, chat gone) and page-contract violations (duplicate/ascending ids, id ≥ `from`, foreign chat_id — SYNC-003 at message granularity) fail ONE chat as explicit `CrawlStep::Unavailable{reason}`, never the run; the other N chats continue. Only runtime-level failures (ClientClosed/Shutdown/protocol) are machine-fatal. A malformed message OBJECT inside a sound page degrades: counted in `skipped_malformed`, its id still advances the cursor (no wedge, no silent drop).
- DECISION (scheduling = priority, then page-by-page round-robin): one outstanding request (SEC-031), re-pick after EVERY page: `CrawlPriority` (Visible > Requested > Background), then fewest pages served this run, then plan order. Equals round-robin per page → a huge history cannot starve the account; a Visible chat takes every page until done. `set_priority` takes effect at next pick (in-flight page never abandoned). Machine reads no clock — flood 429/500 arms `CrawlBackoff{retry_after_secs, attempt}` and re-issues the identical request; pacing/attempt caps compose at the caller seam (TGC-22).
- FINDING (`getChatHistory` paging contract the machine enforces): with `offset: 0`, answers carry ids strictly BELOW `from_message_id` (`0` = from the newest, inclusive), strictly descending; an empty answer = end of history in the paged direction; short pages are normal (TDLib chooses the count). Cross-page duplicates are impossible by construction (each backward page < committed oldest), which is what makes restart duplicate-free before the state layer's replay even engages.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/history.rs` (new), `tests/history_crawl.rs` (new), `src/error.rs` (`retryable_after` moved from snapshot — one flood classification for both machines), `src/snapshot.rs` (re-points), `src/lib.rs` + `README.md` (module docs/re-exports). No state-schema change (`chat_sync_state` + `record_chat_sync`/`apply_message_changes`/`backfill_backlog` already landed with STORY-260715-16ik2x).
- STATUS: to-review. Composing-caller contract: plan from `chat_sync_state` rows (+ `backfill_backlog` for ordering hints), drive next_step/on_response, persist each commit + `record_chat_sync` in one transaction, record `Unavailable` explicitly, own the pacing between steps.

### 0418 — MILESTONE: message normalization (TASK-260715-1ynmct → to-review)
- MILESTONE: pure sans-IO message normalizer in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` (`src/message.rs`) — one TDLib `message` object → one typed `MessageRecord` covering the PRD-022 fact list: identity, time (ms by boundary rule), sender, text/caption entities, reply target, topic, album key, reactions, service actions (19 modeled), POL-4 protection, attachment descriptors for all PRD-030 v1 classes. `make check` 8/8 green; 10 unit tests + 17-fixture PRD-022 corpus (`tests/message_normalization.rs`). Entry points sized for the follow-up tasks: `normalize_message` (crawl/`updateNewMessage`), `normalize_content` (`updateMessageContent`, takes explicit `ProtectionFacts`), `normalize_reactions` (`updateMessageInteractionInfo`).
- DECISION (degradation split — strict identity, degrading periphery, PRD-024): missing integer `id`/`chat_id`/`date`, no `content`, or typeless content → typed `MessageError::Malformed`, never a guessed record. Unknown content `@type` — or a known type with broken required members — → `MessageContent::Unsupported{raw_type, raw_json, raw_schema_version}`: the ONE place raw TDLib JSON is preserved (versioned raw preservation only where required for migration; sorted-key compact JSON → deterministic bytes; round-trip asserted). Unknown sender/reply/topic/reaction/self-destruct shapes → per-vocabulary `Unknown` variants; broken text entities dropped (text never lost), unknown entity types keep their span. Polls/locations/contacts etc. deliberately unmodeled v1 → `Unsupported` with raw kept, promotable later without data loss.
- DECISION (POL-4 availability is derived, never parsed): every `AttachmentDescriptor` carries `AttachmentAvailability` computed from `ProtectionFacts` — self-destructing (any flavor, unknown included — fail-closed) or content-level `is_secret` → `ViewOnce`; `can_be_saved == false` → `Restricted`; else `Fetchable`. `messageExpired*` → `MessageContent::Expired` (no attachment, no fabricated recoverability). Vocabulary mirrors state's `AttachmentFacts`/`AttachmentAvailability` (source-tdjson cannot link state; layer rule).
- FINDING (pinned TDLib 022d6020 message wire shape, verified against `td_api.tl`, not memory): `message` carries `topic_id:MessageTopic` (`messageTopicForum`/`DirectMessages`/`SavedMessages`) — there is NO `message_thread_id` field anymore; `reply_to:MessageReplyTo` is `messageReplyToMessage{chat_id(0=same), message_id, quote}`/`messageReplyToStory`; `media_album_id`/`custom_emoji_id` are int64 → decimal STRINGS on the wire. tdjson omits default-valued fields entirely, so an ABSENT `can_be_saved` is the wire shape of a protected message — normalizer defaults it `false`, which is both wire-correct and fail-closed.
- DECISION (payload serialization is NOT this task): `MessageRecord` is the intended `message_events.payload` value; encoding to bytes + `payload_schema` belongs to the event writer (TASK-260715-10p5zp). `RAW_SCHEMA_VERSION=1` versions only the Unsupported raw dialect.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/message.rs` (new), `tests/message_normalization.rs` (new), `src/wire.rs` (`parse_order` → `parse_int64`, it was always the general int64 decoder), `src/snapshot.rs`/`src/updates.rs` (call sites), `src/lib.rs` (module + re-exports). Unblocks TASK-260715-37nhe5 (edit/delete mapping) and TASK-260715-23arcu; the crawl (26dnp6) and update loop (10p5zp) compose it.
- STATUS: to-review.

### 0355 — MILESTONE: folder (chat filter) catalog machine (TASK-260715-54nopz → to-review)
- MILESTONE: deterministic sans-IO `FolderCatalogMachine` in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` — discovers the custom Telegram folders that populate "Telegram Folders/" from TDLib's `updateChatFolders` and folds them into a normalized folder create/rename/delete/reorder change stream (`FolderCatalogBatch`). Pure full-state reducer: no requests, no client (`on_update` → `take_batch`). `make check` 8/8 green; 12 unit tests (`src/folders.rs`) + `tests/folder_catalog.rs` (5 integration suites against the real store). Mock-only, artifact-free.
- DECISION (a folder is TWO facts — definition here, membership already done): folder *membership* (which chats, what order) is ordinary `chatListFolder` positions the snapshot/update machines already fold into `chat_list_entries` appearances. What was missing is the folder *definition* (id, title, tab position), carried by exactly one update — `updateChatFolders`, pushed on connect and on every change, always the COMPLETE ordered list. So the machine is a full-state diff reducer: replace the observed catalog wholesale on each push; `take_batch` diffs observed-vs-committed → created/renamed/reordered upserts + removed ids. Duplicate/replay → empty batch; restart re-push converges without churn.
- DECISION (POL-1 split mirrors the chat machine): a rename (title change) → `FolderInvalidation::Renamed`; a folder that only shifted tab position → `CatalogOrdering` only (a reorder is content, never a rename — POL-1/SYNC-011); creations/deletions → `Created`/`Removed`, and any set/order change adds one `CatalogOrdering`. Deleting a folder in the middle legitimately shifts survivors' positions → they are re-upserted for order (never renamed) — verified by `deletion_removes_the_folder_and_shifts_the_survivors` and its last-folder counterpart.
- DECISION (folder-definition SQL persistence deliberately OUT of scope — would be a forced fit): there is no table for folder names/order, and adding one means the FIRST real schema migration (bump `SCHEMA_VERSION`, add `MIGRATIONS` entry). The migration machinery's own tests are written around "MIGRATIONS empty, version == BASELINE" — e.g. `the_v1_fixture_opens_at_the_current_version` asserts `schema_history == vec![SCHEMA_VERSION]` (a single application). Forcing the migration here to store folder titles would break sibling state tests and steps on the metadata-state-store story (STORY-260715-16ik2x). The catalog machine emits typed folder definitions the composing caller/tree consumes; name/order persistence lands with the tree builder's own storage. `folders()` yields the ordered folder set for the `SnapshotPlan` (the snapshot machine snapshots the lists it is given and left discovery here).
- FINDING (folder deletion = clear appearances only, on existing state): `replace_chat_list(folder, &[])` already clears one folder's `chat_list_entries` without touching `chats` or other lists — no new state method needed. The integration suite drives both machines into a real `StateStore` and reads back: a chat in Main+two folders is ONE `chats` row with three appearances (`a_chat_in_two_folders_is_one_canonical_record_with_three_appearances`); deleting a folder removes only its appearances and both canonical chats survive, including one whose LAST folder appearance was the deleted folder (`deleting_a_folder_removes_appearances_only`).
- FINDING (TDLib folder title wire shape, pinned commit 022d6020): `updateChatFolders chat_folders:vector<chatFolderInfo>`; `chatFolderInfo id:int32 name:chatFolderName …`; `chatFolderName text:formattedText …`; so the title is `name.text.text`. Parser reads it across shapes (`name.text.text`, `name.text`, bare `name`, legacy `title`) for version tolerance; an entry without an int id fails safe (skipped, never guessed) but still consumes its tab index. `main_chat_list_position`/`are_tags_enabled` ignored — Main/Archive are separate top-level dirs, not catalog entries.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/folders.rs` (new), `src/lib.rs` + `README.md` (docs/re-exports), `tests/folder_catalog.rs` (new). No state-schema or state-repo change. Consumes the snapshot baseline (TASK-260715-30amrq) and the update mapper (TASK-260715-1c8fea).
- STATUS: to-review. Composing-caller contract: feed `updateChatFolders` → `on_update`; per checkpoint apply `FolderCatalogBatch` (upsert changed defs where the tree persists them; for each `removed`, `replace_chat_list(folder, &[])`); `folders()` feeds the `SnapshotPlan`.

### 0330 — MILESTONE: live chat-metadata/list update mapper (TASK-260715-1c8fea → to-review)
- MILESTONE: deterministic sans-IO `UpdateMachine` in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` — folds TDLib live push updates into the same normalized change stream the snapshot commits in, keeping the state baseline current (SYNC-026). Pure reducer: no requests, no client (`on_update` → `take_batch` → apply). `make check` 8/8 green; 14 new unit tests (`src/updates.rs`) + `tests/chat_updates.rs` (5 integration suites) + 1 state-repo test. Mock-only, artifact-free.
- DECISION (invalidation split from `order.json` shape — POL-1): `order.json` embeds the sanitized chat name and is keyed by `(list, schema_family)`, not by order (`gramdrive-model/README.md`). So a reorder is content-only → `Invalidation::ListOrdering` (regen order.json), never a rename and never a canonical-row rewrite; a rename (title/username of a known chat) is the ONLY folder-rename trigger → `Invalidation::FolderName`; first sight/avatar/protection → `Invalidation::Metadata`. This is exactly "reorder → order.json regen only, rename → folder rename event."
- DECISION (no live resume token — durability is convergence, not a fake cursor): the live update stream has no server-side offset (as `loadChats` has none). Inventing a live `ChangeCursor` would be a token that only pretends to resume. Durability = idempotent convergence (last-write-wins + no-op coalescing + idempotent state upserts) plus snapshot re-baseline on a detected gap (SYNC-023). The "transactional checkpoint" is the atomic application of each `take_batch` (chats before memberships for the FK), not a separate offset.
- FINDING (avatar is not persisted anywhere): no photo/avatar column in `chats` and no chat-avatar concept in state/model/render (only message `MediaKind::Photo`). Modeled `ChatMetadata.photo` as an opaque token (TDLib `small.remote.unique_id`, fallback to the object's text form) that feeds only the caller's content-derived `metadata_version` — a changed avatar advances DOM-003 without a column, and an unchanged one (restart re-push) keeps the version, so no churn.
- DECISION (incremental list-entry writes added to state): `replace_chat_list` is whole-list; a partial in-memory model applying live deltas through it would silently WIPE a list. Added `WriteTxn::upsert_chat_list_entry` / `remove_chat_list_entry` (idempotent, `chat_list_entries → chats` FK enforced) so one chat's move touches only its row. Order stays POL-1-consistent because `chat_list` sorts `pinned DESC, sort_order DESC` at read time.
- FINDING (gaps are transient by TDLib contract, but reported anyway): TDLib pushes `updateNewChat` before any update about a chat, so an update naming an unknown chat is a contract violation. The machine drops the value (cannot forge a row; FK would reject the membership) and reports the chat in `UpdateBatch::unresolved`; caller `getChat`s it and feeds the full object back as `updateNewChat` (carries current title/avatar/positions → nothing lost). Secret/unknown chat types are remembered as excluded — never emitted, never mistaken for gaps.
- DECISION (shared wire parsers extracted): moved `parse_order`/`parse_list`/`active_username`/`parse_chat_kind`/`KindFact` from `snapshot.rs` into a new `pub(crate) src/wire.rs` — the string-int64 order parse is subtle and both machines must not drift. Snapshot's two moved unit tests travel with them; snapshot public API and its integration suite unchanged.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/updates.rs` (new), `src/wire.rs` (new, shared), `src/snapshot.rs` (re-points to wire), `src/lib.rs` + `README.md` (docs/re-exports), `tests/chat_updates.rs` (new); `crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/chats.rs` (2 incremental methods) + `tests/repo_snapshots.rs` (coverage). Consumes the snapshot baseline (TASK-260715-30amrq); unblocks TASK-260715-10p5zp / -rhcnhc.
- STATUS: to-review. Composing-caller contract: pump `UpdateStream` → `on_update`; per checkpoint apply `UpdateBatch` (chats upsert first, then membership set/remove) + advance a content-derived `metadata_version` only on real change, all in one `WriteTxn`; on `unresolved`, `getChat` and feed back or re-baseline.

### 0250 — MILESTONE: initial chat-list snapshot machine (TASK-260715-30amrq → to-review)
- MILESTONE: deterministic sans-IO `SnapshotMachine` in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` — the initial chat-list snapshot per SYNC-020 (metadata only, no history/media requests, ever). `make check` 8/8 green; 8 new unit tests + `tests/chat_snapshot.rs` (8 integration suites incl. 1800-chat synthetic fixture with interrupt/resume). Mock-only, artifact-free.
- FINDING (loadChats has no offset → resume is list-granular by API shape): TDLib owns the load position; the only durable progress unit the API supports is the completed list. Sub-list checkpoints would be an invented resume TDLib cannot honor. An interrupted list restarts from its beginning, served from TDLib's local DB (re-enumeration, not re-download). Resume token = versioned JSON of completed lists, persisted as a `ChangeCursor` payload under `SNAPSHOT_CURSOR_STREAM` in the same transaction as the commit's rows (SYNC-022; SYNC-004 scope rejection for free).
- DECISION (membership truth = position map, not the getChats order witness): position updates consumed after `getChats` answers are NEWER than the answer — treating the witness sequence as truth would spuriously fail whenever a chat's order bumps mid-snapshot (new message). Entries = chats with a live position, sorted (pinned desc, order desc, id desc) — TDLib's own sort pair and byte-for-byte the state `chat_list` read order. Witness contributes: duplicate detection (typed SYNC-003 failure), gap detection (listed chat unknown even after lazy `getChat` → typed `MissingPosition`), and the lazy-resolution work list. Explicit order-0 = "left the list" → excluded + counted, not a gap.
- FINDING (tdjson int64 wire shape): `chatPosition.order` is int64 → arrives as a decimal STRING in the C JSON interface (int53 ids arrive as numbers). Parsed exactly; a float round-trip would corrupt orders near the int64 ceiling and scramble lists (pinned by `multi_page_snapshot_recovers_ordering_from_string_int64_orders`).
- DECISION (per-list commits are typed outputs; state persists them, dev-dep only): layer-1 `gramdrive-source-tdjson` cannot depend on `gramdrive-state`. `ListCommit` (chat facts + ordered entries + resume token) is the typed directive the composing caller persists via `upsert_chat`/`replace_chat_list`/`put_cursor` in one `WriteTxn`. Architecture direction table binds `[dependencies]` only — `gramdrive-state` added as a dev-dependency so the integration suite proves the whole loop against the real store. Same layering pattern as removal's `SignalQuiesce`/`PurgeState`.
- DECISION (flood wait is advice, not failure — SYNC-044): 429/`FLOOD_WAIT` (delay parsed via shared `trailing_integer`, moved `auth.rs` → `error.rs`) and 500 arm one typed `Backoff{retry_after_secs, attempt}` step, then the identical request re-issues; machine never sleeps, caller owns time and retry caps. `loadChats` 404 = pagination terminator, not an error. Everything else = typed terminal `SnapshotError::Request`.
- DECISION (fail-safe exclusions): secret chats (POL-4 out of v1) and unknown chat types excluded from commits and counted (`excluded_secret`/`excluded_unsupported`) — state `ChatType` cannot represent them and a guessed row is worse than an honest count. Usernames captured opportunistically from `updateUser`/`updateSupergroup` TDLib pushes during load — zero per-chat fan-out; keeping them current is TASK-260715-1c8fea's job.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/snapshot.rs` (new), `src/error.rs` (shared `trailing_integer`), `src/auth.rs` (uses it), `src/lib.rs` + `README.md` (docs/re-exports), `Cargo.toml` (dev-dep `gramdrive-state`), `tests/chat_snapshot.rs` (new). Unblocks TASK-260715-1c8fea (metadata updates), TASK-260715-54nopz (folder sync), TASK-260715-26dnp6 (history crawl).
- STATUS: to-review. Wiring contract for the composing caller: pump `UpdateStream` → `on_update` before feeding each response; persist every `ListCommit` + its token atomically; on cursor scope/parse rejection, clear the cursor and re-baseline with a fresh full snapshot.

### 0304 — MILESTONE: account-removal workflow (TASK-260715-wjaux5 → to-review)
- MILESTONE: crash-resumable, idempotent account-removal workflow implementing the SEC-004 cleanup sequence. New `removal` module in `gramdrive-source-tdjson`; `make check` 8/8 green; new unit tests + `tests/account_removal.rs` (7 integration tests). Mock-only, artifact-free.
- DECISION (Telegram logout vs local-only is `RemovalMode`): `RevokeSession` → `logOut` (server-side authorization terminated; TDLib also deletes its own DB before closing); `LocalOnly` → `close` (server session left intact, only local state torn down — the offline / "forget the account" path). The session request is the ONLY per-mode difference; every stage after it is identical. AC "Telegram logout vs local-only clearly distinguished".
- DECISION (durable journal outside the account subtree): checkpoint lives at `root/.gramdrive-removal/account-<id>.json`, NOT inside `account-<id>/`, so `WipeDatabase` can't delete the record of its own progress. Atomic write (temp + fsync + rename, best-effort dir fsync); `finalize` removes it last → no trace. Enables crash-resume (`pending()`) and the concurrency guard.
- DECISION (effect-before-record + idempotent stages = crash-safe by construction): driver loop is `next_pending` → do effect → durably `complete`. A crash after effect/before record re-runs the idempotent effect on resume; after record, skips it. No window where a stage is neither redone nor skipped. Every stage idempotent (missing dir / absent key / already-closed client all = success).
- DECISION (layering: cross-crate stages are typed directives, not forced-fit deps): `gramdrive-source-tdjson` (layer 1) may depend only on model/source. Cancelling transfers/unregistering provider state (`gramdrive-engine`, layer 2) and purging state rows (`gramdrive-state`, composed layer 2/3) are above it. Rather than invert the dep direction or fake those crates, `SignalQuiesce`/`PurgeState` are typed `RemovalStep` directives the composing caller (engine/FFI) executes; the workflow only sequences + checkpoints them. Owned stages (session request, on-disk wipe, keychain revoke, journal) run directly.
- FINDING (concurrency fails safe via journal-backed guard): `AccountRemoval::guard_open` refuses (`RemovalError::InProgress`) while the journal exists, so a concurrent open never sees a half-wiped account; `begin` adopts an in-progress removal instead of racing a second. Integration test: reader thread sampling `guard_open` 200× across the destructive window is always refused. The journal target path is never absent mid-removal (rename-over is atomic), so the guard has no false-open window.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/removal.rs` (new), `src/removal/journal.rs` (new), `src/lib.rs` (mod + re-exports + docs), `README.md` (module table + section), `tests/account_removal.rs` (new). SEC-004. Consumes the on-disk wipe (`StorageLayout::wipe_account`) + keychain (`SecretStore::delete_account`) halves from TASK-260715-2odowl.
- STATUS: to-review. Integration contract for later wiring: account-open path must call `guard_open` before `AccountConfig::resolve` and run `pending()` on startup; engine/FFI supplies the `SignalQuiesce`/`PurgeState` effects.

### 0212 — MILESTONE: session-secret key lifecycle seam (TASK-260715-2odowl → to-review)
- MILESTONE: extended the `config` secret seam in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` with the full database-key lifecycle — creation, validated retrieval, rotation, logout deletion. `make check` 8/8 green; 47 lib unit tests + 6 new `tests/secret_storage.rs` integration tests, all green. Mock-only, artifact-free.
- DECISION (macOS Keychain impl stays native, not core): the checklist's "macOS Keychain impl" is the native-adapter's job, NOT a Rust `cfg(target_os="macos")` Security-framework impl. `crates/README.md` platform policy bans platform code + `core-foundation` in every core crate (fail-closed scan) and puts secure-store APIs in native layers; DEC-002 keeps provider entry points native. A core-crate keychain impl would fail the architecture gate and invert the layering. Not a forced fit — the intended boundary (matches the 2026-07-17 "keychain is a seam" decision). No Swift app exists in the workspace yet; `InMemorySecrets` is the reference the native adapter reproduces.
- FINDING (no plaintext fallback = reject empty key): TDLib reads an EMPTY `database_encryption_key` as "database not encrypted". So `DatabaseKey::from_stored` fails closed on wrong-length (incl. empty) or all-zero → new `SecretError::Corrupt`; `AccountConfig::resolve` propagates it and never fabricates a key. That is the concrete meaning of the AC's "missing/corrupt fails safely, no plaintext fallback".
- DECISION (SecretStore is `&self` + Send+Sync, sibling of SecretSource): write half of the seam (`put_database_key`, `delete_account`) mirrors the shared-mutable OS keychain and the eventual foreign-trait shape (`ProgressListener`). `InMemorySecrets` key map moved behind a poison-recovering `Mutex` so the core's `unwrap`/`expect` ban holds (`lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)`); dropped its `Clone` derive (unused).
- DECISION (rotation = tell TDLib first, persist on ok): `set_database_encryption_key_request` (`setDatabaseEncryptionKey`/`new_encryption_key`) submitted to the live client, then `put_database_key` only after `ok` — persisting first would strand the DB under a key the keychain dropped. Recovery on unreadable key = wipe on-disk + regenerate + re-auth, never fallback.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/config/secret.rs` (const, `from_entropy`/`from_stored`, `Corrupt`, `SecretStore`, `InMemorySecrets` store impl), `src/config.rs` (rotation builder), `src/config/storage.rs` + `src/lib.rs` (docs/re-exports), `tests/secret_storage.rs` (new). SEC-002/003 + SEC-004 keychain half. Unblocks TASK-260715-wjaux5 (account removal).
- STATUS: to-review.

Unit side pins the input-validity state table, full classification + advice tables, malformed-update typed errors with state unchanged, resume mid-flow, Debug redaction of code/password.

Implementer's logbook entry below stamped 0134, slightly ahead of wall clock at review time (0129) — cosmetic.

### 0134 — Authorization state machine lands (TASK-260715-51n6jb → to-review)
- MILESTONE: new `auth` module in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` — `AuthMachine`, deterministic sans-IO authorization flow: typed `AuthState`/`AuthInput`/`AuthRejection`/`RetryAdvice`/`AuthError`, phone→code→optional-2FA and QR→optional-2FA paths. `make check` 8/8; 13 new unit tests + 8 scripted integration flows (`tests/auth_flow.rs`) over real `TdRuntime` + mock, all deterministic (no sleeps/timing).
- DECISION (sans-IO, updates are the only state mover): machine holds no client, does no I/O; typed state advances ONLY on `updateAuthorizationState` — inputs validate against current state and yield exactly one request (or typed error), TDLib confirms progress. Consequence: retries need no special path (rejected code/password leaves state where TDLib says), and interrupted sign-in resumes from whatever state TDLib reports first. This is what makes every AC scenario a scripted deterministic test.
- DECISION (unknown states fail safe, product scope): v1 = existing personal account sign-in. Email gates (`WaitEmailAddress`/`WaitEmailCode`), registration, and any future TDLib state → typed `AuthState::Unsupported{td_type}`; all inputs but `Cancel` → typed `AuthError::UnsupportedState`; no panic, no wedge. First-class email support = new task extending the enums.
- DECISION (Cancel = local `close`, not logout): runtime already treats `authorizationStateClosed` as end-of-client. Server-side logout/revocation + storage wipe stay in account removal (TASK-260715-wjaux5, SEC-004). Clean ownership boundary, no premature logOut semantics.
- DECISION (rejection classification contract): matches Telegram's contractual uppercase identifiers (`PHONE_CODE_INVALID/EXPIRED`, `PASSWORD_HASH_INVALID`, …); TDLib code 500 read as transient Network → RetrySameInput (misread internal error costs one failed retry, never a wrong transition); flood-wait seconds parsed from both message shapes. `TdError::Shutdown/ClientClosed` → `SessionEnded`.
- DECISION (secrets): login code + 2FA password ride in existing `Secret` (redacted Debug, plaintext only via crate-private builder onto the wire — SEC-020). Phone number deliberately NOT wrapped: TDLib echoes it in clear in `code_info` and UI renders it; wrapping would feign protection.
- DECISION (machine owns the config answer): on `waitTdlibParameters` machine emits `TdlibConfig::startup_requests()` itself — plumbing the user never sees; the reason `AuthMachine` holds `TdlibConfig`.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/auth.rs` (new), `tests/auth_flow.rs` (new), `src/lib.rs` (+module, re-exports), crate `README.md` (auth section + module table).
- STATUS: to-review.

### 0102 — TDLib configuration + storage policy lands (TASK-260715-1hdnuy → to-review)
- MILESTONE: new `config` module in `gramdrive-source-tdjson` — per-account `TdlibConfig`: `setTdlibParameters`/`setOption`/`addProxy` request builders, on-disk isolation (`StorageLayout`) with a clean logout wipe, and the `SecretSource` keychain seam. `make check` 8/8; 25 lib unit tests (13 new in `config`) + 4 new integration fixtures in `tests/config.rs`, all green. Mock-only, artifact-free.
- DECISION (secret-free plan vs resolved config — load-bearing for "never logged"): `AccountConfig` is the secret-free plan (derives `Debug`); `AccountConfig::resolve(&SecretSource)` attaches `api_id`/`api_hash`/db-key → `TdlibConfig`, whose `Debug` is manual and redacts them. `Secret`/`DatabaseKey`/`ApiCredentials` redact in `Debug`; plaintext leaves only through crate-private `expose()`/`base64()`, used by exactly the request builders (→ TDLib wire, never a log). Log-scrub fixture asserts no sentinel (api_id incl.) reaches any `Debug` form, while the wire request MUST carry it. SEC-020/SEC-023.
- DECISION (keychain is a seam, not code in core): `SecretSource` trait resolves product creds (Keychain service `gramdrive-telegram`) + per-account db key at runtime (SEC-003). Native adapter implements it over the OS keychain; core ships only the trait + `InMemorySecrets` (tests/CI, env-injected). Keeps the platform ban intact — no `core-foundation`/Security in a core crate.
- DECISION (storage/memory policy, recorded evidence): `StoragePolicy::mirror` = file+chat-info+message DBs on (mirror needs TDLib's local DB as history source, `.spec/architecture.md` LocalTdlibSource; TDLib requires the lower two when message DB is on — `is_coherent()` states it), secret chats off. `MemoryOptions::minimal` = `use_storage_optimizer` OFF (GramDrive owns cache quota+LRU per POL-2; TDLib deleting unpromoted files would corrupt accounting), `ignore_background_updates` OFF (mirror correctness), `message_unload_delay`=60s, `disable_persistent_network_statistics` ON, `notification_group_count_max`=0. Memory rationale ties to td#2516/#2807 (same as the TDLib-in-extension exclusion).
- DECISION (db key base64): TDLib's JSON interface encodes `bytes` fields as base64 — `database_encryption_key` held as raw bytes, base64-encoded onto the wire; passing raw bytes as a string would key the DB off a different value. Tiny in-crate RFC-4648 encoder (7 canonical vectors pinned) rather than a new dep moving the POL-6 gate.
- DECISION (isolation = disjoint subtrees): `StorageLayout::account_dir` = `root/account-<id>`, injective over `AccountId` (i64, negatives valid); db + files dirs live inside it. `wipe_account` = idempotent `remove_dir_all` of exactly one account's subtree (on-disk half of SEC-004; keychain half is native). Upgrade fixture: version bump changes only `application_version`; every DB-identity field byte-identical → reopens same store.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/src/config.rs` + `config/{secret,storage}.rs`, `tests/config.rs`; `lib.rs` (+`pub mod config`, re-exports, module doc); `Cargo.toml` (+`gramdrive-model` — allow-listed dep, already in graph, no supply-chain change); crate `README.md` (config section, module table, deps).
- NOTE (downstream): option NAMES validated only for JSON shape here (runtime is mock); real-linkage validation of each `setOption` name is the `real_tdjson` smoke's job in a follow-up. `DriveSource` adapter, auth flow (sends `startup_requests()`), `TdError`→`SourceError` still pending in STORY-260715-3elo6l.
- STATUS: to-review.

### 0042 — Safe tdjson runtime lands: gramdrive-source-tdjson (TASK-260715-2ulon7 → to-review)
- MILESTONE: new workspace crate `gramdrive-source-tdjson` (the reserved local-TDLib-source name) — safe runtime over the modern tdjson interface: single receive-loop owner, `@extra` request correlation, typed error conversion, bounded per-client update queues, cancellation, deadlock-free drain-first shutdown. `make check` 8/8; 33 deterministic tests (no artifact needed); `make tdjson-smoke` green against the real staged `libtdjson.dylib` (correlated `getOption version`, clean close via `authorizationStateClosed`, shutdown).
- DECISION (env gate, NOT a cargo feature — load-bearing): lint/test gates run `--all-features`, so a `real-tdjson` feature would force TDLib linkage into exactly the runs that must stay artifact-free. `build.rs` enables `cfg(real_tdjson)` + link flags + rpath only when `GRAMDRIVE_TDLIB_ARTIFACT_DIR` is set. Rpath, not `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH` — macOS SIP strips `DYLD_*` across the make→sh→cargo chain. Documented in crate README + `crates/README.md` feature policy.
- DECISION (one receive owner in the type system): `TdReceiveApi::receive(&mut self)` + the runtime moves the receiver into its loop thread; real impl additionally single-claims the process receiver (`RealTdJson::claim`, atomic). Misuse of `td_receive`'s no-concurrency rule = compile error.
- DECISION (no tokio; hand-rolled slot + queue): response slot = oneshot with bounded blocking wait AND waker-correct `Future` polling (tokio oneshot lacks a timed blocking wait; no async runtime dep wanted). Update queue = bounded Mutex/Condvar queue whose `close()` is callable by a third party — the property std `sync_channel` lacks and shutdown needs: shutdown closes queues BEFORE joining the loop, waking a producer blocked on backpressure; that ordering is the deadlock fix, proven by a watchdog test.
- DECISION (backpressure over dropping): full update queue blocks the receive loop — TDLib update order is contractual; mid-stream drops not an option. Tradeoff: one slow consumer stalls the shared loop (v1 single-account, stated in docs). Everything absorbed (discarded/dropped/unroutable/malformed) is counted in `RuntimeStats` — what the cancellation tests assert on.
- FINDING (pointer safety by construction): the `TdSendApi`/`TdReceiveApi` seam traffics only in owned `String`s; real impl copies every C string before returning (`td_execute` answers are thread-local in TDLib, so a concurrent receive can't invalidate them). Miri can't run FFI — ownership justification lives in `src/real.rs` module docs per the AC's alternative; mock runs the identical runtime logic in every gate.
- FINDING (close is destroy): modern tdjson has no destroy call — `authorizationStateClosed` ends a client (pending → `ClientClosed`, stream ends after delivering the closed update, new requests rejected). Deprecated `td_json_client_*` stays link-proved, never called.
- SCOPE: new `crates/gramdrive-source-tdjson/` (src: api/runtime/error/envelope/slot/queue/mock/real + build.rs, 5 test suites); `Cargo.toml` (+workspace `serde_json` — already in graph via uniffi, build script already in deny [bans.build]); `.scripts/check_crate_architecture.py` + `crates/README.md` policy rows (same commit, per sync rule); `Makefile` (+`tdjson-smoke`); `README.md` tools rows (also backfilled the missing `build_tdlib.py` row — omission from TASK-260715-rxjkpi); stale "reserved crate" wording in `.scripts/tdlib/` docs updated.
- NOTE (downstream): `DriveSource` adapter, TDLib parameters/db paths (TASK-260715-1hdnuy), auth flow, `TdError`→`SourceError` normalization (DEC-003), flood-wait `retry_after` parsing — all follow-up tasks of STORY-260715-3elo6l.
- STATUS: to-review.

## 2026-07-17

### 2359 — Reproducible TDLib tdjson artifact lands (TASK-260715-rxjkpi → to-review)
- MILESTONE: `.scripts/tdlib/build_tdlib.py` (`make tdlib`) fetches pinned TDLib, builds `libtdjson.dylib` + C headers, stages `manifest.json` + `CHECKSUMS.sha256`, proves it with a Rust link smoke. Built for real: TDLib **1.8.66**, dylib 23,661,456 B, macos-arm64. `make check` 8/8 (new self-tests included).
- DECISION (pin a commit, not a tag): TDLib's last git tag `v1.8.0` is from 2022 while the lib is on 1.8.66; the ecosystem pins commits. Pinned `022d60202e446ad1287b9fb68e687c8a0760788b` (tdlib/td master, resolved 2026-07-17) in one constant `TDLIB_COMMIT`. Bumping = edit that + rebuild.
- DECISION (build only the `tdjson` shared target): it links every TDLib sub-lib into itself and exports only the C JSON interface → single self-contained dylib; skips the CLI examples/static libs → short build, small tree. Public headers = 2 source (`td_json_client.h`, `td_log.h`) + cmake-generated `tdjson_export.h`.
- DECISION (link-smoke crate is NOT in the gramdrive workspace): `.scripts/tdlib/link-smoke/` carries its own `[workspace]` table so `cargo build --workspace` / `make check` never link libtdjson — same isolation the reserved `gramdrive-source-tdjson` keeps. Gate stays green without the artifact, Xcode, or network; only the faked-subprocess self-test runs there.
- FINDING (version read from the running library, per packaging philosophy): smoke calls `td_execute`/`td_create_client_id`/`td_send`/`td_receive`, reads `getOption "version"` (answered pre-auth, no api_id/network) → prints `TDLib version: 1.8.66`, which the pipeline records as `runtime_version`. Deprecated `td_json_client_create/destroy` are referenced (addresses printed) for link proof only — TDLib forbids mixing the two client interfaces in one process, so they are not called against the live modern scheduler.
- FINDING (reproducibility scoped honestly, NFR-052): guarantee is *attributability* — pinned commit + recorded toolchain (cmake/clang/OpenSSL 3.6.3/zlib 1.2.12/gperf) + checksums + `otool -L` linkage (openssl@3, libz, libc++, libSystem). Byte-identical rebuild pursued same-machine (Release, `-ffile-prefix-map`, `ZERO_AR_DATE`, ld64 content-hashed LC_UUID); `make tdlib-verify` = 2 clean builds compared. Manifest `path_independent` is *derived* from `clean_build_tree` ∧ remap, not asserted. Cross-machine identical bytes NOT claimed.
- SCOPE: new `.scripts/tdlib/{build_tdlib.py,README.md,link-smoke/}`, `.scripts/tests/test_build_tdlib.py` (20 tests, in `repo` gate), `Makefile` (+`tdlib`, `tdlib-smoke`, `tdlib-verify`). No gramdrive crate touched; zero new workspace deps. Output under gitignored `.temp/tdlib/`.
- NOTE (downstream, not this task): dylib links OpenSSL/zlib dynamically (brew absolute install names). Self-contained/static-OpenSSL bundling + notarization/signing belong to the release-signing task (TASK-260715-3bhbkv). The tdjson wrapper (TASK-260715-2ulon7) consumes this artifact via `GRAMDRIVE_TDLIB_ARTIFACT_DIR` + rpath.

### 2358 — Incremental render planner lands (TASK-260715-22l8zy → to-review)
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive-engine::render_plan` — computes which generated docs a normalized-change batch made stale (whole-chat `messages.ndjson` always; the transcript of each touched civil month only) and turns them into a `RenderPlan` of `RenderJob`s against the chat's current event watermark, skipping anything already current. `make check` 8/8; +11 planner tests + 2 civil tests; zero new deps.

Render crate: `Civil` hoisted `markdown/text.rs` → new `pub mod civil` with a public `year_month(instant_ms, offset_seconds)` (markdown re-uses it). READMEs updated.

Render exposes only the shared `civil` calendar so the planner and the Markdown day-grouping can never disagree about a month boundary (single source of truth). No year-level generated doc exists in v1. `chat.json` (`DocFormat::Json`) renderer not built → deliberately out of catalog; `DocClass::for_key` returns `None` for it (a future planner's work), so a dirty chat.json in `render_state` is left, not mis-rendered.
- FINDING (AC realized, not re-implemented): "only affected partitions regenerate" = the pure `affected_documents` (BTreeSet of civil months + the single NDJSON, dedup, deterministic order). "Interrupted regeneration leaves old valid version / resumes safely / no partial published" is the existing `state::publish_render` watermark protocol + atomic promotion — planner never publishes; a crashed render keeps the prior `content_version` readable and the doc dirty, and a raced publish lands `clean=false` and is re-planned at the newer watermark. Both proven end-to-end in `tests/render_plan.rs`.
- FINDING (scope bound, not a defect): full render *driver* (decode `message_events.payload` → `MessageHistory` → bytes → publish) is downstream — no payload decoder exists yet. This task is the planner: affected-set + dirty-marking (`dirty_affected`, txn-atomic with the change per SYNC-022) + worklist→plan (`plan_worklist`) + change→plan (`plan_for_changes`). `dirty_affected` needs the generated-doc items projected (tree builder's job); it fails loudly on the item FK rather than marking phantom work.

### 2312 — Monthly Markdown renderer lands (TASK-260715-hmmiay → to-review)
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive-render::markdown` — deterministic human-readable monthly transcript (`YYYY/MM.md`), sibling of the NDJSON renderer. Pure fn `render_transcript`/`write_transcript` (streams block-by-block, one reused buffer). `make check-core` 6/6 + `make check-repo` 2/2; +20 markdown tests (17 unit + Mirror/Audit goldens); 643 workspace tests green; **zero new deps**.
- SCOPE: new `src/markdown/{mod,render,text}.rs`, `tests/{markdown_unit,markdown_golden}.rs`, `tests/golden/corpus_{mirror,audit}.md`; README "Monthly Markdown transcript (v1)" section. Refactor: `ndjson/record.rs` → crate-level `record.rs`, re-exported by both `ndjson` and `markdown` (existing `ndjson::*` paths preserved, NDJSON goldens byte-unchanged).
- DECISION (media links caller-supplied): added `Attachment::media_name: Option<String>`. Collision-resolved media file names are the naming policy's (`resolve_siblings` needs the whole media-dir sibling set the renderer lacks), so the engine supplies the name; renderer only percent-encodes + links `media/<enc>`. NDJSON ignores the field (links by opaque `AttachmentKey` id instead). Not-downloaded/restricted/view-once/unavailable → explicit note, no link (SYNC-032/POL-4).
- DECISION (timezone-explicit, no tzdata): SYNC-031 "timezone-explicit" satisfied by a caller-supplied fixed `UtcOffset` (seconds east of UTC), declared in the front matter; every civil date/time computed in it via hand-rolled Howard-Hinnant `civil_from_days` (POL-6, dependency-free). IANA/DST out of v1 scope — engine picks the partition's offset.
- DECISION (injection safety = one total rule): `& < >`→HTML entities (block structure resolves pre-entity, so `&gt;` starts no blockquote/tag), all other CommonMark/GFM-active ASCII punctuation→backslash-escaped, C0 controls→U+FFFD, multi-line text joined with hard breaks so it stays ONE inert paragraph (kills indented-code + block re-interpretation), media links percent-encoded (also blocks `../`). Verified structurally: injected `#`/`>`/fence/`---`/`<script>`/table/`[](js:)` produce no new structure.
- DECISION (content-version token mirrors NDJSON `gramdrive.transcript/s1/r1/w<wm>`): retention mode AND timezone are account-level render config held constant per document (re-rendered by engine on change), not per-doc version state — same treatment NDJSON gives retention mode. Title-independent (DOM-023): doc uses `chat_id`, never the mutable title.
- FINDING (scoping, not a defect): entity-aware rich formatting (bold/italic/links from `entities`) deferred — needs UTF-16 offset splicing + URL-scheme sanitization, adds injection surface, not in AC. v1 = safe escaped plain text; lossless entity data stays in `messages.ndjson`. `MONTH_MARKDOWN_SCHEMA_FAMILY = SchemaFamily(1)`: family lineage is per-format (id codec discriminates `DocFormat`), distinct from NDJSON family-1; engine wires it into `tree::DocSchemas.month_markdown`.
- STATUS: to-review. Notes + `TASK-260715-hmmiay_results.md` on the board.

The renderer threads `input_watermark_seq` into header + `content_version` token (`gramdrive.messages/s1/r1/w{seq}`) so it lines up with `state::WriteTxn::publish_render` (SYNC-024).
- FINDING (POL-3 in the renderer): Mirror = one `present` record per live msg, deleted omitted, prior revisions purged. Audit = every revision (`superseded`…`present`), observed deletion → content-preserving `deleted` tombstone (last-known content + `deleted_ms`). Renderer owns the projection; tested both modes + tombstone content preservation.

### 2135 — Review ACCEPTED: cache quota, pinning, eviction (TASK-260715-11abx8 → done)
- VERDICT: accepted. Both AC verified by reading `engine/src/cache/{quota,pin}.rs` + `state/src/repo/{cache,transfers}.rs` against the contracts and rerunning `make check` 8/8 (test 11.4s), zero new deps.
- FINDING: eviction eligibility (`pinned = 0 AND verification = 'verified'`) lives IN the `evict_cache_entry` DELETE, not just the planner — a stale plan can't remove pinned/in-flight bytes. Dedup last-referrer delete + row-before-file confirmed in code and `a_shared_object_is_deleted_only_when_its_last_referrer_is_evicted`.

- NOTE (non-blocking, no rework): (1) default is binary 10 GiB vs `.spec/policies.md:19` "10 GB" (+7.37%) — deliberate/documented/configurable; flag to product if decimal intended. (2) `enforce` may leave a small *reclaimable* residual if an item becomes blocked between plan and execute — self-corrects next pass, status honest, never silent loss. ### 2205 — Cache quota, pinning, eviction land (TASK-260715-11abx8 → to-review)
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive-engine::cache::Evictor` + `cache::{pin,unpin}` — device accounting by category incl. partial transfers (SYNC-050), quota (`QuotaPolicy`, 10 GiB default, configurable), actionable status without mutation (`assess` → over_by/reclaimable/residual, SYNC-054), LRU eviction of unpinned-verified only (`enforce`/`reclaim`, POL-2/SYNC-051-052). Stateless over the store like `TransferMachine`/`Promoter`. `make check` 8/8; +11 cache_quota + 4 lib-unit engine tests, +5 state tests; zero new deps.
- DECISION: quota *value* durability is the host's (device config), not the core's. SYNC-054 "durable" satisfied by the durable *consequence* (evicted `cache_entries` rows) + immediate `assess`. Persisting the scalar in-DB would need a v2 migration — `schema/v1.sql` is frozen (NFR-041) and is another task's territory. Clean boundary, not a forced fit; core-owned quota persistence is an explicit future v2 if product wants it.
- DECISION: quota governs **unpinned** bytes; pinned/Archive-Mode are exempt-but-counted (surfaced as `pinned_bytes`). Unpinned-**unverified** bytes count toward the budget (SYNC-050) but are never evicted (SYNC-052) → they can force eviction of verified content or leave an honest `residual_bytes` (over quota, can't reclaim — never silent loss). Tested.
- DECISION: two byte axes — quota is row-size (`cache_entries.size`, so dedup'd entries count twice); report `reclaimed_bytes` is physical (a shared object frees once, on its last referrer). On-disk object deleted only when no surviving entry references its `materialization_ref` (dedup, per 3s6cpe handoff). Row-before-file deletion: crash leaves an orphan `StateStore::reconcile` reclaims (SYNC-053). Reuses the existing `LocalStorage` port.
- DECISION: eviction no-race is explicit + testable — skip host-declared open reads (`EvictionRequest::protected`) and durable live transfers (`has_live_transfer`); keyset frontier walk advances its cursor past skipped rows so a wall of in-use rows never stalls the drain. Under dedup, disk-full `reclaim` may free < target physical; caller retries with the deficit.
- FINDING: closed the LOGBOOK #70 pin-downgrade gap at the engine layer — `cache::pin` is directional (user pin wins over Archive-Mode, survives archive teardown; archive never downgrades user) without changing `pin_item`'s blind ON CONFLICT. Directional unpin releases only the matching origin.
- FINDING: no "media-cache-policy fixtures" existed; the repo convention is Rust-coded fixtures, so `tests/cache_quota.rs` (11 cases, `entry` seeds real `Promoter::promote` output) *is* the POL-2 fixture suite.
- SCOPE: engine `src/cache/{mod,quota,pin}.rs` + `src/lib.rs`/`README.md` + `tests/cache_quota.rs`; state `src/repo/{cache,transfers,mod}.rs` (additive read queries `cache_totals`/`cache_usage_by_kind`/`eviction_candidates_after`/`materialization_ref_referenced`/`has_live_transfer`/`staged_transfer_bytes` + `CacheTotals`, no schema change) + 2 state test files.
- STATUS: to-review. Handoff (assess/accounting = app usage surface; host passes protected reads; directory/archive pin = caller enumeration + per-item `cache::pin`) in TASK-260715-11abx8_results.md.

### 2115 — Review ACCEPTED: integrity verification + atomic promotion (TASK-260715-3s6cpe → done)
- VERDICT: accepted. All three AC verified by reading `cache/{mod,promote}.rs` + `model/hash.rs` against the state contracts and rerunning: `make check` 8/8, 12 promotion + 8 hash KAT + 4 lib tests green. Fail-closed (truncation → short read `IntegrityFailed`, unreadable/vanished staging, version drift), dedup-with-provenance (one blob row + one on-disk object for identical bytes, each attachment keeps name/version and links, `attachments_referencing_blob` returns both), crash-safe+idempotent (file-before-row + real reconcile `OrphanCacheObject`/`LeakedStaging`/`MissingCacheObject` findings; content-addressed promote idempotent; `AlreadyMaterialized` never re-reads consumed staging).
- FINDING: "integrity" here is completeness(extent)+version-pin+readability with SHA-256 as content identity, NOT comparison against a source-provided expected digest — none exists (Telegram exposes no verifiable content hash; `AttachmentFacts`/`FileFacts` carry no digest, `blob_hash` is the download *result*). Length-preserving in-transit corruption is caught below this layer (MTProto/TDLib), not here. Correct design for the domain, worth stating so a future reader does not look for a missing hash check.
- FINDING: vendored SHA-256 validated against genuine external vectors (empty/`abc`/1M-'a' match canonical FIPS 180-4/NIST digits, not self-consistency) — the KAT net is real. `attachment_linked: false` silent-skip when the projection has no attachment row is a reasonable degradation (blob still cached/servable, backreference re-linkable later), surfaced on the outcome, not a defect.
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive-engine::cache` lands — `Promoter` layers over `transfer::CompleteOutcome::Promoted`: hash whole staged content (SHA-256), re-check pin, atomic host promote, then one txn recording blob + `verified` cache entry + attachment blob link. Plus `gramdrive-model::hash` (streaming SHA-256) and `EngineError::Storage`. 4 lib + 12 integration tests + 8 model KAT; `make check` 8/8; no `gramdrive-state` change needed.
- DECISION: vendored SHA-256 in `gramdrive-model`, not `sha2` — `sha2` pulls `typenum`'s build script + ~6 crates into a tree where every build script is named on purpose (`deny.toml [bans.build]`, POL-6). A fully-specified hash with published KATs (FIPS 180-4 §D + NIST 1M-'a' + boundary + streaming-equals-one-shot) trades the dep for a test obligation. Content identity/integrity of public bytes only — no constant-time claim. `cargo deny` unchanged (zero new deps), confirming the trade.
- DECISION: file-before-row ordering is the crash-safety design, not a distributed txn — host `PromotionHost::promote` (content-addressed, fsync-durable rename) runs before the publishing txn. Crash after file/before commit → reconcile `OrphanCacheObject` deletes it; crash before file → `LeakedStaging`; OS eviction of the object → `MissingCacheObject` drops the row, keeps the pin (SYNC-053). Verified end-to-end against `StateStore::reconcile`.
- DECISION: content-addressed `materialization_ref` gives dedup + idempotency for free — identical bytes rename onto an existing name (`deduplicated` no-op), so two attachments share one blob row + one on-disk object while each keeps its own identity/name/version and links to the blob (`attachments_referencing_blob` returns both). Re-invoking `promote` after commit is `AlreadyMaterialized` (verified entry at this version), so a consumed staging is never read twice.
- DECISION: blob = whole content only — a partial-range transfer streamed to readers is not a blob (domain-model § Blob) → `NotWholeContent`; gate is `completed_ranges` covering `[0, extent)`. Fail-closed everywhere: short/unreadable staging → `IntegrityFailed`, departed pin → `VersionDeparted`, both hand intact/untrusted staging back for disposal.
- FINDING: the `done` transfer's `temp_ref` is left inert after the rename — the handle names nothing a live transfer claims, and reconcile's `LeakedStaging` only flags staging *objects* that exist, so no `gramdrive-state` API to clear it was needed.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-model/src/{hash.rs,lib.rs,README.md}`, `crates/gramdrive-engine/src/cache/{mod,promote}.rs`, `src/{lib.rs,README.md}`, `src/transfer/error.rs` (`EngineError::Storage`), `tests/promotion.rs`.
- STATUS: to-review. Handoff notes for 11abx8 in TASK-260715-3s6cpe_results.md (Storage = evict+retry signal; pin already folded; dedup shares mat_ref).

### 2005 — Review TASK-260715-22fh09 accepted; ranged fetch coordinator lands
- DECISION: ranged fetch coordinator → done. All four AC verified by reading `fetch/{mod,plan,sink,staging}.rs` against the `TransferMachine` contract and rerunning everything: range bytes exact through a contract-verifying sink (`[5,23)` demand → aligned `[0,16)+[16,32)` on the wire, crash-resume reassembles all 64 bytes); cancellation prompt on both paths (queued cancel → zero network; mid-run durable cancel injected via a second SQLite connection observed at the next checkpoint, remaining chunks never fetched; dropped run future leaves a resumable row); stale version cannot publish (mid-fetch race → terminal `failed/version_conflict`, staged wiped, disposal returned, readers failed — promotion only via `machine.complete`'s atomic coverage+pin gate); duplicate compatible work bounded (coalesced readers share one wire fetch, retry and crash-resume never re-fetch staged chunks, fanout=2 → exactly 2 in flight).

- STATUS: review rerun: `cargo test -p gramdrive-engine` 23 unit + 17 fetch + 18 transfer green; `make check` 8/8 (provenance `.temp/acceptance/local-all`). No rework items; verdict in TASK-260715-22fh09_review-verdict.md.

### 2001 — Ranged fetch coordinator: live policy over the durable machine (TASK-260715-22fh09)
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive-engine::fetch` lands — `FetchCoordinator` drives any `DriveSource` through `TransferMachine` claims: reader coalescing + streaming (SYNC-046), chunk-grid alignment (SYNC-041), bounded per-item fanout, in-attempt locator refresh (SYNC-045), prompt two-path cancellation (SYNC-043/005), promotion only via the machine's atomic gate (SYNC-042). 17 integration tests over the fake source (faults, version races, crash-resume, two-connection durable cancel) + 16 unit tests; `make check` 8/8.
- DECISION: no async runtime in the engine — sub-fetch parallelism is a hand-rolled `poll_fn` fleet with deterministic poll order; each `source.fetch` lives in an async block that owns its sink (no self-reference, no unsafe), so dropping a future is the prompt per-chunk cancel. Testkit's single-threaded executor and tokio both drive it; the run future is `Send` (test-asserted).
- DECISION: readers stream *from staging*, not from the network path — one code path serves out-of-order parallel sub-fetches, resume-from-staged after crash, and late-attaching coalesced readers. Requires `Staging::read_at` on the host port; a reader is version-pinned at open and never topped up across versions (uncovered remainder re-requests on transfer finish, `ReaderEnd::Reattached`).
- DECISION: `StaleReference` refreshes in-attempt (re-ask the undelivered tail, same identity, bounded by `FetchConfig::stale_refresh_limit`); everything else classifies through `machine.fail` — no second retry policy in the coordinator. `StagingError::Full` → DiskFull/park, `StagingError::Failed` → Integrity/wipe+refetch.
- DECISION: whole-object claims with unknown extent suspend fail-closed (`AttemptEnd::ExtentUnknown`) instead of probing blind; metadata refresh + `resume` continues. Mirrors the promotion gate's stance.
