- FINDING: `claim_next_transfer` filters `cancel_requested` rows, so a queued cancel is never claimed — it is acknowledged by fresh demand (`request` displacement), and the running case is covered by the coordinator's pre-network checkpoint at claim start plus the per-batch checkpoint (verified with a second SQLite connection injecting the cancel mid-run).
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-engine/src/fetch/{mod,plan,sink,staging}.rs`, `tests/fetch_coordinator.rs`, `src/transfer/mod.rs` (pub(crate) `ranges`/`item_standing`), `Cargo.toml` (testkit dev-dep), `src/lib.rs`, `README.md`.
- STATUS: to-review.

### 1928 — Review TASK-260715-g4k3zm accepted; durable transfer state machine lands
- DECISION: durable transfer state machine → done. AC verified by reading `transfer/{mod,ranges,retry,error}.rs` against the `gramdrive-state` repo contract and rerunning everything: invalid transitions rejected at two layers (typestate `ClaimedTransfer` capability + repo `InvalidTransition` from stored state, both tested via stale-claim probes); crash-resume proven over a real file-backed store with reopen + `reconcile` (resumed claim plans exactly the missing suffix, gate still refuses pre-suffix promotion); promotion gate checks coverage + version pin + `done` transition in one transaction, fails closed on unknown extent; version races converge on one terminal residue from all four discovery points. `cargo test -p gramdrive-engine` 7+18 green, `make check` 8/8 green in review rerun.
- FINDING: the abandoned-cancel displacement in `request` is broader than its docs — the module docs say a cancel on a *queued* transfer is acknowledged by the next request, but the code displaces any live cancel-requested row, including `running` ones mid-fetch (the requester's host then gets a `StagingDisposal` for a staging area the claim holder may still be writing). Durably safe — the stale claim answers `InvalidTransition` at its next boundary and reconciliation backstops the disposal — so accepted as-is; a doc-precision nit for a future pass, not rework.
- NOTE: priority ordering is enforced and tested at the repo layer (`claims_come_in_priority_order_and_respect_backoff_and_cancel`), where it is owned; the engine adds only the `Priority` vocabulary. Extent enforcement on progress uses the claim-time extent, but `complete` re-derives standing fresh, so a late-learned extent cannot admit a wrong promotion.
- STATUS: done.

### 1923 — Durable transfer state machine: durable rows are the machine, policy is stateless (TASK-260715-g4k3zm)
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive-engine::transfer` lands — the engine crate's first real code. `TransferMachine` is stateless policy over the `gramdrive-state` transfer journal: request/pin/coalesce, claim with resume plan, monotonic progress, checkpoint (cancel > drift), SYNC-044 classification with bounded retry budget, promotion gate, deterministic invalidation. 7 unit + 18 integration tests; `make check` 8/8; no `gramdrive-state` changes needed.
- DECISION: `complete` *borrows* the claim token instead of consuming it. Consuming on a refused gate (IncompleteContent/UnknownExtent) strands a `running` row with no token able to move it — suspend/fail also need the token, and `claim` cannot re-claim a running row. Found while writing the gate test; API deadlock, not a style choice.
- DECISION: `AuthRequired`/`DiskFull` (and source-observed local stops with no durable cancel flag) *park* — suspend with progress kept — instead of retry-with-backoff or terminal failure. No budget burn, no queue polling; "why parked" is reported in-memory but not persisted, because any resume just re-claims and a still-failing precondition re-parks (convergent, so hosts resume coarsely after reauth/space-freed).
- DECISION: no auto-re-enqueue after version invalidation — fresh demand re-requests at the current version. Auto-replacement would need range clipping against the new extent and second-guesses demand the fetch coordinator (TASK-260715-22fh09) owns.
- FINDING: a cancel flagged on a *queued* row is unclaimable forever (claim skips `cancel_requested`) and no repo API lists flagged rows — a zombie until demand returns. Resolved convergence-on-demand: the next `request` for the same (item, version) acknowledges the abandoned row and starts fresh (`transfer/mod.rs:request`).
- FINDING: whole-object promotion with unknown extent is unprovable from ranges alone → fails closed (`EngineError::UnknownExtent`) until a metadata refresh records `logical_size` at the same content version. Tested including the refresh-then-promote path.
- NOTE: uniform invariant — terminal non-`done` rows claim no staging: every terminal path wipes ranges + `temp_ref` and returns a `StagingDisposal`; reconciliation stays the backstop for dropped disposals. This also neutralizes the previously logged dangling-`temp_ref` cosmetic for rows this machine finishes.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-engine/src/transfer/{mod,ranges,retry,error}.rs`, `tests/transfer_machine.rs`, `src/lib.rs`, `README.md`.
- STATUS: to-review.

### 1852 — Review TASK-260715-21clwh accepted; startup reconciliation lands
- DECISION: startup reconciliation → done. AC verified by reading `reconcile.rs`/`tests/reconcile.rs` and rerunning: corruption/missing/extra fixtures each converge and are idempotent (14/14, incl. a real re-exec+abort process-crash test, exit 134), no Telegram writes is structural (no `gramdrive-source` dep, `LocalStorage`-only entrypoints), pins survive dropped cache entries (`remove_cache_entry` is `DELETE FROM cache_entries`; `pins` FK cascades from `items`, not `cache_entries`). `make check` 8/8 green in review rerun.
- FINDING: SYNC-070's "provider registrations" and "item versions" are absent from the diff but correctly so — provider-registration reconciliation cannot live in a platform-neutral crate (belongs to platform/engine layer + sibling TASK-260715-1nuhxj), and item-version/`items`-projection rebuild is reported as `ProjectionRebuildPending` with the marker left raised, deferred to the engine-side projection builder. Both are traced, not silent gaps.
- NOTE: crash-idempotency is genuine, not asserted — every finding is re-derived from durable DB/disk state each pass, and repairs run in per-finding short txns with in-transaction re-checks (`WHERE state = 'running'`). The dangling terminal-row `temp_ref` after a mid-repair crash is cosmetic (`live_staging_refs` ignores terminal states) and already tracked via the `mark_transfer_done` follow-up.

### 1838 — Reconciliation requires engine quiescence; in-flight policy flag and lease columns both rejected (TASK-260715-21clwh)
- DECISION: `StateStore::reconcile` requires that no engine is running against the file — an `fsck`-style caller contract, not a checkable precondition. That is what makes a `running` transfer legible: a dead claim and a live one are identical rows, and `gramdrive-state` has no liveness primitive (no pid, no platform API) to tell them apart. With no engine live, every `running` row is dead by construction.
- FINDING: the first design had the host declare topology (`InFlightPolicy::Reclaim|Leave`, per PLAT-MAC-003). It shipped green, then broke on review: under `Leave`, orphan-object and leaked-staging repair race a live engine that has written an object but not yet committed its row (or staged bytes but not yet recorded `temp_ref`) → reconciliation deletes what the engine is at that moment writing. Not patchable per-finding without incoherence, because *every* check races a live engine. `gramdrive-engine` is 32 lines, so no write-ordering contract exists to lean on either.
- DECISION: rejected lease/owner columns on `transfers` (migration v2) — the topology-independent answer, but lease *renewal* is engine behavior and the engine is empty; the protocol would have had no participant except its own tests.
- FINDING: `mark_transfer_done` does not clear `temp_ref` (`repo/transfers.rs:567`), so a terminal row can name a staging area whose bytes are gone. Reconciliation clears the handle off terminal rows only; handles claimed by live rows are protected, since a requeued transfer resumes from exactly those bytes.
- NOTE: requeue keeps `completed_ranges`/`temp_ref` and does not touch `retry_count` — a crash is not a failed attempt (SYNC-044). A missing cache object drops the `cache_entries` row but never the `pins` row (POL-2 intent is independent of materialization); a generated doc also goes back on the dirty worklist.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/reconcile.rs`, `tests/reconcile.rs` (14 tests, 5 mutations verified), `StateError::LocalStorage`.
- STATUS: to-review.

### 1755 — Review TASK-260715-1opnb2 accepted; pin_item origin overwrite is directionally unguarded
- DECISION: repository layer accepted → done. AC evidence verified by reading the modules and the tests, `cargo test -p gramdrive-state` green in review rerun.
- FINDING: `pin_item` upserts `origin = excluded.origin` unconditionally (`crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/cache.rs:496`): an Archive-Mode re-pin over an existing User pin silently downgrades intent, and Archive-Mode teardown (`pins(Some(ArchiveMode))` → unpin) would then release the user's pin — the POL-2 outcome the docstring says cannot happen. Tested direction (ArchiveMode → User upgrade) holds; the reverse is unguarded in a layer whose stated philosophy is enforcing invariants in the layer.
- NOTE: non-blocking — engine (STORY-260715-2hs8cf) owns pin orchestration and can check before archive-pinning; a directional upsert (user wins; explicit unpin+pin still downgrades) would close it at zero expressiveness cost. For the cache-quota-and-eviction task to pick up.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/cache.rs:490-503`, test `repo_cache_render.rs::pins_are_durable_intent_with_origin_semantics`.

### 1754 — sanitize_is_idempotent proptest counterexample: name drifts on second pass (gramdrive-model)
- REGRESSION: `make check` rerun during TASK-260715-1opnb2 review failed `gramdrive-model naming_properties::sanitize_is_idempotent` — a fresh random counterexample (57 successes, then failure), deterministic on seed replay. First pass leaves `.._` (dot run adjacent to characters replaced with `_`, e.g. `..` before `|`); second pass collapses it to `._`. Input class: zero-width joiners + combining marks + forbidden punctuation around dot runs. Violates POL-1 stable names: a name passing the policy twice (re-sync, rebuild) drifts.
- NOTE: unrelated to the task under review (its diff touches `gramdrive-state` only); sanitizer is TASK-260715-1ffbkg (commit b8d9b2b). Filed as BUG-260717-3rr59f under STORY-260715-3qxar5; deterministic repro seed preserved in outcome resource TASK-260715-1opnb2_review.md (regressions file restored to handed-off state to keep the review read-only).
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-model/src/naming.rs:568`, `crates/gramdrive-model/tests/naming_properties.rs:171`.

### 1748 — Repository layer derives item identity columns from the ItemId itself (TASK-260715-1opnb2)
- DECISION: `upsert_item` derives kind, account scope, canonical link, and view columns by decoding the `ItemId` (`repo/items.rs:derive_identity`) instead of taking them in the record — a caller cannot write identity columns that disagree with the id. Account-root epoch is the one underivable column; it is read from the account row at upsert (DOM-021 keeps the epoch out of the root's id on purpose).
- NOTE: `Message`/`Blob` keys and appearance-of-account are typed `InvalidArgument` at the repo layer — the schema `kind` CHECK would also refuse, but as an untyped SQL error.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/items.rs`.

### 1747 — Stale-revision guard: replayed pre-edit history pages must not rewind the projection (TASK-260715-1opnb2)
- DECISION: `apply_message_changes` skips a revision whose effective revision time (`edited_at_ms` else `sent_at_ms`) is older than the projected one (`repo/changes.rs`). Exact-replay idempotence alone is not enough: a history page fetched before an edit and replayed after the edit's change-feed application would otherwise append an `edited` event that rewinds current state to content Telegram no longer shows.
- NOTE: relies on Telegram edit timestamps being per-message monotonic. Equal-time different-payload still applies as an edit (deliberate: no silent divergence).
- NOTE: two more asymmetries in the same function, both POL-3: deletion of a never-observed message is a skip (never imply unobserved history); a revision after a witnessed deletion is a skip (tombstone wins).
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/changes.rs`, tests `repo_changes.rs`.

### 1746 — publish_render re-checks the event log inside the publishing transaction (TASK-260715-1opnb2)
- DECISION: `publish_render` takes the source `ChatKey` and checks `EXISTS(events > watermark)` in its own write transaction; if events arrived while the renderer worked, the publication lands but `dirty` stays 1 (`repo/render.rs`). Closes the render/append race without locks: published bytes never silently claim to reflect events they predate (SYNC-024). Watermark regression (publish below stored watermark) is a typed refusal.
- NOTE: alternative — trusting the caller to re-check — leaves the race open by default; making the repo own it means the safe path is the only path.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/repo/render.rs`, test `repo_cache_render.rs::publication_never_regresses_and_never_hides_late_events`.

### 1745 — WAL concurrency proven with two connections in one process (TASK-260715-1opnb2)
- FINDING: SQLite locking is file-based, so two `StateStore` connections in one test process exercise the same primitives as app + FP extension in two processes: read snapshots stable across a foreign commit, `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` writers serialized through the busy handler with no double-claim of a transfer, and a concurrent reader never observing a cursor ahead of the state it seals (20 batches, invariant asserted per snapshot).
- NOTE: `read_txn`/`write_txn` take `&mut self` — one connection, one transaction at a time, compile-enforced; concurrency is more connections, not shared ones. Statement cache bumped to 64 (`store.rs`) since the repo layer's distinct statements outnumber rusqlite's default 16.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/tests/repo_concurrency.rs`, `src/repo/mod.rs`, `src/store.rs`.

### 1659 — Migration runner reads user_version outside its transaction: two processes both apply the same migration (TASK-260715-18l9xz)
- FINDING: `migrate::run` reads `PRAGMA user_version` outside any transaction (`migrate.rs:200`) and `apply` never re-checks it inside the transaction it opens. `rusqlite`'s `conn.transaction()` is DEFERRED, so nothing serializes two processes that both read v1 and both decide to apply v2. Migrations run inside `StateStore::open`, and the crate's premise is that the app and the File Provider extension share the file (`lib.rs:6-9`) — first launch after an upgrade shipping a v2 is exactly when they race.
- NOTE: reproduced at the SQLite level, mirroring `run`/`apply` statement-for-statement (`.temp/TASK-260715-18l9xz/concurrent_migration_probe.py`): A commits v2, B then fails with `duplicate column name`. B's `StateStore::open()` returns `Err(MigrationFailed)` against a healthy v2 database, with no retry.
- NOTE: **no corruption**. The `schema_history` PRIMARY KEY on `version` means a double-apply can never silently succeed even if the migration SQL were idempotent, and the user_version-stamps-with-its-work invariant holds throughout. Impact is a failed open on the losing process, recoverable by reopening.
- NOTE: latent — `MIGRATIONS` is empty (`SCHEMA_VERSION` is the baseline), so unreachable in the shipped build. It bites whoever writes the first real migration. Standard remedy is `TransactionBehavior::Immediate` + a version re-read inside the transaction, treating already-at-this-version as success; fail-fast vs. wait vs. recheck-and-skip is an undecided design choice, not a typo.
- STATUS: pending — found in review, accepted as out of AC scope for this task. Recommend a follow-up under STORY-260715-16ik2x, sequenced before the first migration ships.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/migrate.rs:195-233`, `crates/gramdrive-state/src/store.rs:49-52`.

### 1658 — POL-3 append-only trigger blocks in-place payload migration; a future payload_schema bump must choose (TASK-260715-18l9xz)
- FINDING: `message_events_append_only` permits exactly one UPDATE shape — payload and payload_schema both going to NULL (the Mirror purge). A `payload_schema` 1→2 migration therefore **cannot** rewrite payloads in place: the trigger aborts it. Discovered while picking a realistic chunked-migration test case.
- NOTE: leaves three options for whoever bumps the payload format — (a) lazy read-time interpretation keyed on `payload_schema`, no migration at all; (b) DROP the trigger, rewrite, recreate — but a *chunked* rewrite leaves the log unprotected across chunk transactions; (c) append new-schema revision events rather than rewriting, which is what POL-3's model actually implies. Not decided here; no payload v2 exists yet.
- NOTE: this is why the framework's test migration backfills `messages` (a projection, no trigger) instead of `message_events`. NFR-041's "serialized metadata" migration story routes through this constraint.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/schema/v1.sql:147`, `crates/gramdrive-state/src/migrate.rs`.

### 1657 — Migration journal must sit outside the numbered schema (TASK-260715-18l9xz)
- DECISION: `migration_progress` + `repair_markers` live in `src/schema/journal.sql`, applied `IF NOT EXISTS` on every open at any version — not introduced by a migration.
- NOTE: the reason is circular otherwise — a file written by a build older than the runner has no journal, and the runner needs the journal to run the migration that would create it. `schema_history` stays in `v1.sql` because it genuinely is part of v1.
- NOTE: bootstrap runs only *after* the future-version check, so a file this build admits it cannot read is never written to. `tests/migrations.rs` asserts exactly that by checking no journal tables appear after a refused open.

### 1656 — Runner stall guard catches only the unchanged-checkpoint case (TASK-260715-18l9xz)
- FINDING: mutation-testing the runner (hardcoding `read_checkpoint` → None) produced an **infinite loop**, not a test failure: the chunk restarted from 0 each pass and returned a checkpoint differing from the `None` it was given, so `MigrationStalled` never fired.
- NOTE: unreachable in real code (the runner reads the checkpoint it committed), but it marks the guard's true limit — a chunk returning a *fresh* useless checkpoint forever is indistinguishable from a long migration making progress. Any chunk bound the runner imposed would be a guess. Documented on `ChunkFn`; the per-migration fixture + interruption tests are where that bug is meant to die.
- NOTE: three realistic mutations *are* caught (early version stamp → 5 tests, preamble re-run → 5, marker never cleared → 2). Suite has teeth.

### 1613 — SQLite partial-index prover rejects IN-lists; the EXPLAIN gate caught it (TASK-260715-1ceq7h)
- FINDING: `transfers_queue` partial index declared `WHERE state IN ('queued','running','suspended')` was **ignored** for `WHERE state = 'queued'` — plan fell to `SCAN transfers` + `USE TEMP B-TREE FOR ORDER BY`. SQLite's theorem prover accepts equality against an OR of equalities but not against an `IN` list.
- FIX: predicate restated as `state = 'queued' OR state = 'running' OR state = 'suspended'` (`crates/gramdrive-state/src/schema/v1.sql`); plan now `SEARCH transfers USING INDEX transfers_queue`.
- NOTE: found by the plan gate in `tests/query_plans.rs` — every required query's EXPLAIN must show an index and no temp b-tree, asserted against the loaded 110k-message fixture after ANALYZE. An index a query cannot prove is an index that does not exist; only an executable check notices.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/src/schema/v1.sql`, `crates/gramdrive-state/tests/query_plans.rs`.

### 1612 — rusqlite pinned at 0.39: libsqlite3-sys 0.38 needs a compiler newer than the toolchain pin (TASK-260715-1ceq7h)
- ROOT CAUSE: rusqlite 0.40 → libsqlite3-sys 0.38.1, whose build script uses `cfg_select!` — unstable on the pinned Rust 1.91 (E0658). Not a license or policy issue; purely the toolchain pin.
- FIX: `rusqlite = "0.39"` (libsqlite3-sys 0.37) with the reason in the workspace `Cargo.toml` comment. Bump together with `rust-toolchain.toml`.
- FINDING: cargo-deny evaluates all targets, so libsqlite3-sys's wasm32 fallback (`sqlite-wasm-rs` → wasm-bindgen tree) enters the graph and its build scripts needed `[bans.build]` names despite never compiling for a GramDrive target — same precedent as `wit-bindgen`.
- SCOPE: `Cargo.toml`, `deny.toml`, `crates/gramdrive-state/Cargo.toml`.

### 1611 — Schema v1: append-only enforced by trigger with exactly one escape hatch (TASK-260715-1ceq7h)
- MILESTONE: gramdrive-state schema v1 — 15 STRICT tables (accounts, chats, chat_list_entries, message_events+messages, attachments, blobs, items, transfers, cache_entries, pins, change_cursors, chat_sync_state, render_state, schema_history), WAL + per-connection FKs, `user_version` gate with named refusals (newer build / needs-migration). 30 state tests + 9 generator tests; `make check` 8/8.
- DECISION: POL-3 append-only is a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger, and its `WHEN` clause permits exactly one write shape — payload+payload_schema → NULL together (Mirror-mode purge keeps markers, drops content). DELETE stays legal (Audit purge tool, account cascade) but `messages.latest_event_seq` FK (no cascade) refuses to purge an event that is still current state. `AUTOINCREMENT` so purged sequence numbers never return — they are render watermarks.
- DECISION: `items` holds canonical structural roots and appearance rows in one table; `canonical_item_id` is indexed identity **bytes, not an FK** — the canonical side lives in per-kind tables reached by decoding the ItemId, so a single FK target is unrepresentable. Tree structure is a real self-FK. One-appearance-per-(canonical,view) unique index needs `COALESCE(view_folder_id, 0)` — SQLite treats NULLs as distinct in unique indexes, so without the sentinel the constraint would be fiction.
- DECISION: fixture generator lives in testkit (`synthetic`), emits model-vocabulary data only (no SQL, no state dep — dependency direction forbids it); state's tests map it to rows. Zipf-skewed 2048 chats / 110k messages, digest-pinned for drift detection; synthetic 31-day-month calendar so partitioning is integer arithmetic.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-state/**`, `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/synthetic.rs`.

### 1552 — SYNC-046 does not mean what the range cases were using it for (TASK-260715-3e8q4m)
- FINDING: SYNC-046 verbatim is *"**Concurrent** requests for the same item/version coalesce where safe and do not corrupt range/accounting state"* (`.spec/sync-and-filesystem-semantics.md:62`). Six range-delivery cases were labelled SYNC-046 and **not one issued a concurrent call**. A range failure printed a requirement about concurrency.
- ROOT CAUSE: `gramdrive-source/src/fetch.rs:39` cites SYNC-046 for the `FetchProgress` accounting fold, which is the "do not corrupt range/accounting state" half. Defensible for the *engine*; wrong as the clause a single-threaded range case pins. The clause those cases actually pin is **SYNC-041** ("Fetch accepts byte ranges even if a source internally downloads larger aligned chunks"), which was not in the `Clause` enum at all.
- FIX: added `Clause::Sync041`, re-labelled the six range cases. SYNC-046 now pins one real case — `fetch.concurrent-fetches-do-not-corrupt-each-other`, two overlapping fetches of the same item/version in flight at once via a hand-rolled `both()` join (`support.rs`; no async dep by design).
- FINDING: `source.rs:25-30` makes concurrency an explicit source obligation ("concurrent calls never corrupt each other's answers") and nothing tested it before this.
- NOTE: the mislabel was self-inflicted and invisible while everything was green — `clauses_upheld()` was crediting SYNC-046 against a clause that had never run. Verbatim `statement()` text is what surfaced it; a paraphrase would have agreed with the code forever.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/conformance/{report.rs,support.rs,cases/fetch.rs}`.

### 1551 — Six conformance cases removed: vacuous or over-strict (TASK-260715-3e8q4m)
- FINDING: a conformance suite has two failure modes worse than a missing case — a case that **cannot fail**, and a case that **fails a correct backend**. Adversarial review (wrapper backends honoring the contract but differing from the fake) proved four of the latter and one of the former. All were written by me and all looked reasonable.
- DECISION: removed *"a directory advertises no write"* (SYNC-060). `SourceItem::capabilities()` (`item.rs:172-187`) hardcodes writes to `false` on **every** branch → no impl can produce a writable set → unfalsifiable. Same tautology as asserting `retry_advice()` on an already-matched variant. SYNC-060 also says *"not advertised **through native providers**"* — a File Provider obligation, not `DriveSource`'s — so the clause left the suite entirely.
- DECISION: removed *"a generous page completes in one page"*. `page.rs:118` says a source *"may return fewer, never more"*, and `next: None` **means** complete rather than obliging a source to know it without another round trip. `source.rs:203` — the contract's own stub — violates the case.
- DECISION: removed *"a thumbnail of an absent item is NotFound"*. `source.rs:97-99` blesses `Ok(None)` as *"a normal answer, not an error"* and mandates a failure only for restricted content. The case was inventing a rule.
- DECISION: removed `!is_complete()` on an abandoned fetch — a source may deliver a small range in one chunk and suspend before resolving. The module's own doc had warned against counting bytes, eight lines above the line that counted bytes.
- FIX: flood-wait backoff 1500ms → whole seconds; Telegram `FLOOD_WAIT_n` is integer seconds, so a tdjson harness could not have staged the old value.
- NOTE: the surviving rule — **assert the category the backend chose, never the classification derived from it**. Sole exception `RateLimited::retry_after`, which is a number the backend must carry across the boundary intact and nothing else would notice.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/conformance/cases/{shape,enumeration,cancellation,failures}.rs`.

### 1550 — Conformance suite: generic harness seam, skips are not passes (TASK-260715-3e8q4m)
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive_testkit::conformance` — 38 cases / 13 clauses, `run<H: SourceHarness>(&H) -> Report`. Fake passes 38/38, 0 skipped. `make check` 8/8.
- DECISION: generic over `H`, **not** a trait object. `block_on` is generic in the future's output because how futures are driven is the backend's business — the fake needs no runtime, tdjson needs tokio. Dyn-compatibility would have forced an executor choice into the suite.
- DECISION: arming (`Perturbation`, pre-live: unreachable, expired reference, rate limit, auth revoked, slow, mid-fetch race) split from mutating (`Mutation`, live: child appears/removed, content changes). Backends implement them in different layers. The mutation **plan is declared up front** so a harness can prepare — `SourceScript` is immutable, so `FakeHarness` compiles the plan into change batches at build time and `Control::advance` is `FakeSource::advance`. Plan order *is* revision order.
- DECISION: `Capability` gating with `Skipped` ≠ `Passed`. A backend that cannot stage a version race has not proved it survives one; crediting it would make the suite most flattering to the backends supporting least. `clauses_upheld()` credits only clauses that ran; the skip list prints under the pass count.
- DECISION: cases return `Failure`, never assert. Forced by the workspace `panic` denial (this is `src/`, not a test) and independently right: a run reports every broken clause instead of stopping at the first, in the contract's vocabulary rather than this crate's. `assert_conforms` is the only panic and its caller is a test.
- FINDING: `Clause::statement()` is **verbatim** `.spec/` text. A paraphrase is where a suite quietly acquires opinions the specification does not hold — and it hid a real mislabel until replaced (see 1552).
- FINDING: the fake's `advance()` mid-enumeration always rejects the continuation, so `does_not_splice_when_the_listing_moves` only ever exercises the reject branch. The keep-serving-the-snapshot branch is equally contract-legal and stays untested until a backend takes it.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/conformance/**`, `tests/conformance.rs`, READMEs.

### 1549 — Saboteur sources are what make the suite worth running (TASK-260715-3e8q4m)
- FINDING: the fake passing 38/38 proves ~nothing on its own — a suite whose every case asserted `true` passes exactly as loudly. `tests/conformance.rs` therefore wraps the fake in `Saboteur`, a source that breaks **one** clause on purpose, and asserts the suite fails *on the case owning that clause*.
- SCOPE: caught — duplicated child (SYNC-003), snapshot shifting per page (SYNC-003), any cursor served regardless of scope (SYNC-004 ×2), delivery one byte past the range (SYNC-041), right offsets + wrong bytes (SYNC-041 **and** SYNC-046), every failure reported as `Unavailable` (SYNC-044 ×2 — auth and expired reference).
- FINDING: "right offsets, wrong bytes" is invisible to `FetchProgress` — it accounts offsets and lengths and never looks at a byte. Only the byte comparison catches it, which is exactly the shape a concurrency bug takes (two fetches served from one buffer). Proves the concurrency case's assertion can actually fail.
- NOTE: an "austere" harness supporting nothing is also tested — it gets skips, stays `is_conformant()` (a skip breaks no clause), and is **not** credited with the clauses it never ran. That test is the guard on the skip-vs-pass distinction.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/tests/conformance.rs`.

### 1459 — Outcome::Failed now carries its delivered count; no delivered() accessor (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- DECISION: `Outcome::Failed(SourceError)` → `Outcome::Failed { error, delivered }` (`record.rs`). Review flagged this as a decision, not rework; taken **now** rather than after the conformance suite (TASK-260715-3e8q4m) builds on the type.
- ROOT CAUSE of the flag: AC says "assert requests, cancellation **and side effects**", but a version race or sink-stop that moved bytes reported them only via the sink, never via `interactions()`. `a_race_records_the_bytes_it_delivered` was asserting the *sink* under a name promising the *record* — the test name was evidence of the type gap, not of a sloppy test.
- DECISION: **no `delivered()` accessor**, deliberately. It would return `0` for `Outcome::Ok` — including a successful fetch that delivered its whole range — i.e. a lossy answer to the exact question it names. Callers match the variant; only the two variants that can be partial carry a count.
- DECISION: `Outcome::Ok` carries no count either — a successful fetch delivered its whole range by contract, and the range is already in `Call::Fetch`.
- NOTE: blast radius nil outside the testkit — no product crate depends on it (dev-dep only, re-verified), so `tests/fake_source.rs` is the only consumer.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/record.rs`, `README.md`.

### 1458 — clear_interactions() misattribution fixed by log epoch (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- FIX: log now carries an epoch (`record.rs`, `struct Log { entries, epoch }`); `clear()` bumps it, each `CallGuard` remembers the epoch it began in, `settle()` returns early on mismatch. A guard from a cleared epoch settles **nothing** — its entry was discarded deliberately, so writing anything would be fabrication. Resolves 1444.
- DECISION: epoch over monotonic ids (review endorsed either). Epoch fixes the defect without changing what `seq` means — it stays positional-from-zero, so a test clearing between phases still reads `seq: 0` for the first call it cares about. Monotonic ids would have leaked "every call ever" into a field documented as positional.
- FINDING: verified two ways, not by green tests. (1) The reviewer's repro binary run **unchanged** against the fixed crate now prints `Ok` where it printed `Cancelled { delivered: 0 }`. (2) Mutation — commenting out the epoch check fails **exactly** the two new tests written for it (`record::tests::a_call_cleared_while_in_flight_cannot_settle_a_later_call`, integration `a_call_still_in_flight_across_a_clear_cannot_rewrite_a_later_one`) and nothing else.
- ANOMALY: the "silent loss" half of the defect (stale `seq` past the end) was already safe **by accident** — `get_mut` on a short log returns `None`. Its two tests pass under the mutation too, so they are intent-documentation, not regression coverage. Recorded rather than claimed as coverage.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/record.rs`, `src/fake.rs`. Repro kept at `.temp/TASK-260715-3uft8j/repro/`.
- STATUS: resolved. `make check` 8/8; testkit 151 tests (76+68+7), was 144.

### 1446 — Review: fake DriveSource sent back for rework (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- VERDICT: review → **changes requested** → `to-dev`. One confirmed correctness defect (see 1444), one AC-coverage gap, two doc contradictions.
- FINDING: implementer's claims verified independently and **all measurable ones held**: `make check` 8/8 re-run clean; 144 tests exact (72+65+7); `result_large_err` sizes measured **168/288/288 exactly** as argued, so the "Err rides free inside the Ok footprint" reasoning is sound; SplitMix64/FNV-1a constants are the canonical published values; boundary holds with zero new deps. The mutation claim reproduced exactly — removing `wake_by_ref` from `exec.rs:Yield::poll` in a throwaway copy failed **only** `every_yield_wakes_itself_so_a_real_runtime_can_drive_the_fake`, other 64 integration + 72 unit tests passed. That test is genuinely load-bearing, not decorative.
- FINDING: one overstatement — `results.md` claims `a_full_scripted_scenario_reaches_every_configured_event` "asserts the exact 9-call interaction log". It builds its summary by **discarding the arguments** (`tests/fake_source.rs:1581-1584`), then asserts `len == 9`, operation for 2 of 9, outcome for 6 of 9 (indices 4/5/6 — `latest_cursor`, `changes`, `changes` — never touched), no arguments compared. Its docstring (`:1484-1486`) also claims cancellation coverage the test does not have, and its `delay(1)` is unobservable because every call goes through `exec::drive`. A docstring that overstates coverage is how a gap survives review.
- FINDING: AC-coverage gap — `Call::Fetch` arguments are asserted **nowhere** (`grep -c 'Call::Fetch'` → 0). The AC is "assert requests"; fetch is the richest call (item + pinned version + range). Also no fault is ever *played* on `Operation::Changes` or `Operation::Thumbnail` — `script.rs:839-841` builds such faults but nothing runs one.
- NOTE: every design decision endorsed on review — delay-as-yields, revision-pinned page tokens, written-out PRNG, up-front `build()` validation, both lint exemptions. The rework is narrow.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/`. Evidence: board resources `TASK-260715-3uft8j_review.md`, `TASK-260715-3uft8j_repro-clear-interactions.rs`.

### 1444 — FakeSource::clear_interactions() silently misattributes outcomes (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- ROOT CAUSE: `Recorder::begin` assigns `seq = log.len()` and `Recorder::settle` writes `log[seq]` — a **positional** index. `clear()` (`record.rs:194`) empties the log without invalidating the `seq` held by live `CallGuard`s, so indices are reused. A guard outstanding across a clear settles whatever entry now occupies its old index.
- FINDING: reproduced through the **public API only** — a fully successful `root()` is recorded `Ok`, then flips to `Cancelled { delivered: 0 }` the moment an unrelated in-flight `fetch` is dropped. The wrong answer is a *plausible* one (`Cancelled` is an expected outcome here), so it misleads rather than crashes.
- FINDING: second silent mode — a stale `seq` past the end makes `settle`'s `log.get_mut(seq)` return `None` and the outcome **vanishes**. A cancellation test that cleared first records nothing at all.
- FINDING: missed because `interactions_can_be_cleared_between_phases` (`tests/fake_source.rs:1336`) only clears when every call has already settled — the safe path. The unsafe path has no test.
- NOTE: matters disproportionately here — this module's whole product is trustworthy evidence, and the downstream consumer is the conformance suite (TASK-260715-3e8q4m). "The harness silently lied about what the source did" is the one failure mode a conformance fixture must not have.
- FIX: pending — generation/epoch bumped by `clear()` and carried by the guard, or monotonic ids instead of positional indices. Needs a test that clears with a call in flight.
- STATUS: pending → `to-dev`.

### 1442 — fault.rs module doc states the opposite of implemented fault counting (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- FINDING: `fault.rs:34-36` says a second matching fault "does not fire for that call, **and its counter does not advance, because it never matched**". The implementation advances the counter for **every** fault whose `matches(...)` is true, firing or not (`fake.rs:225-238`), and `fake.rs:214-220` documents exactly that. The two module docs contradict each other.
- FINDING: verified empirically — two `Fetch` faults at `Nth(1)`/`Nth(2)` fire on calls 1 and 2 respectively, then call 3 succeeds. That only happens if the second fault's counter advanced on call 1.
- DECISION: **the behavior is correct and the doc is the defect.** "Every matching fault's counter advances" is the only rule under which `Nth(n)` means "the n-th call to this operation for this item" regardless of what other faults the script carries — i.e. the only rule that composes. `faults_on_different_occurrences_compose` (`tests/fake_source.rs:1012`) passes only under the implemented rule.
- NOTE: `fault.rs` is the module doc a fixture author reads to reason about multi-fault scripts, so the wrong half is the one that gets read.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/fault.rs`.

### 1436 — Fake source: a delay is a count of yields, not a Duration (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- DECISION: `Fault::delay(n)` returns `Pending` **n times**; it is not a wall-clock wait. A fake that slept on a real clock is non-reproducible by construction — a deterministic fixture whose result depends on host load — and would force an async runtime dependency on every consumer. Nothing the delay exists to test is about elapsed time: tests need what an `await` *provides* (a point to cancel at, to interleave at, to observe an in-flight call at). Wall-clock time reaches the contract only where the contract names it — the `Duration` inside `SourceError::RateLimited`, which is data the caller reads, not time anyone waits.
- FINDING: consequence — `gramdrive-testkit` gained **zero new dependencies**. No runtime, no `rand`, no clock. `exec.rs` is a ~40-line noop-waker poll loop.
- DECISION: every yield wakes itself before parking (`yield_now` pattern), so the fake is drivable by the bundled `exec` **and** by the engine's tokio. Load-bearing and easy to break silently.
- FINDING: `exec`'s noop-waker loop **structurally cannot** catch a missing wake — it re-polls regardless, so a fake that parked forever would still pass every test driven by it; a real runtime catches it by hanging. `tests/fake_source.rs:every_yield_wakes_itself_so_a_real_runtime_can_drive_the_fake` drives with a waker-respecting executor (`std::task::Wake`, polls only when woken). Verified by mutation: removing `wake_by_ref` from `exec.rs:Yield::poll` fails **only** that test; the other 64 pass. Without it the claim was unfalsified.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/{exec,fault}.rs`.

### 1436 — Fake source page tokens name their revision: refuse rather than splice (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- DECISION: a `PageToken` minted by `FakeSource` carries the revision it was minted at (`rev:{n}/at:{offset}`); a continuation presented at another revision is refused with `CursorRejected`. **Stricter than SYNC-003 requires** — a real source *may* keep serving an old snapshot — and deliberate: the alternative is a fake that splices two states into one enumeration and returns a listing with a duplicate or a hole, which is the exact contract failure the conformance suite (TASK-260715-3e8q4m) exists to catch. Refusing is always contract-legal; splicing never is.
- NOTE: practical consequence for test authors — a test wanting an uninterrupted enumeration does not call `advance()` mid-enumeration; a test proving the caller re-baselines correctly does exactly that (`advancing_mid_enumeration_rejects_the_continuation`).
- DECISION: the source changes **only** when the test says so (`advance()` applies one scripted batch; revisions are materialized at `build()` so advancing is a bounds check that cannot fail). A version race is `advance` between a fetch's start and its next chunk. There is no scheduler to lose a race against.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/{fake,script}.rs`.

### 1436 — Fake source PRNG written out rather than depended on (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- DECISION: SplitMix64 + FNV-1a are implemented in `rng.rs` with pinned outputs rather than pulling `rand`/`DefaultHasher`. Neither dependency guarantees cross-version output stability (`DefaultHasher` explicitly reserves the right to change), and a fixture whose chunk boundaries move when a dependency is bumped is a flake with a seed field. `rng::tests` pins concrete values so a change fails the suite instead of silently re-cutting every scripted delivery.
- DECISION: the per-fetch chunk seed folds the request (`script seed ^ fnv1a(item) ^ fnv1a(version) ^ range`), not running generator state — a retried range must chunk identically whatever ran between, and a source shared across concurrent callers has no meaningful "next" draw. Pinned by `the_same_range_chunks_the_same_way_on_every_fetch`.
- DECISION: `ScriptBuilder::build()` validates the whole script up front (one root; tree is a tree; every batch applies at its own revision; every fetchable file has bytes matching its declared size at every revision it exists at). Past `build`, every `SourceError` the fake emits is one the contract specifies or the script asked for — never the fake improvising around a malformed fixture.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/{rng,script}.rs`.

### 1436 — clippy::result_large_err on ScriptError measured, then exempted (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- FINDING: `ScriptError` is 168 bytes — three variants name two `ItemId`s, and an `ItemId` is 80 (56-byte typed key + `Vec<u8>` of canonical bytes). Trips `result_large_err` (128-byte threshold) at 9 sites.
- DECISION: exempted module-level in `script.rs`/`tree.rs` with the measurement recorded, not silenced. The lint targets a fat `Err` taxing a hot success path; **neither half applies**. Measured: `Result<SourceScript, ScriptError>` is **288 bytes = exactly `size_of::<SourceScript>()`**, so the error rides free inside the `Ok` footprint. And these run once per scripted item at fixture-build time. Both remedies cost more than they save — boxing puts an allocation in front of a free value; shrinking means storing identities as strings, trading the typed `ItemId` a caller can assert against for bytes nothing copies.
- FINDING: clippy's `allow-unwrap-in-tests`/`allow-expect-in-tests` (clippy.toml) key on `#[test]` fns and `#[cfg(test)]` modules — they do **not** reach module-level helpers in a `tests/` integration binary, which inherit the workspace deny. Restated the exemption at the top of `tests/fake_source.rs`; the rationale (test code, never links into a product artifact) holds in full. Worth knowing for every future integration test in this workspace.
- SCOPE: `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/{script,tree}.rs`, `tests/fake_source.rs`.

### 1436 — Tree::upsert silently created a second root (TASK-260715-3uft8j)
- ROOT CAUSE: `upsert` compared `existing.parent == item.parent`, and on mismatch detached from the old parent and re-`link`ed. But `link`'s `parent: None` arm just inserts — it sees one item and cannot tell *the* root from *a* parentless item. So an upsert setting `parent: None` on a child produced a second root, and the "reparent" guard below it never fired for that direction.
- FIX: explicit rootness check first — `existing.parent.is_none() != item.parent.is_none()` → `ScriptError::RootReparented`. Rootness is structural and cannot be edited: a child cannot become parentless, the root cannot acquire a parent. `crates/gramdrive-testkit/src/tree.rs:76`.
- NOTE: caught by the test written for it (`upserting_an_item_into_rootlessness_is_rejected`), which failed on first run against the buggy implementation.

### 1408 — Review: source contract types accepted (TASK-260715-1j4ij3)
- VERDICT: review → **accepted** → `done`. `make check` 8/8 and `cargo test -p gramdrive-model -p gramdrive-source` 168/168 re-run independently. Cursor golden fixtures recomputed independently (Python, RFC 4648 base32 over the documented v1 layout) — both literals match byte-for-byte, so the frozen format is pinned by two independent derivations.
- FINDING: base32 extraction verified as a 1:1 code move (diff of `identity/codec.rs` against `base32.rs`); identity goldens/properties untouched-green confirms no encoding change. SYNC-044 coverage checked against the spec sentence: all six task classes present; disk-full/integrity absence is correctly argued (local failures, not backend-reportable) and routed to TASK-260715-3b9w8x.
- FINDING: invalid-state prevention is genuinely structural — `ItemContent` enum (directory-with-bytes unrepresentable), derived read-only `capabilities()` (no writable set constructible, DEC-007), non-empty `ByteRange`/`ContentChunk`/`Thumbnail`, `NonZeroU32` page/thumbnail bounds, validated opaque tokens with explicit caps.
- NOTE [nit, non-blocking]: `gramdrive-source/src/lib.rs:40` claims "no borrowed … types in any exposed struct" while `ContentChunk<'a>` borrows its bytes — the claim holds for ffi-mirrored records (chunks cross via a copying callback), but the sentence overreaches slightly. Fix opportunistically.
- NOTE [nit, non-blocking]: `SourceItem.parent == None` ⟺ account root is documented, not structural. Within AC's "where practical"; the conformance suite (TASK-260715-3e8q4m) is the right place to hunt violations.

### 1010 — Versions and cursors landed in model, not source: layering forced it (TASK-260715-1j4ij3)
- DECISION: `MetadataVersion`/`ContentVersion` and `ChangeCursor` live in `gramdrive-model` (`version.rs`, `cursor.rs`), not in `gramdrive-source`, even though the task belongs to the source-contract story — `gramdrive-state` persists both and may depend only on layer 0 (crates/README.md allow list, which already assigned "versions, cursors" to model). Source re-exports model as `model`.
- DECISION: two version types, not one — a metadata version where a content version belongs is the mistake that publishes stale bytes under a fresh stamp; the type system refuses the substitution. Equality-only, no `Ord`: DOM-003 allows monotonic *or* content-derived schemes, so cross-token ordering is meaningless.
- DECISION: cursor format v1 frozen by goldens (`tests/cursor_golden.rs`, expected strings computed independently) — same evolution policy as identity: new format byte decoded alongside v1, never a mutation. Text prefix `"gdc-"`: the `-` guarantees a cursor never parses as an `ItemId` and vice versa. No checksum on purpose — payload is opaque provider state; scope/schema rejection is the guarantee, corruption detection is not.
- FIX: identity codec's strict base32 extracted to crate-private `base32.rs` (parameterized prefix, neutral errors) so cursor text shares one codec; identity goldens/properties untouched and green — that is the proof the refactor changed no encoding.
- SCOPE: model: `base32.rs`, `version.rs`, `cursor.rs` (new), `identity/codec.rs` (delegation), README; source: `error.rs`, `item.rs`, `page.rs`, `fetch.rs`, `source.rs` (new), lib.rs, README. `make check` 8/8, 168 tests across both crates.

### 1010 — SourceError taxonomy: disk-full and integrity deliberately absent (TASK-260715-1j4ij3)
- DECISION: `SourceError` (11 variants) covers the task's six specified classes — auth, flood-wait (`RateLimited{retry_after}`), restricted (POL-4), stale reference (DOM-007), transient network (`Unavailable`), cancellation — plus SYNC-044's source deletion (`NotFound`), version race (`VersionConflict`), rejected anchor (`CursorRejected`, SYNC-004), `InvalidRequest`, `Internal`. Disk-full and integrity are **local** (state/engine) failure classes no backend can report; forcing them into the source enum would make adapters invent them. Cross-layer mapping is TASK-260715-3b9w8x.
- DECISION: retry classification is a derived method (`retry_advice()`, one exhaustive match → `Never`/`AfterBackoff`/`AfterReauth`/`AfterRefresh`/`AfterRebaseline`), not a stored field — category and retry class cannot drift apart, and the engine keeps policy (bounds, jitter) per NFR-033.
- DECISION: `DriveSource` uses boxed `SourceFuture` returns instead of `async fn` — the engine selects local vs remote at runtime (`Box<dyn DriveSource>`), and `async fn` in traits is not dyn-compatible. Zero new dependencies; dyn-compatibility pinned by test.
- NOTE: `FetchProgress::record` is verified delivery accounting (rejects gap/overlap/overrun at the first bad chunk) — intended as the shared assertion tool for the conformance suite (TASK-260715-3e8q4m) and the engine (SYNC-046).

### 0930 — Review: ordering projection accepted; two non-blocking follow-ups (TASK-260715-1jmsdp)
- VERDICT: review → **accepted** → `done`. `make check` 8/8 and `cargo test -p gramdrive-model` 118/118 re-run independently. AC pinned by tests (reorder changes metadata only; rename changes name only; property generalizes). Duplicate-detection fix verified independently — old `windows(2)` assumed sorted⟹adjacent but the sort is `(order, chat_id)` desc, so `[(c5,20),(c9,15),(c5,10)]` hides the second copy; set-based scan is correct, both regression tests genuine.
- FOLLOW-UP [owner]: `.spec/sync-and-filesystem-semantics.md:32` SYNC-011 still reads "Numeric order prefixes are an optional presentation mode" — predates DEC-013. Code, `policies.md` POL-1 and `docs/TRACEABILITY.md:100` all read it the DEC-013 way; the spec sentence is the lone holdout. One-sentence owner edit; implementer correctly flagged rather than unilaterally rewriting an owner-governed spec. Did not gate acceptance.
- FOLLOW-UP [hardening]: no cross-module test pins ordering↔tree consistency. `OrderEntry.id` and the tree's ChatList children build the same `AppearanceKey` independently, and order.json's `name` must match what a tree consumer derives via `resolve_siblings` with `fixed: true`. Holds by construction today and there is no production tree-naming consumer yet — but a future consumer forgetting `fixed: true` would make order.json name paths that do not exist, silently. Add the pin when the provider layer lands.

### 0843 — order.json needed two structural extensions the ordering task exposed (TASK-260715-1jmsdp)
- FINDING: POL-1 puts `order.json` at each **list** root, but `GeneratedDocKey` is chat-scoped by field type (`chat: ChatKey`) — a list-scoped generated document is **not representable**. Added `OrderDocKey { list: ChatListKey, schema_family }` + canonical tag `0x0b`, additive in the extension room the v1 range reserved (same move the tree builder made for `0x08`–`0x0a`). Pre-existing goldens unchanged, which is what pins it as additive.
- FINDING: `order.json` at a list root shares a directory with user-titled chat folders, so a chat titled `order.json` collides with it. `resolve_siblings` is deliberately **symmetric** (every collision member suffixed), which would have suffixed the metadata to `order.json (k3m9xq2)` — or handed a provider two children with one name. Added `SiblingName::fixed`: constants never take a suffix, titles yield.
- DECISION: the asymmetry is principled, not a special case. The two reasons symmetry exists (don't privilege an arbitrary member; don't rename the survivor when the other is deleted) **neither apply to a constant** — `order.json` is GramDrive's name, and it cannot be deleted. Documented on `resolve_siblings`.
- SCOPE: `src/ordering.rs` (new), `src/identity.rs`, `src/identity/codec.rs`, `src/naming.rs`, `src/tree.rs`, README. `make check` 8/8, 118 tests.

### 0843 — Duplicate chats slipped past an adjacency check because the sort key starts with `order` (TASK-260715-1jmsdp)
- ROOT CAUSE: first implementation rejected duplicates via `records.windows(2).find(|w| w[0].chat_id == w[1].chat_id)` after sorting, reasoning "sorted ⟹ duplicates adjacent". False: the sort is `(order, chat_id)` **descending**, so `order` is the primary key. Two records for chat 5 (orders 20 and 10) are separated by any chat whose order falls between — e.g. `[(20,c5), (15,c9), (10,c5)]`.
- FINDING: the 2-record fixture passed anyway — duplicates are trivially adjacent at n=2. The test agreed with the bug instead of catching it.
- FINDING: consequence was not just a doubled `order.json` entry: the duplicate reaches `resolve_siblings`, violating its stated distinct-ids precondition (`naming.rs`), which resolves to **two identically-named folders**.
- FIX: set-based scan after the sort (`ordering.rs:168`) — deterministic reported chat regardless of input order.
- NOTE: both regression tests confirmed to fail against the reverted fix — `duplicate_chat_records_are_rejected_even_when_not_adjacent` (fixture) and `duplicate_chats_are_rejected_at_any_distance` (property, shrank to the 3-record interleave). Seed checked in per the `naming_properties` convention.
- STATUS: resolved.

### 0843 — order.json emits `order` as a string: int64 does not survive a JSON number (TASK-260715-1jmsdp)
- DECISION: `chats[].order` is a **JSON string**, `chats[].chat_id` a **number**. `chatPosition.order` is int64 and a JSON number is an IEEE-754 double to most parsers (JavaScript, `jq`) — the top-of-range values Telegram gives **pinned** chats round silently, and two distinct pinned chats can compare equal after the round trip. `chat_id` is int53 by Telegram's own schema, where no such loss exists.
- NOTE: `rank` (0-based, resolved) is the field readers should use; `order` is the raw server rank, kept for reconstruction. TDLib's own JSON interface serializes int64 as string for the same reason.
- DECISION: `is_pinned` recorded but **not** a sort key — Telegram already encodes pinning in `order`, and sorting by it again would be a second, disagreeing implementation of the server's ranking. Kept because the pinned boundary is not recoverable from the sequence alone.

### 0843 — Composition docs corrected: first fold is the free half, not last (TASK-260715-1ffbkg follow-up)
- FIX: `naming.rs` `fold_key` rustdoc and `fold_key_merges_whatever_any_platform_merges` doc — `last` → `first`, per the 0821 review finding, folded into this task as the next one touching `naming.rs`.
- NOTE: `fold_key = Windows(Apple(x))` for `ALL = [Apple, Windows, ..]`, so `Apple(a) == Apple(b)` forces equal keys for *any* outer folds — the first applied fold is free; the Windows guarantee is the Unicode fact. Comment-only; no test or behavior change.
- STATUS: resolved (closes the 0821 `NOTE`).


### 0821 — Composed fold key verified hole-free; composition docs name the wrong half as "free" (TASK-260715-1ffbkg, review round 2)
- STATUS: review → **accepted** → `done`. `make check` 8/8, 88/88 tests, re-run independently.
- FINDING: the safety claim *"if any platform merges two names, `fold_key` merges them too"* verified **exhaustively**, not sampled — all 1,112,064 codepoints grouped by each `Platform::fold`, every group maps to one key: **0 holes on every platform, in both composition orders**. Probe: `.temp/TASK-260715-1ffbkg/foldprobe/`.
- FINDING: the rework's rejection of the review's recommended `to_uppercase().to_lowercase()` is correct and reproducible — mutating `fold_key` to it fails the shipped suite on `case 'capital sharp s vs sharp s': ["ẞ", "ß"] still collide on Apple`. The round-1 verdict would have shipped a second `ΟΔΟΣ`/`οδοσ`. A reviewer's named fix is a hypothesis, not an instruction; measuring it beat adopting it.
- FINDING: `naming.rs:707` and `naming.rs:1006` state the composition gives the guarantee "for the last fold applied" and leaves earlier ones to Unicode's tables. **Inverted.** With `ALL = [Apple, Windows, ...]`, `fold_key = Windows(Apple(x))`: `Apple(a)==Apple(b)` ⟹ keys equal for *any* functions, so the **first** applied fold is the free half; the Windows guarantee is the Unicode fact. The sentence then correctly names `Platform::Windows.fold` — the last effective fold — as the half that is *not* free, contradicting its own first clause.
- NOTE: non-blocking, comment-only (`last`→`first`). Nothing is mis-tested: the suite checks both halves and the actionable claim (Windows is the non-free check) is stated correctly. Risk is a maintainer trusting the framing and dropping the only non-free check. Fold into the next task touching `naming.rs`.
- FINDING: measured the targeted property's teeth under the `to_lowercase` mutant with the regressions file removed — caught **9/10** pure-random runs at default 256 cases. "Fails within a few hundred cases" is accurate; random search alone is marginally flaky. Irrelevant in practice: the checked-in `naming_properties.proptest-regressions` seed (`"II"`/`"Iı"`) replays first and makes it deterministic, and unit + corpus + goldens catch the same mutant deterministically.
- NOTE: mutation testing ran in a throwaway `git worktree` under `.temp/`; `crates/` never touched by the reviewer.
- SCOPE: review only — no code changed.

### 0809 — Case folding: no stock Rust mapping models the platforms; round-trip is not enough either (TASK-260715-1ffbkg, rework)
- FINDING: measured over the whole `char` space (`.temp/TASK-260715-1ffbkg/coarseprobe/`, `caseless` as ground truth) — the review's recommended `to_uppercase().to_lowercase()` round trip has **exactly one hole**: `ẞ` (U+1E9E) vs `ß`/`ss`. APFS case-folds all three to `ss`; the round trip sends `ß`→`ss` but `ẞ`→`ß`. So the recommended fix would have shipped the *same class of defect* it was written to close, one codepoint over.
- FINDING: no single mapping works, in either direction. `to_lowercase` misses every Windows-only pair (sigma, dotless `ı`); `to_uppercase` misses every Apple-only one (Kelvin `K` U+212A, Angstrom); true Unicode case folding **also** misses `ı`/`i`, which NTFS `$UpCase` merges — so `caseless` alone is not a fix either. Neither case-insensitive platform's fold contains the other.
- DECISION: model the fold per platform (`Platform::fold`: Windows = `to_uppercase`/`$UpCase`, Apple = full case folding, Android/Linux = identity/bytes) and **compose** all four for the collision key, derived from `Platform::ALL` like `ComponentBudget::strictest`. Verified 0 holes vs both platform folds across the char space, in either composition order.
- FINDING: NFC settles the compatibility singletons (Kelvin U+212A → `K`, Angstrom U+212B → `Å`) *before* folding is reached, so those never were fold cases. The Apple-only pair that survives NFC and justifies the `caseless` dep is `ẞ`/`ß`.
- FINDING: re-pointing the *broad* property suite at `Platform::fold` fixes the tautology but does **not** catch the bug — `arb_raw` samples too wide a space to land two siblings on one folded name; measured, the broad property stays green against the `to_lowercase` key. A targeted generator over a case-variant-only alphabet (`case_variant_siblings_never_collide`) is what actually fails. Re-pointing alone would have been false comfort.
- FIX: `naming.rs` `Platform::fold` + composed `fold_key`; fold corpus of *pairs* with a hand-written `merged_by` column (`naming_fixture.rs`); targeted property + `CASE_VARIANTS` in `NASTY` (`naming_properties.rs`). New dep `caseless` 0.2 (MIT, inside POL-6 allow list, no DEC-021 exception; `cargo deny` green).
- NOTE: general constraint — a corpus with one expected output per input structurally cannot catch a wrong case fold. Every row passes while two rows silently name one folder. Folding is a property of *pairs* and needs a pair-shaped table.
- SCOPE: crates/gramdrive-model/src/naming.rs, tests/naming_fixture.rs, tests/naming_properties.rs, tests/naming_collisions.rs, Cargo.toml, crates/gramdrive-model/README.md
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8; mutation-verified — both the old `to_lowercase` key and the review's recommended round trip fail the new suite.

### 0744 — Naming review: case-fold key misses collisions the target platforms make (TASK-260715-1ffbkg, review)
- STATUS: review → changes requested → `to-dev`. `make check` 8/8 and 83/83 tests re-run green independently; the defect is invisible to both.
- FINDING: `naming.rs:630` `fold_key` folds with `str::to_lowercase`. Its doc comment claims this is "at least as aggressive as the platforms' own tables" — **false**. `to_lowercase` applies the Unicode **contextual final-sigma rule** (emits `ς` at word end); NTFS `$UpCase` folds via uppercase (`ς`→`Σ`, `σ`→`Σ`) and APFS folds via true Unicode case folding (`ς`→`σ`). Both collapse what `to_lowercase` keeps apart.
- ROOT CAUSE: structural, not a typo. `Platform` models per-character rules and budgets and exposes `case_insensitive() -> bool`, but never models **how** a platform folds. A bool without a fold behind it means the "one output satisfies all four platforms" corpus cannot catch a folding mismatch by construction.
- FINDING: reproduced through the public API — siblings titled `ΟΔΟΣ` and `οδοσ` (ordinary Greek word, caps vs lowercase) get **no suffix** and resolve to **one directory** on Windows/APFS; one chat silently shadows the other. Same for `οδος`/`οδοσ` and dotless `ı`/`i`. Exactly the failure SYNC-012/SYNC-013 and the DoD's "case-insensitive collision handling" exist to prevent. Probe: `.temp/TASK-260715-1ffbkg/sigmaprobe/`.
- FINDING: `naming_properties.rs:169` (`resolved_siblings_never_collide`) asserts collision-freedom by folding with `to_lowercase()` — **the same function the implementation uses**. `resolve_siblings` iterates until `fold_key` reports no collisions, so the property is tautological for this bug class and cannot fail regardless of whether the fold matches any real filesystem. A test that restates the implementation is not a check on it.
- NOTE: neither single-direction mapping is sufficient — `to_lowercase` misses sigma/dotless-i/`ß`; `to_uppercase` misses Kelvin `K` (U+212A) and Angstrom `Å` (U+212B). The round-trip `to_uppercase().to_lowercase()` catches all six with no new dep, over-colliding only on `ß`/`ss` — the safe direction, per the implementer's own principle ("a spurious collision costs a suffix; a missed one would ship a broken tree"). A true case fold (`caseless`) is the more principled option. Keep the `.nfc()` re-normalization.
- NOTE: `resolve_siblings` loop bound `min(len + 2, 64)` **is** adequate, despite worst-case useful bumps being `3 × len`. Escalation chains run in *parallel* rounds, and each extra round requires a distinct sibling crafted to impersonate another's suffixed name, so rounds are bounded by the longest chain (≤ `len`). The code's "backstop" framing is accurate — checked by hand-tracing a 4-link chain.
- NOTE: lazy paging vs set-relative naming — `resolve_siblings` needs the *whole* sibling set while the tree enumerates lazily per page, so a consumer naming page 1 must materialize every sibling (a collision may involve page 50). Inherent to SYNC-012 and correctly flagged by the implementer as provider-layer, but it partly defeats TASK-260715-3tjduq's paging and deserves an explicit architecture note before the adapter task.
- NOTE: everything except the fold is right. Strip-before-NFC ordering, grapheme-boundary truncation, the held-back escape byte, identity-digest-over-prefix, and suffixing every member of a collision set all verified correct; 53-case corpus, 37 new tests, POL-6 license claim, and the behaviour-preserving `tree.rs` refactor all confirmed as reported.

### 0734 — Cross-platform naming policy landed (TASK-260715-1ffbkg)
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive_model::naming` — `sanitize` (total: no input has a failure mode) + `resolve_siblings` (collision-free sibling set). `crates/gramdrive-model/src/naming.rs`. SYNC-012, SYNC-013, PLAT-021, POL-1. `make check` 8/8; 83/83 tests in gramdrive-model, 37 new: 7 unit, 6 fixture (over a 53-case corpus table), 15 collision, 9 property.
- DECISION: **one name for the strictest target, not one per platform.** A per-platform name would make one chat a different path per device and break `chat.json` links (SYNC-032). `Platform::check` models each of the four faithfully so the corpus asserts a single output satisfies all; the policy is their union plus GramDrive-only rules (NFC, no invisibles) that no filesystem enforces.
- DECISION: pipeline order is load-bearing — strip invisibles **before** NFC. Deleting a control from between a base char and its combining mark leaves a sequence that composes; normalizing first emits a non-NFC name. Pinned by corpus case "control between base and combining mark" (`Cafe\u{7}\u{301}` → `Café`).
- DECISION: ZWJ (U+200D) and ZWNJ (U+200C) are **kept** though they sit inside the stripped zero-width range. Stripping ZWJ tears 👨‍👩‍👧‍👦 into separate people; stripping ZWNJ corrupts Persian/Indic. Bidi overrides/isolates/LRM/RLM are stripped — spoofing vector (`photo\u{202e}gnp.exe` renders as `photoexe.png`), not a display preference.
- DECISION: truncation cuts at **grapheme-cluster** boundaries, not codepoints — a codepoint cut halves a flag into a stray regional indicator and leaves dangling ZWJs. Codepoint fallback only for a single cluster larger than the whole budget (zalgo); `compose` re-normalizes after that path.
- DECISION: collision suffix = base32 of FNV-1a + splitmix64 finalizer over `ItemId` bytes — **not** an id prefix. Sibling ids share long prefixes (version, kind tag, account scope) and differ deep in the payload, so a prefix would be identical across the very items being distinguished. Non-cryptographic on purpose: a forced digest collision is just another collision, absorbed deterministically by escalation (7 chars → 13 → full id text, which cannot collide).
- DECISION: **every** member of a collision set is suffixed, not just the losers. Leaving one bare privileges an arbitrary member and renames the survivor when the other is deleted. Collision detection runs on **final** names, so a title crafted to impersonate another chat's suffixed name joins the collision set instead of colliding with it (`a_crafted_title_impersonating_a_suffix_still_resolves`).
- DECISION: `compose` holds back 1 byte/unit unconditionally for the reserved-name escape underscore. Costs one byte of 255; buys the guarantee that escaping cannot push a fitted name over budget — no re-truncation loop, no case analysis about whether truncation just created a `CON`.
- DECISION: whole-path budgets (`MAX_PATH` 260) stay with the Windows adapter (PLAT-WIN-004), documented as a capability (PLAT-022). The core does not know where a sync root is mounted, and the layout nests 6 deep; meeting 260 by component truncation would mangle names on the three platforms with no such limit.
- FINDING: `unicode_normalization::is_nfc_quick` returns `Maybe` (not `No`) for a string starting with a combining mark. Treating `!= Yes` as unnormalized rejected `"\u{301}"` — a lone accent, unusual but perfectly NFC. Found by the property suite on its first run, minimal-shrunk to the exact input. FIX: use the full `is_nfc`. Pinned as corpus case "lone combining mark" + `tests/naming_properties.proptest-regressions` (checked in per proptest convention; repo's first such file).
- DECISION: first external runtime deps of layer 0 — `unicode-normalization`, `unicode-segmentation` (+ transitive `tinyvec`). UCD tables, not logic: cannot be hand-written correctly. All MIT OR Apache-2.0, inside the POL-6 allow list, so **no DEC-021-style named exception needed**; `cargo deny check licenses` green. Neither is platform-specific, so layer 0 stays platform-neutral (architecture check green).
- SCOPE: POL-1 formatting moved `tree.rs::chat_display_name` → `naming::chat_folder_name` (single source of truth; tree delegates, output unchanged, tree tests untouched). Tree still emits **raw** names by design — sanitization is applied by the consumer over a sibling set, since collision resolution is set-relative and the tree enumerates lazily per page.
- NOTE: `resolve_siblings` precondition — ids must be distinct. Two siblings sharing one identity are the same item twice (the tree builder cannot produce it) and would get the same name; no identity-derived suffix can separate identical identities.

### 0714 — Tree builder review accepted; logbook 0655 header restored (TASK-260715-3tjduq, review)
- STATUS: review accepted → done. Independently re-ran `make check` (8/8) and `cargo test -p gramdrive-model` (46/46: 10 unit, 8 golden, 13 identity property, 12 tree fixture, 3 tree property). All ACs verified: spec layout pinned literally against `.spec/sync-and-filesystem-semantics.md`; one canonical record/blob behind multiple appearances (structural — views hold only chat-ID references into one `ChatState` map); seeded-shuffle determinism; `ItemId`-anchored paging with `ForeignPageBoundary` on foreign cursors; read-only capabilities unrepresentable as writable (DEC-007); POL-1 `<Display Name> — @<username>` names. Identity extensions verified additive: original goldens byte-identical, new tags confined to reserved `0x08`–`0x0a` + format `0x03`.
- REGRESSION: implementer's logbook edit replaced the `### 0655` header line instead of inserting above it, merging the identity-review record into the 0710 entry — its "review accepted → done" line then misread as this task's verdict. Restored the 0655 header during review; logbook is append-only.
- NOTE: `children()` is O(siblings) per call (materializes the full sibling key list, linear boundary scan). Acceptable at the model layer for v1 sibling counts; the exact-match `ItemId` boundary contract permits a seek-based implementation later with no API change.

### 0710 — Virtual tree builder landed; identity format extended additively (TASK-260715-3tjduq)
- MILESTONE: `gramdrive_model::tree::TreeProjection` projects normalized records into the spec layout (Main/Archive/Telegram Folders, chat.json, messages.ndjson, YYYY/MM.md, YYYY/media/). Lazy per-parent paged enumeration anchored by the last child's `ItemId` (SYNC-003); read-only `Capabilities` unrepresentable as writable (DEC-007/SYNC-060). `crates/gramdrive-model/src/tree.rs`.
- DECISION: identity format v1 extended **additively** in the room the codec reserved ("tree builder will add directory kinds"): folder catalog `0x08`, year dir `0x09`, media dir `0x0a`, `DocFormat::Json` `0x03` for chat.json. No pre-existing encoding changed; original goldens pass untouched; new goldens pin the new kinds. Not a format-version bump — additions only.
- DECISION: `chat.json` modeled as `GeneratedDoc{partition: Chat, format: Json}` per DOM-023 ("chat metadata view"), not a new key kind. `media/` is per-year (sibling of MM.md inside `YYYY/`), exists iff the year has attachments; month docs and media are independent.
- DECISION: (view, item) discipline — appearances exist only for chats and their subtrees; account/chat-list/catalog/message/blob keys never wrap in a view. Records carry bare IDs; the projection derives keys from its own scope, so cross-account records are unrepresentable.
- DECISION: page boundaries are exact-match and snapshot-scoped: a boundary from another snapshot fails `ForeignPageBoundary` (re-enumerate) rather than resuming approximately — SYNC-003 requires repeatability per declared snapshot, which the immutable projection is.
- FIX: codec.rs max-key-size comment corrected per 0655 finding (blob appearance 49 B, capacity 64 aligned to tested bound).
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8; 46/46 tests in gramdrive-model (12 tree fixture, 3 tree property incl. seeded-shuffle determinism). Handed to review, not committed.

### 0655 — Identity v1 review accepted; max-key-size comment is wrong (TASK-260715-1qz1g5, review)
- STATUS: review accepted → done. Independently re-ran `make check` (8/8) and `cargo test -p gramdrive-model` (31/31 green: 10 unit, 8 golden, 13 property). Type model matches DEC-008/DOM-001..024; golden pins, version gating, parser strictness, and namespace-separation properties all verified against the spec rows in `.spec/domain-model.md`.
- FINDING: doc accuracy — `codec.rs:64` claims "largest v1 key (attachment appearance) is 40 bytes"; actual max is the **blob appearance at 49 bytes** (1+1+5 folder view+1 tag+8 account+1 hash tag+32 digest); even a canonical blob is 43. Same wrong figure in the results artifact. The enforceable contract is unaffected: `encoded_size_is_bounded` pins ≤64 bytes / ≤128 text chars, and no dependent carrier is fixed-width below that (Windows FILE_IDENTITY is 4 KiB). `Vec::with_capacity(48)` merely reallocs once for blob appearances. Fix the comment opportunistically; not worth a rework cycle.
- NOTE: dependents should size buffers from the tested ≤64-byte bound, never from prose.

### 0638 — Identity format v1 frozen: typed keys + opaque serialization in gramdrive-model (TASK-260715-1qz1g5)
- MILESTONE: DEC-008/DOM-001..024 identity vocabulary landed — canonical keys (account, chat list, chat, message, attachment, generated doc, blob), appearance keys (view × canonical, non-nesting), opaque `ItemId` (binary + `gd`-prefixed lowercase base32 text). `crates/gramdrive-model/src/identity.rs`, `src/identity/codec.rs`.
- DECISION: `AccountKey` excludes `NamespaceVersion`; Telegram-derived keys carry `AccountScope` (account + epoch), blob keys the bare account — content identity is orthogonal to the Telegram namespace epoch, so an epoch bump retires chats/messages/attachments/docs but not content-addressed blobs.
- DECISION: encoding is a hand-rolled fixed-width prefix code, no serde — injectivity provable from layout, zero product-dep supply-chain surface. Text form is strict canonical base32 (one spelling per key; case-fold cannot alias).
- DECISION: v1 pinned by golden fixtures (`tests/identity_golden.rs`); any encoding change must be a new version byte, never a v1 edit. Non-`0x01` version bytes fail as `UnsupportedVersion` by design.
- FINDING: clippy's `allow-expect-in-tests` exempts only code inside `#[test]` fns — a helper fn in an integration test file still trips `expect_used`. Helpers must return `Result`.
- NOTE: proptest (dev-dep, `default-features = false, features = ["std"]`) added three build-script names to `deny.toml` allowlist: `num-traits` (autocfg probe), `zerocopy` (via ppv-lite86/rand_chacha, cfg emitter), `wit-bindgen` (getrandom's WASI-only dep, never compiled for our targets). All dev-tree only; licenses gate unaffected.
- STATUS: `make check` 8/8 green; 31 tests in gramdrive-model (13 property, 8 golden/error, 6 codec unit, 4 pre-existing).

### 0616 — `cargo clean -p` and artifact uplift silently fake a "rebuild" measurement (TASK-260715-3akqs8, review)
- FINDING: measuring build reproducibility by hand has two traps that both return a *stale* digest from a command that exits 0 and looks like it built. Hit both while verifying 0552.
- FINDING: `cargo clean -p <crate>` **without `--target <triple>`** does not clean the cross-target directory. A `clean -p` + `cargo rustc --target …` cycle finished in 0.36s and reported the pre-existing artifact's digest.
- FINDING: deleting the `.a` from `target/<triple>/release/` does not force a rebuild either. Cargo keeps the real artifact in `deps/` and hardlinks ("uplifts") it into `release/`, so the next build re-uplifts in 0.03s with no `Compiling` line.
- NOTE: to genuinely recompile one crate inside an otherwise-populated target dir: `cargo clean -p <crate> --target <triple> --release`, then assert `Compiling <crate>` actually appears in cargo's stderr. Timing alone is the cheapest tell — an LTO staticlib build here is ~25s; anything under a second did not happen.
- NOTE: this is why 0552's `cargo clean -p` framing understates the old check's weakness — a same-path `clean -p` + rebuild may not have rebuilt at all.
- SCOPE: `.task-board/.resources/TASK-260715-3akqs8/TASK-260715-3akqs8_reviewer-measurements.py` (evidence).

### 0615 — 0552 independently confirmed; a reused target dir is unstable, not just different (TASK-260715-3akqs8, review)
- FINDING: **confirms 0552, refutes 0542.** Independent re-measurement driving the pipeline's own `build_env()`/`cargo_staticlib_argv()`: at the *exact main-checkout path* 0542 attributed `bab48d50…` to, a clean build produces `110b1b9a…` — identical to both staged paths and to the library inside the shipped XCFramework. The checkout path and the target-dir path are both measured non-variables.
- FINDING: **new, strengthens 0553.** A polluted target dir does not yield a second *stable* value. Forced genuine rebuild (`Compiling` confirmed) in the repo's `target/` produced `a098d5f5…` — a **third** digest, neither `110b1b9a…` nor 0542/0552's `bab48d50…`; the pollution state had drifted in between. Reuse makes the bytes *unpredictable*; a clean target dir is stable at `110b1b9a…` everywhere.
- NOTE: the two polluted digests share size 7,920,568 B, distinct from the clean build's 7,920,584 B — a coherent signature of the LTO delta.
- NOTE: consequence — the dedicated wiped target dir (0553) is better justified than its own write-up claims. `.scripts/packaging/README.md` and `build_slices`'s docstring frame reuse as producing `bab48d50…`; "a reused target directory makes the bytes unpredictable" is truer and a stronger argument. No action taken (docs' conclusion unaffected).
- FINDING: doc accuracy — `.scripts/packaging/README.md:144` cites zip `a3e976af…`; actual is `619a82df…` (stable across two full runs). The *property* (two runs identical) verified true. The value is inherently ephemeral: the zip contains the manifest, which embeds `git.describe` and `worktree_clean`, so any absolute zip sha there goes stale on the next commit. Recommend dropping the number rather than updating it.
- STATUS: resolved — task accepted → `done`. `make check` 8/8, 113 script tests, `make package` + Swift consumer + `make smoke-bindings` all green.

### 0552 — The reproducibility variable is target-dir reuse, not the checkout path (TASK-260715-3akqs8, rework)
- FINDING: **corrects 0542 below.** The shipped (LTO) library *is* path-independent. Measured, clean builds of identical source: `110b1b9a…` at three different paths (`.temp/…/repro/a`, `.temp/…/repro/b-considerably-longer-path-name`, `/private/tmp/gramdrive-core-build`), across two different target-dir paths and two different `CARGO_HOME`s. Path, target path and `CARGO_HOME` are all measured non-variables.
- ROOT CAUSE: the real variable is **prior state in the target directory**. Same path (main checkout), only target state varying: existing `target/` + `cargo clean -p` → `bab48d50…` (7,920,568 B); fresh target dir → `110b1b9a…` (7,920,584 B). The main checkout's `target/` carries 547 dep artifacts, an `incremental/` dir, and a stale rlib/dylib from earlier plain `cargo build` runs.
